admits a quasiprojective good moduli space
M_{B}(X)
in the sense of
[
2
]
(or GIT
[
90
]
). This in particular means there is an affine scheme
M_{B}(X)
and a morphism
c_{X}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)\to M_{B}(X)
which is surjective on
\mathbb{C}
-points and universally closed (see
[
118
,
Tag 0513
]
) in the Zariski topology (see
Section

5.1
for more discussion on the Zariski topology for stacks). We may construct it as follows. After choosing a basepoint
x
, observe that
R_{B}(X,x,r)=\Spec H^{0}(R_{B}(X,x,r),\mathcal{O}_{R_{B}(X,x,r)})
is affine. Then
M_{B}(X,r)=\Spec(H^{0}(R_{B}(X,x,r),\mathcal{O}_{R_{B}(X,x,r)})^{\mathbf{GL}_{r}})
is the spectrum of the invariant ring and the
\mathbb{C}
-points of
M_{B}(X)
are naturally identified with isomorphism classes of semisimple complex local systems
[
115
, Proposition 6.1]
. Denote by
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X,r)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X,r)(\mathbb{C})
the set of rank
r
semisimple local systems, which is easily seen to be
\mathbb{Q}
-constructible. We call the restriction
p_{X}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}\to M_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
and the resulting constructible retraction
\mathrm{ss}_{X}=p_{X}^{-1}\circ c_{X}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
the semi-simplification.

Lemma 4.6
.

For any (Zariski/euclidean) closed
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
,
\mathrm{ss}_{X}(\Sigma)\subset\Sigma
.

Proof.

The proof is standard: for a representation
V
, taking a filtration with semisimple subquotients
V_{i}
and choosing a splitting
V=\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}V_{i}
as complex vector spaces, conjugation by the operator
\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}t^{i}
limits to the semisimplification as
t\to 0
.
∎

For any morphism
f:X\to Y
of algebraic spaces there is a representable
\mathbb{Q}
-morphism of algebraic stacks
f^{*}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
given by pull-back, which is in fact the disjoint union of the quotients of the natural pull-backs
f^{*}:R_{B}(Y,y,r)\to R_{B}(X,x,r)
of framed local systems.

Remark 4.7
.

More generally, observe that for any finitely generated group
\Gamma
, there is a representation stack
\mathcal{M}_{B}(\Gamma)
with affine good moduli space
M_{B}(\Gamma)
. Moreover, these structures are functorial with respect to
\Gamma
.

It will be useful throughout to restrict to curves, so we introduce the following:

Definition 4.8
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space. A
Lefschetz curve
is a locally closed immersion
i:C\to X
of a connected smooth affine curve such that
i_{*}:\pi_{1}(C,c)\to\pi_{1}(X,x)
is surjective (for compatibly chosen basepoints) and for some projective resolution
\pi:X^{\prime}\to X
for
X^{\prime}=\bar{X}^{\prime}\setminus D
with
(\bar{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime})
a log smooth projective variety, there is a factorization
i^{\prime}:C\to X^{\prime}
of
i
through
\pi
such that the closure of
i^{\prime}(C)
in
\bar{X}^{\prime}
meets every irreducible component of the regular locus of
D^{\prime}
.

Lemma 4.9
.

For any connected normal algebraic space
X
a Lefschetz curve
i:C\to X
exists.

Proof.

Since
X
is normal,
\pi_{1}(X^{\prime},x^{\prime})\to\pi_{1}(X,x)
is surjective for
\pi:X^{\prime}\to X
as in the definition, so it suffices to take
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
for
(\bar{X},D)
a log smooth projective variety, which is given by
[
53
, §II.5.1]
.
∎

Remark 4.10
.

Recall that a local system
V
on a connected normal algebraic space
X
has quasiunipotent local monodromy if for some (hence any) projective resolution
\pi:X^{\prime}\to X
by
X^{\prime}=\bar{X}^{\prime}\setminus D
for
(\bar{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime})
a log smooth projective variety, the local monodromy of
\pi^{*}V
is quasiunipotent. It follows that for any Lefschetz curve
i:C\to X
,
i^{*}V
has quasiunipotent local monodromy if and only if
V
does.

4.2.
Mixed twistor/Hodge structures and their variations: definitions

In this section we recall the definitions of complex mixed Hodge structures (
\mathbb{C}
-MHSs), mixed twistor structures (
\mathbb{C}
-MTSs), admissible complex variations of mixed Hodge structures (
\mathbb{C}
-VMHSs), and admissible complex variations of mixed twistor structures (
\mathbb{C}
-VMTSs). The main references are Deligne
[
35
]
and for example
[
46
]
(and the references therein) in the Hodge case, and
[
110
,
86
]
in the twistor case.

4.2.1.
Over a point: Hodge case

Definition 4.11
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. A complex pure Hodge structure (
\mathbb{C}
-HS) of weight
k
is a triple
(V,F^{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet})
where
V
is a finite-dimensional
\mathbb{C}
-vector space and
F^{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet}
are
k
-opposed decreasing filtrations. Recall that this means that
\operatorname{gr}_{F}^{p}\operatorname{gr}_{F^{\prime}}^{q}V=0
if
p+q\neq k
, and implies that there is a splitting
V=\bigoplus_{p+q=k}V^{p,q}
such that
F^{p}=\bigoplus_{i\geq p}V^{i,k-i}
and
F^{\prime q}=\bigoplus_{i\geq q}V^{k-i,i}
given by
V^{p,q}=F^{p}\cap F^{\prime q}
. We often refer to the
\mathbb{C}
-HS by just
V
.

Morphisms of
\mathbb{C}
-HS are filtered morphisms. A polarization of
V
is a hermitian form
h
on
V
such that the splitting
V=\bigoplus_{p+q=w}V^{p,q}
is orthogonal and
\bigoplus_{p+q=k}(-1)^{p}h|_{V^{p,q}}
is positive definite.

Note that any
\mathbb{C}
-HS is polarizable.

Definition 4.12
.

A complex mixed Hodge structure (
\mathbb{C}
-MHS) is a quadruple
(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet})
where
V
is a finite-dimensional
\mathbb{C}
-vector space,
W_{\bullet}
is an increasing filtration (called the weight filtration), and
F^{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet}
are decreasing filtrations, such that
(\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{k}V,F^{\bullet}\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{k}V,F^{\prime\bullet}\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{k}V)
is a weight
k

\mathbb{C}
-HS for all
k
. Equivalently, we ask that
\operatorname{gr}^{p}_{F}\operatorname{gr}^{q}_{F^{\prime}}\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{k}V=0
if
p+q\neq k
.

Morphisms of
\mathbb{C}
-MHS are morphisms compatible with all three filtrations. A graded polarization
h_{\bullet}
is a polarization
h_{k}
on the graded object
\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{k}V
for each
k
.

Note that any
\mathbb{C}
-MHS is graded-polarizable. Morphisms of
\mathbb{C}
-MHS are automatically strict for each filtration, and so the category of
\mathbb{C}
-MHS is abelian.

4.2.2.
Over a point: twistor case

Definition 4.13
.

A complex mixed twistor structure (
\mathbb{C}
-MTS) is a pair
(V,W_{\bullet})
where
V
is a locally free coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-module on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
W_{\bullet}
is an increasing locally split filtration (called the weight filtration) by
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-submodules such that for each
k
,
\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{k}V\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(k)^{n_{k}}
for some
n_{k}
. A
\mathbb{C}
-MTS is pure of weight
k
if
\operatorname{gr}_{j}^{W}V=0
for all
j\neq k
. A morphism of
\mathbb{C}
-MTS is a filtered morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-modules.

Morphisms of mixed twistor structure are automatically strict with respect to the weight filtration.

4.2.3.
Splittings of
\mathbb{C}
-MTS

Deligne
[
35
]
shows the existence of functorial splittings of either
(W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
or
(W_{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet})
in the category of
\mathbb{C}
-MHS. We do the same for
\mathbb{C}
-MTS.

It is first useful to have the following version of the Rees construction, which is the twistor version of
[
95
]
. Let
\mathbb{L}
be the total space of the line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with the scaling action by
\mathbf{G}_{m}
,
\mathbf{0}_{\mathbb{L}}\subset\mathbb{L}
the zero section, and
\pi:\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
the projection. There is a natural equivalence of categories between coherent sheaves
V
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-equivariant coherent sheaves
\mathcal{V}
on
\mathbb{L}\setminus\mathbf{0}_{\mathbb{L}}
given by pullback along the quotient
\mathbb{L}\setminus\mathbf{0}_{\mathbb{L}}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
by the
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-action. There is moreover an equivalence of categories between filtered
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-equivariant coherent sheaves
(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{W}_{\bullet})
on
\mathbb{L}\setminus\mathbf{0}_{\mathbb{L}}
and
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-equivariant coherent sheaves
\bar{\mathcal{V}}
on
\mathbb{L}
with no embedded points along the zero section.
In the forward direction, we associate to a filtered equivariant sheaf
(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{W}_{\bullet})
(corresponding to a filtered sheaf
(V,W_{\bullet})
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
) the equivariant sheaf on
\mathbb{L}
generated by
\pi^{*}W_{k}(-k\mathbf{0}_{\mathbb{L}})
, or equivalently generated by the sections of
\mathcal{W}_{k}
with torus weights
\geq k
. Note that
\bar{V}|_{\mathbf{0}_{\mathbb{L}}}
is canonically isomorphic to
\bigoplus_{k}\pi^{*}\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{k}V(-k)
, and
\pi^{*}\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{k}(-k)
has pure torus weight
-k
. An equivariant sheaf
\bar{\mathcal{V}}
on
\mathbb{L}
has a natural filtration
\bar{\mathcal{W}}_{k}
by sections of torus weight
\geq-k
, and we associate the restriction to
\mathbb{L}\setminus\mathbf{0}_{\mathbb{L}}
. We have therefore proven the following:

Lemma 4.14
.

There is a natural equivalence of categories as above between the category of complex mixed twistor structures and the category of
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-equivariant locally free sheaves on
\mathbb{L}
whose restriction to
\mathbf{0}_{\mathbb{L}}
is trivial.

As a consequence, we obtain a version of the Deligne splitting.

Lemma 4.15
.

Fix a point
\lambda\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Then every mixed twistor structure admits a functorial splitting of its weight filtration in restriction to
\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\{\lambda\}
. The splitting is moreover compatible with tensor products and duals.

Proof.

Let
s\subset\mathbb{L}
be a section of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
vanishing at
\lambda
. To any mixed twistor structure
(V,W_{\bullet})
, consider the corresponding
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-equivariant sheaf
\bar{\mathcal{V}}
on
\mathbb{L}
. Observe that since
\bar{\mathcal{V}}|_{\mathbf{0}_{\mathbb{L}}}
is trivial, the same is true for
\bar{\mathcal{V}}|_{s}
. Indeed, this is true in the pure case, since it is true for line bundles on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and so
\bar{\mathcal{V}}|_{s}
is an iterated extension of trivial vector bundles, hence trivial. It further follows that the filtration restricted to
s
is by trivial subbundles. If
\mathbf{0}_{x}
is the zero of
s
(which maps to
\lambda
), then the fiber of
\bar{\mathcal{V}}
at
\mathbf{0}_{x}
has a canonical grading which splits the weight filtration. Since
\bar{\mathcal{V}}|_{s}\cong H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}|_{s})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{s}
and the filtration is induced by the filtration on
H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}|_{s})
, the claim follows. Note that the splitting only depends on
\lambda
and not
s
, since there is a unique
s
vanishing at
\lambda
up to scale, and the splitting is functorial and compatible with tensor products and duals.
∎

In the case of
\mathbb{C}
-MHS (that is, the
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-equivariant
\mathbb{C}
-MTS case), we obtain the two Deligne splittings by taking sections vanishing at the two torus fixed points, since the fibers there are canonically
\operatorname{gr}_{F}\operatorname{gr}^{W}
and
\operatorname{gr}_{F^{\prime}}\operatorname{gr}^{W}
.

4.2.4.
Over a base: Hodge case

Definition 4.16
.

Let
X
be a complex manifold. A graded polarizable complex variation of mixed Hodge structures (
\mathbb{C}
-VMHS) on
X
is a quadruple
(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet})
where

•

V
is a complex local system;

•

W_{\bullet}
is a flat increasing filtration of
V
(called the weight filtration);

•

F^{\bullet}
is a locally split decreasing filtration of
\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}V
such that
\nabla F^{p}\subset F^{p-1}\otimes\Omega_{X}
for each
p
, where
\nabla
is the natural flat connection;

•

F^{\prime\bullet}
is a locally split decreasing filtration of
\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}V
such that
\bar{\nabla}F^{\prime p}\subset F^{\prime p-1}\otimes\bar{\Omega}_{X}
for each
p
, where
\bar{\nabla}
is the natural flat connection;

•

There exists a flat hermitian form
h_{k}
on each
\operatorname{gr}_{k}^{W}V
such that for each
x\in X
,
(V_{x},(W_{\bullet})_{x},(h_{\bullet})_{x},F_{x}^{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet}_{x})
is a graded polarized
\mathbb{C}
-MHS.

A
\mathbb{C}
-VMHS for which
\operatorname{gr}_{k}^{W}V=0
for all but one
k
is a polarizable complex variation of pure Hodge structures (
\mathbb{C}
-VHS). A morphism of
\mathbb{C}
-VMHS is a morphism of local systems which is compatible with all three filtrations.

For background on the definitions of (pre)-admissibility, see
[
119
,
63
]
.

Definition 4.17
.

Let
(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet})
be a
\mathbb{C}
-VMHS on the punctured disk
\Delta^{*}
. Assume
V
has unipotent monodromy. We say
(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet})
is pre-admissible if:

•

F^{\bullet}
(resp.
F^{\prime\bullet}
) extends to a filtration of the Deligne extension
\mathcal{V}
(resp.
\bar{\mathcal{V}}
) of
(\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}V,\nabla)
(resp.
(\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}V,\bar{\nabla})
) such that each
\operatorname{gr}_{F}^{p}\operatorname{gr}_{k}^{\mathcal{W}}\mathcal{V}
(resp.
\operatorname{gr}_{F^{\prime}}^{p}\operatorname{gr}_{k}^{\bar{\mathcal{W}}}\bar{\mathcal{V}}
) is locally free.

•

There exists a relative monodromy-weight filtration.

Definition 4.18
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a log smooth compact manifold with
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. An admissible graded polarizable complex variation of mixed Hodge structures (
\mathbb{C}
-AVMHS) on
X
is a
\mathbb{C}
-VMHS
(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet})
such that

•

V
has quasiunipotent local monodromy.

•

For any map
f:(\Delta,0)\to(\bar{X},D)
such that
f^{*}V
has unipotent local monodromy,
f^{*}(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet})
is pre-admissible.

4.2.5.
Over a base: twistor case

Example 4.19
.

Let
X
be a complex manifold. Let
\mathbf{V}=(\mathcal{V},h,\nabla)
be a tame harmonic bundle on
X
with underlying
C^{\infty}
bundle
\mathcal{V}
and
\nabla=\nabla_{h}+\theta+\theta^{*}
(see
Section

7.3
for background). Let
\mathscr{A}_{X}:=C^{\infty}_{X}\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
be the sheaf of
C^{\infty}
functions on
X_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}=X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
which are holomorphic in the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
direction. Let
\mathscr{V}:=\mathcal{V}\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
, which is naturally an
\mathscr{A}_{X}
-module on
X_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
. Then, choosing generating sections
x,y
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
vanishing at
0
and
\infty
respectively, there is a natural
x\partial_{X_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}+y\bar{\partial}_{X_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-connection
\mathscr{D}:\mathscr{V}\to\mathscr{V}\otimes\Omega_{X_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
given by
\mathscr{D}=xD^{\prime}+yD^{\prime\prime}
where
D^{\prime}=\nabla_{h}^{1,0}+\theta^{*}
and
D^{\prime\prime}=\nabla_{h}^{0,1}+\theta
which satisfies the integrability condition
0=\mathscr{D}^{2}=x^{2}D^{\prime 2}+xy(D^{\prime}D^{\prime\prime}+D^{\prime\prime}D^{\prime})+y^{2}D^{\prime\prime 2}
. The resulting
(\mathscr{V},\mathscr{D})
is the variation of pure twistor structures (
\mathbb{C}
-VTS) of weight 0 associated to the tame harmonic bundle
\mathbf{V}
. If the harmonic bundle is tame and purely imaginary, the harmonic metric is unique up to flat automorphism, and such an automorphism induces an isomorphism of the associated
\mathbb{C}
-VTS. Thus, any semisimple complex local system underlies a
\mathbb{C}
-VTS which is unique up to isomorphism.

Definition 4.20
.

Let
X
be a complex manifold. A graded polarized variation of mixed twistor structures (
\mathbb{C}
-VMTS) on
X
is a triple
(\mathscr{V},W_{\bullet}\mathscr{V},\mathscr{D})
where

•

\mathscr{V}
is a
\mathscr{A}_{X}
-module on
X_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
;

•

\mathscr{D}:\mathscr{V}\to\mathscr{V}\otimes\Omega_{X_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
is an integrable
x\partial_{X_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}+y\bar{\partial}_{X_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-connection as above;

•

W_{\bullet}\mathscr{V}
is a
\mathscr{D}
-flat increasing filtration of
\mathcal{V}
(called the weight filtration);

•

There exists a harmonic hermitian metric
h_{k}
on each
\operatorname{gr}_{k}^{W}\mathscr{V}
such that
(\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{k}\mathscr{V},\operatorname{gr}_{k}^{W}\mathscr{D})
is the
\mathbb{C}
-VTS of weight
k
associated to a tame purely imaginary harmonic bundle by shifting by
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(k)
(see
Section

7.3
).

A morphism of
\mathbb{C}
-VMTS is a morphism of
\mathscr{A}_{X}
-modules which is compatible with the filtration and the operator
\mathscr{D}
.

Remark 4.21
.

Mochizuki
[
86
]
develops the theory quite generally, but we will only consider tame purely imaginary variations of mixed twistor structure. In fact, only the results of this section in the quasiunipotent local monodromy case will be used.

The admissibility conditions are more complicated in the twistor case, so we make the following definition without fully explaining what twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules are. See
[
86
]
for details.

Definition 4.22
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a log smooth projective variety with
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. A
\mathbb{C}
-AVMTS on
X
is a mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module on
\bar{X}
whose restriction to
X
underlies a tame purely imaginary graded polarized variation of mixed twistor structures as above.

4.3.
Ext groups over a point

We employ
Notation

4.1
, so most results are stated for “mixed structures”. For notational simplicity we denote “
\Hom_{\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MS}}
” and “
\Hom_{D^{b}(\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MS})}
” by “
\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}
”, and “
\Ext^{i}_{\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MS}}
” by “
\Ext^{i}_{\mathrm{MS}}
”

Lemma 4.23

(Beilinson
[
11
, Cor. 1.10]
+\epsilon
)
.

Let
M
and
N
be two
\mathbb{C}
-MS. Then
\Ext^{i}_{\mathrm{MS}}(M,N)=0
for
i>1
. Thus, for any object
E
of
D^{b}(\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MS})
, there is a noncanonical isomorphism
E\cong\bigoplus_{i}\mathscr{H}^{i}(E)[-i]
.

Proof.

The result of Beilinson is in the case of
\mathbb{R}
-MHSs, and since the functor
V\mapsto V\oplus\bar{V}
is an exact functor to the category of
\mathbb{R}
-MHS, so we obtain the result for
\mathbb{C}
-MHSs.

In the twistor case, morphisms of mixed twistor structure are strict with respect to the weight filtration, so
W_{0}
is an exact functor. Clearly
H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},W_{0}\Hom(M,N))=\Hom_{\mathrm{MTS}}(M,N)
, and the claim follows.
∎

As usual, the splitting is not canonical, but there is a canonical filtration which is noncanonically split.

Corollary 4.24
.

For any object
M
of
D^{b}(\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MS})
, there is a canonical short exact sequence

0\to\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),\mathscr{H}^{i-1}(M))\to\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),M[i])\to\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),\mathscr{H}^{i}(M))\to 0.

In fact, the proof of
[
11
]
shows that, for any object
M
of
D^{b}(\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MHS})
(thought of as a complex), there is a natural identification

R\Hom_{\mathrm{MHS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),M)=[F^{0}W_{0}M\oplus F^{\prime 0}W_{0}M\xrightarrow{\iota-\iota^{\prime}}W_{0}M]

where
\iota:F^{0}W_{0}M\to W_{0}M
and
\iota^{\prime}:F^{\prime 0}W_{0}M\to W_{0}M
are the inclusions, since the functors
F^{0}W_{0},F^{\prime 0}W_{0},W_{0}
are exact by strictness of the morphisms in
(\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MHS})
. Putting this together with the proof of
Lemma

4.23
in the twistor case, we obtain:

Corollary 4.25
.

(1)

For any
\mathbb{C}
-MHS
M
, we have a natural identification

\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MHS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),M)\cong W_{0}M/F^{0}W_{0}M+F^{\prime 0}W_{0}M.

(2)

For any
\mathbb{C}
-MTS
M
, we have a natural identification

\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MTS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),M)\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1},W_{0}M).

We can think of how an element
\alpha\in\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(M,N)
acts on an extension

0\to N\to E\to M\to 0

in the two cases as follows. In the Hodge case we represent
\alpha
by
f\in W_{0}\Hom(M,N)
and twist
F^{\prime\bullet}
by the endomorphism
1+f
of
E
. In the twistor case, we may represent
\alpha
by a Čech 1-cocyle
f
valued in
W_{0}\Hom(M,N)
, and twist the gluing map for the filtered bundle
E
by
1+f
. The result is a mixed twistor structure by the twistor version of
[
46
, Lemma 1.6]
using
Lemma

4.14
.

4.4.
The results of Saito, Sabbah and Mochizuki

In this section we review the results of Saito, Simpson, Sabbah, and Mochizuki putting functorial mixed Hodge/twistor structures on the cohomology groups of admissible variations of mixed Hodge/twistor structures.

4.4.1.
Hodge case

Cohomology groups of
\mathbb{C}
-AVMS are equipped with functorial
\mathbb{C}
-MSs by using Saito’s theory of mixed Hodge modules in the Hodge case and Mochizuki’s theory of mixed twistor modules in the twistor case. The latter is developed in the case of complex coefficients, so we may quote the necessary results. In the former case, most of the results in the literature are stated in the case of
\mathbb{Q}
or
\mathbb{R}
coefficients, so we take some time to precisely define the required notions for complex coefficients. For background, see
[
99
,
100
]
, and specifically
[
101
]
for the case of real coefficients. The extension to complex coefficients is discussed in
[
40
, §3.2]
.

For any algebraic space
X
, there is a category
\operatorname{MHM}(X,\mathbb{R})
of algebraic real mixed Hodge modules. The algebraicity condition means they extend to an algebraic compacification. For smooth
X
, the category of pure Hodge modules
\operatorname{HM}(X,\mathbb{R})
is a subcategory of the category
\operatorname{MF}(X,\mathbb{R})
of filtered regular holonomic
D
-modules with real structure
(M,F^{\bullet},\alpha,V)
, where
(M,F^{\bullet})
is a filtered regular holonomic
D
-module,
V
a
\mathbb{R}
-perverse sheaf on
X
, and
\alpha:\operatorname{DR}(M)\to\mathbb{C}_{X}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}_{X}}V
an isomorphism in
D^{b}(\mathbb{C}_{X})
between the De Rham complex of
M
and
\mathbb{C}_{X}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}_{X}}V
. The category of mixed Hodge modules is then a subcategory of the category
\operatorname{MFW}(X,\mathbb{R})
of filtered objects
(\mathcal{M},W_{\bullet})
in
\operatorname{MF}(X,\mathbb{R})
.

Again for smooth
X
, the subcategory of smooth objects in
\operatorname{MHM}(X,\mathbb{R})
(namely those for which
V
is a shifted local system) is equivalent to the category of admissible graded polarizable real variations of mixed Hodge structures on
X

[
101
]
. For arbitrary
X
, we can still make sense of the category
\operatorname{MFW}(X,\mathbb{R})
locally using an embedding in an ambient smooth variety and gluing, or globally for projective
X
using a projective embedding, and this is how
\operatorname{MHM}(X,\mathbb{R})
is defined. Note that the category
\operatorname{MFW}(X,\mathbb{C})
can be naturally defined as well, although in this case the complex perverse sheaf is redundant.

The derived category
D^{b}\operatorname{MHM}(X,\mathbb{R})
admits a faithful exact functor

\mathrm{coeff}:D^{b}\operatorname{MHM}(X,\mathbb{R})\to D^{b}_{c}(\mathbb{R}_{X})

to the derived category of constructible
\mathbb{R}_{X}
-modules by extracting the underlying
\mathbb{R}
-perverse sheaf. The restriction to
\operatorname{MHM}(X,\mathbb{R})
is faithful and exact. For algebraic maps
f:X\to Y
, the categories
D^{b}\operatorname{MHM}(X,\mathbb{R})
admit natural exact functors
f_{*},f^{*},f_{!},f^{!},\mathbb{D}_{X},\otimes
which are compatible with the corresponding functors on
D^{b}(\mathbb{R}_{X^{\mathrm{an}}})
via
\mathrm{coeff}
.

As in
[
40
, §3.2]
, we make the following definition.

Definition 4.26
.

Let
X
be an algebraic space. An algebraic complex mixed Hodge module is a direct factor of an algebraic real mixed Hodge module in
\operatorname{MFW}(X,\mathbb{C})
. A morphism of algebraic complex mixed Hodge modules
f:M\to N
is a morphism in
\operatorname{MFW}(X,\mathbb{C})
which arises from restriction to direct factors of a morphism of algebraic real mixed Hodge modules. We denote the category of algebraic complex mixed Hodge modules by
\operatorname{MHM}(X,\mathbb{C})
, and the derived category by
D^{b}\operatorname{MHM}(X,\mathbb{C})
.

We have the following version of the six functor formalism.

Theorem 4.27

(Saito
[
99
,
100
]
)
.

Let
X
be an algebraic space.

(1)

There is a natural faithful exact functor

\mathrm{coeff}:D^{b}\operatorname{MHM}(X,\mathbb{C})\to D^{b}_{c}(\mathbb{C}_{X})

by passing to the underlying perverse sheaf.

(2)

There are natural functors
f_{*},f^{*},f_{!},f^{!},\mathbb{D}_{X},\otimes
(the first four associated to any algebraic map
f:X\to Y
) lifting the corresponding functors on
D^{b}(\mathbb{C}_{X})
. The pair
(f^{*},f_{*})
is adjoint, and
f^{!}=\mathbb{D}_{X}f^{*}\mathbb{D}_{X},f_{!}=\mathbb{D}_{X}f_{*}\mathbb{D}_{X}
.

(3)

For smooth
X
, the category of smooth objects of
\operatorname{MHM}(X,\mathbb{C})
is equivalent to the category of
\mathbb{C}
-AVMHS.

Proof.

The functors are defined on the level of filtered
D
-modules and compatible with taking direct summands, hence follow from the case of real coefficients.
∎

4.4.2.
Twistor case

We denote by
\operatorname{MTM}^{\mathrm{tpi}}(X,\mathbb{C})
the full subcategory of the category of mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules consisting of tame purely imaginary algebraic mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules. The algebraicity condition means the twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module extends to
\bar{X}
.

Theorem 4.28

(Sabbah
[
98
]
, Mochizuki
[
83
,
84
,
86
]
)
.

Let
X
be an algebraic space.

(1)

For each
\lambda\in\mathbf{G}_{m}
there are natural exact specialization functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}\operatorname{MTM}^{\mathrm{tpi}}(X,\mathbb{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}\operatorname{Hol}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{c}(\mathbb{C}_{X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sp}_{\lambda}^{DR}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sp}_{\lambda}^{B}}

by scaling the
\lambda
-connection to the derived category of regular holonomic
D
-modules and the derived category of constructible sheaves, where the right functor is the Riemann–Hilbert functor.

(2)

For
\lambda=1
,
\mathrm{sp}^{B}_{1}
is faithful, and there are natural functors
f_{*},f^{*},f_{!},f^{!},\mathbb{D}_{X},\otimes
(the first four associated to any algebraic map
f:X\to Y
) commuting with the corresponding functors on
D^{b}(\mathbb{C}_{X})
via
\mathrm{sp}^{B}_{1}
. The pair
(f^{*},f_{*})
is adjoint, and
f^{!}=\mathbb{D}_{X}f^{*}\mathbb{D}_{X},f_{!}=\mathbb{D}_{X}f_{*}\mathbb{D}_{X}
.

4.4.3.
Consequences

We now deduce some consequences in both the Hodge and twistor case. By a “mixed module” we mean either an algebraic complex mixed Hodge module or a tame purely imaginary algebraic mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module. We denote the category of mixed modules by
\operatorname{MM}(X,\mathbb{C})
and the derived category by
D^{b}\operatorname{MM}(X,\mathbb{C})
. We make the following definition (see also
[
8
]
):

Definition 4.29
.

For a connected algebraic space
X
, we define a
\mathbb{C}
-AVMS to be a smooth object of
D^{b}\operatorname{MM}(X,\mathbb{C})
whose image under
\mathrm{coeff}
in the Hodge case (or the specialization at
\lambda=1
in the twistor case) is supported in degree 0 (with respect to the standard t-structure)—that is, an object whose image under
\mathrm{coeff}
is a local system in degree 0.

We abusively denote “
\Hom_{D^{b}\operatorname{MM}(X,\mathbb{C})}
” by “
\Hom_{\operatorname{MM}}
”. Note however that if
X
is not smooth, smooth objects of
D^{b}\operatorname{MM}(X,\mathbb{C})
will not generally be elements of
\operatorname{MM}(X,\mathbb{C})
, even up to shifts. We also denote by
(\mathbb{C}_{X}\text{-}\mathrm{Mod})
the category of constructible
\mathbb{C}_{X}
-modules and we likewise denote “
\Hom_{D^{b}_{c}(\mathbb{C}_{X})}
” by “
\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}
”. Note that if
\operatorname{LS}(X,\mathbb{C})
is the category of complex local systems, then the natural functor
\operatorname{LS}(X,\mathbb{C})\to(\mathbb{C}_{X}\text{-}\mathrm{Mod})
is fully faithful with extension closed image.

Lemma 4.30
.

Let
X
be an algebraic space and
M,N
two
\mathbb{C}
-AVMSs on
X
(supported in the same degree).

(1)

The groups
\Ext^{i}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(M,N)
carry functorial
\mathbb{C}
-MSs.

(2)

There is a natural short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(M,N))

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\operatorname{MM}}(M,N[1])

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),\Ext^{1}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(M,N))\to 0

Corollary 4.31

(cf.
[
30
, Lemma 4.3]
)
.

For
X,M,N
as above, an extension of local systems

0\to N\to E\to M\to 0

can be lifted to a distinguished triangle of smooth objects in
D^{b}\operatorname{MM}(X,\mathbb{C})
if and only if the extension class in
\Ext^{1}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(M,N)
is a Tate class. In this case, the set of isomorphism classes of such lifts is a torsor under
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(M,N))
, which acts naturally on the filtrations
F^{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet}
in the underlying
\mathbb{C}
-AVMS via the identification of
Corollary

4.25
.

Proof of
Lemma

4.30
.

(1) follows from
Theorem

4.27
, since for
M,N
smooth we have
\Ext^{i}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(M,N)\cong H^{i}(X,\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.99997pt\operatorname{om}(M,N))
.

For (2), let
\mathrm{pt}_{X}:X\to\Spec\mathbb{C}
be the map to a point.
Because
M
and
N
are smooth we may naturally identify

\Hom_{\operatorname{MM}}(M,N[1])\cong\Hom_{\operatorname{MM}}(\mathrm{pt}_{X}^{*}\mathbf{T}(0),\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.99997pt\operatorname{om}(M,N)[1])

and thus assume
M=\mathrm{pt}_{X}^{*}\mathbf{T}(0)
.
There is an adjunction morphism
\mathrm{pt}_{X}^{*}\mathrm{pt}_{X*}\to 1
in
D^{b}\operatorname{MM}(X,\mathbb{C})
. Thus, we have a natural identification

\Hom_{\operatorname{MM}}(\mathrm{pt}_{X}^{*}\mathbf{T}(0),N[1])\cong\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),\mathrm{pt}_{X*}N[1]).

Since
\mathscr{H}^{i}\mathrm{pt}_{X*}N=H^{i}(X,N)
, by
Lemma

4.23
, there is a natural short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),H^{0}(X,N)[1])

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),\mathrm{pt}_{X*}N[1])

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),H^{1}(X,N))\to 0

which yields the claim.
∎

4.5.
Recollections from deformation theory

We briefly recall some elements of deformation theory as in
[
118
,
Tag 06G7
]
. In practice, one can often get by with the more classical deformation functor of isomorphism classes, but the entire category of deformations (which is naturally fibered in groupoids over the category of artinian affine schemes) is more natural.

Let
\Lambda
be a complete local noetherian
\mathbb{C}
-algebra with residue field
\mathbb{C}
. Let
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
be the category of local artinian
\Lambda
-algebras. We identify
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)^{\mathrm{op}}
with the category of artinian local
\Lambda
-schemes. A deformation category is a category cofibered in groupoids
\mathcal{D}\to(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
(meaning
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{op}}\to(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)^{\mathrm{op}}
is fibered in groupoids) for which
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{C})
is equivalent to a point and such that the Rim–Schlessinger gluing condition holds: for any morphisms
A_{1}\to A
and
A_{2}\to A
in
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
with
A_{1}\to A
surjective, the natural functor
\mathcal{D}(A_{1}\times_{A}A_{2})\to\mathcal{D}(A_{1})\times_{\mathcal{D}(A)}\mathcal{D}(A_{2})
is an equivalence of categories
4
4

4

The fiber product is a fiber product of groupoids, so the objects are pairs
(x_{1},x_{2})
of objects in
\mathcal{D}(A_{1})
and
\mathcal{D}(A_{2})
with a choice of isomorphism of the pullbacks to
A
.
. The condition in particular implies the set of isomorphism classes in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}\epsilon)
(where
\epsilon^{2}=0
and
\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}
kills
\epsilon
) has the structure of a
\mathbb{C}
-vector space, called the tangent space
\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}}
. It also implies that there is a
\mathbb{C}
-vector space
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathcal{D}}
(the infinitesimal automorphisms) which is identified with the group of automorphisms of any object in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}\epsilon)
. We assume both to be finite-dimensional.

For any
A\in(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
, we define
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)_{A}
to be the category of
A^{\prime}\in(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
equipped with a morphism
A^{\prime}\to A
. For
x\in\mathcal{D}(A)
, we define
\mathcal{D}_{x}
to be the category cofibered in groupoids over the category
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)_{A}
whose objects are morphisms
x^{\prime}\to x
in
\mathcal{D}
lying over
A^{\prime}\to A
in
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)_{A}
and whose morphisms are morphisms of
\mathcal{D}
commuting with the map to
x
. For
A^{\prime}\in(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)_{A}
the groupoid
\mathcal{D}_{x}(A^{\prime})
is then the category of lifts of
x
to
A^{\prime}
.

For a complete noetherian local
\Lambda
-algebra
(B,\mathfrak{m}_{B})
with residue field
\mathbb{C}
, a formal point
\hat{x}\in\mathcal{D}(B):=\lim\mathcal{D}(B/\mathfrak{m}_{B}^{n})
consists of an assignment to each

n
of objects
x_{n}
of
\mathcal{D}
lying over
B/\mathfrak{m}_{B}^{n+1}
and morphisms
x_{n+1}\to x_{n}
lying over
B/\mathfrak{m}_{B}^{m+2}\to B/\mathfrak{m}_{B}^{n+1}
. The formal point
\hat{x}
is versal if for any solid diagram in
\mathcal{D}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B/\mathfrak{m}_{B}^{n+1}}
lying above
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B/\mathfrak{m}_{B}^{m+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}

where
A^{\prime}\to A
is surjective, there exists an
m\geq n
and the dashed arrows making the diagram commute, where the vertical arrows are the canonical ones. The formal object
\hat{x}
is miniversal if in addition the natural map on
\Lambda
-tangent spaces
\mathfrak{t}_{B/\Lambda}\to\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}}
is an isomorphism, where
\mathfrak{t}_{B/\Lambda}:=\operatorname{Der}_{\Lambda}(B,\mathbb{C})\cong(\mathfrak{m}_{B}/\mathfrak{m}_{B}^{2}+\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}B)^{\vee}
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an algebraic stack over
\mathbb{C}
(which we think of as a category fibered in groupoids over the category
(\mathrm{Aff}/\mathbb{C})
of affine
\mathbb{C}
-schemes). For any
A\in(\mathrm{Art}/\mathbb{C})
and any
A
-point
x\in\mathcal{X}(A)
, we define
\mathcal{X}_{x}
to be the category fibered in groupoids over the category
(\mathrm{Art}/\mathbb{C})_{A}^{\mathrm{op}}
whose objects are morphisms
x\to x^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{X}
lying over
\Spec A\to\Spec A^{\prime}
in
(\mathrm{Art}/\mathbb{C})_{A}^{\mathrm{op}}
and whose morphisms are morphisms of
\mathcal{X}
commuting with the map from
x
. The groupoid
\mathcal{X}_{x}(A^{\prime})
is then the category of lifts of
x
to
A^{\prime}
, and if
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}(\mathbb{C})
,
(\mathcal{X}_{x_{0}})^{\mathrm{op}}
is a deformation category.

We pause to make these notions more concrete for
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
. We use the following terminology.

Definition 4.32
.

Let
A
be a
\mathbb{C}
-algebra and
X
a topological space homeomorphic to a finite CW-complex. By a (free)
A
-local system on
X
we mean a local system of finitely generated (free)
A
-modules on
X
.

4.5.1.

An object
V
of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
lying over
\Spec(A)\in(\mathrm{Aff}/\mathbb{C})
is a free
A
-local system on
X
, and a morphism
V\to V^{\prime}
in the category
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
lying over a morphism
\Spec(A)\to\Spec(A^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{Aff}_{\mathbb{C}}
consists of a free
A
-local system
V
(resp. a free
A^{\prime}
-local system
V^{\prime}
) and a morphism
V^{\prime}\to V
of
A^{\prime}
-local systems which is an isomorphism upon tensoring with
A
(we say it is an isomorphism over
A
).

4.5.2.

For
V_{0}\in\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
, the category
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)_{V_{0}}
is the category of free
A
-local systems
V
for
A\in(\mathrm{Art}/\mathbb{C})
equipped with a morphism
V\to V_{0}
of
A
-local systems which is an isomorphism over
\mathbb{C}
. Morphisms in
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)_{V_{0}}
are morphisms (of the
V
factor) in
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
commuting with the structure map to
V_{0}
.

4.5.3.

More generally, for
A\in\mathrm{Art}/\mathbb{C}
and a free
A
-local system
V
,
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)_{V}
is the category whose objects are pairs
(A^{\prime}\to A,V^{\prime}\to V)
where
A^{\prime}\to A
is in
(\mathrm{Art}/\mathbb{C})_{A}
,
V^{\prime}
is a free
A^{\prime}
-local system, and
V^{\prime}\to V
is a morphism of
A^{\prime}
-local systems which is an isomorphism over
A
. Morphisms in
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)_{V}
are morphisms in
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
(of the
V^{\prime}
factor) commuting with the structure map to
V
. Note that if
A^{\prime}\to A
is surjective with ideal
J
, then
V^{\prime}\to V
is an isomorphism over
A
if and only if it is surjective with kernel is
JV^{\prime}
.

4.5.4.

For a complete noetherian ring
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
, a formal point of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
over
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
arises from an ordinary
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
-point, namely a free
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
-local system
V
.
Then
V
is versal if the morphism
\Spec\hat{\mathcal{O}}\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
is formally smooth, meaning that for any:

•

surjection
A^{\prime}\to A
of artinian
\mathbb{C}
-algebras;

•

\mathbb{C}
-algebra homomorphism
\hat{\mathcal{O}}\to A
;

•

free
A
-local system
U
with a morphism
V\to U
of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
-local systems which is an isomorphism over
A
;

•

free
A^{\prime}
-local system
U^{\prime}
with a morphism
U^{\prime}\to U
of
A^{\prime}
-local systems which is an isomorphism over
A
;

there is a lift
\hat{\mathcal{O}}\to A^{\prime}
of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}\to A
and a lift
V\to U^{\prime}
of
V\to U
which is an isomorphism over
A^{\prime}
.

In particular, for any
A\in\mathrm{Art}/\mathbb{C}
, any free
A
-local system
U
, and any isomorphism
V/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{O}}V\to U/\mathfrak{m}_{A}U
, there is a
\mathbb{C}
-algebra homomorphism
\hat{\mathcal{O}}\to A
and a morphism
V\to U
of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
-local systems which is an isomorphism over
A
and equal to
V/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{O}}V\to U/\mathfrak{m}_{A}U
over
\mathbb{C}
. If
V
is miniversal then the map
\hat{\mathcal{O}}\to A
is uniquely determined to first order.

Returning to the general setup, we now briefly describe Schlessinger’s construction of a miniversal formal point of
\mathcal{D}
. A canonical first order object
y_{1}
is constructed over
\mathcal{O}_{1}=\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\vee}
(with the trivial
\Lambda
-algebra structure), using the gluing condition. Let
S=\Lambda[[\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\vee}]]=\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes\Sym^{*}\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\vee}
be the formal power series ring over
\Lambda
(over a basis of
\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\vee}
) and let
S\to\mathcal{O}_{1}
be the canonical quotient which is the identity on
\Lambda
-tangent spaces. Suppose we have inductively constructed a quotient
S\to\mathcal{O}_{n}
as well as an object
y_{n}
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{O}_{n})
lifting
y_{1}
. Let
I
be the ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{n}
in
S
, and form the maximal small extension of
\mathcal{O}_{n}
which is an isomorphism on tangent spaces, explicitly given by

(4.5.1)

0\to I/\mathfrak{m}_{S}I\to S/\mathfrak{m}_{S}I\to\mathcal{O}_{n}\to 0.

Then (by the gluing condition again) there is a smallest ideal
I^{\prime}
with
\mathfrak{m}_{S}I\subset I^{\prime}\subset I
such that
y_{n}
lifts to
S/I^{\prime}
, and we take
y_{n+1}
to be any such lift and
\mathcal{O}_{n+1}=S/I^{\prime}
.

Theorem 4.33

(Schlessinger
[
102
]
, see also
[
118
,
Tag 06IX
]
)
.

The formal point
\hat{y}=\lim y_{n}
over
\hat{\mathcal{O}}=\lim\mathcal{O}_{n}
is miniversal for
\mathcal{D}
.

4.6.
Pro-Hodge/twistor structures and their variations

In this section we collect some straightforward definitions that will be needed for the sequel.

Definition 4.34
.

(1)

By a pro-
\mathbb{C}
-MS we mean a pro-object of the category of
\mathbb{C}
-MS.

(2)

By a pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebra we mean a
\mathbf{T}(0)
-algebra object in the category of pro-
\mathbb{C}
-MS. Likewise, for any pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebra
\Lambda
, by a pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebra we mean a pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebra
A
equipped with a morphism
\Lambda\to A
of pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebras.

(3)

We say a pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebra is (complete/noetherian/artinian/local) if the underlying
\mathbb{C}
-algebra is.

(4)

An ideal
I
of a pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebra is a
\mathbb{C}
-MS-ideal if it is a sub-pro-
\mathbb{C}
-MS.

(5)

For
\Lambda
a pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebra, we define a pro-
\Lambda
-MS-module as a pro-
\Lambda
-module object in the category of
\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MS}
.

In practice, all of the pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebras we will consider will be complete noetherian local rings
\Lambda
, where each
\Lambda/\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}^{k}
is equipped with a
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebra structure such that the quotient maps
\Lambda/\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}^{k+1}\to\Lambda/\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}^{k}
are morphisms of
\mathbb{C}
-MS. These algebras are particularly well-behaved if
\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}=W_{-1}\Lambda
. If
\Lambda
is local complete noetherian and
\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}=W_{-1}\Lambda
, then each
\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{k}\Lambda
is finite-dimensional and
W_{0}\Lambda=\Lambda
.

Lemma 4.35
.

Let
A\to B
be a morphism of pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MTS-algebras. Then
A\to B
is locally trivial over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
as a morphism of sheaves of algebras.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that artinian
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MTS-algebras
\Lambda
are functorially trivializable on any (fixed) affine open subset of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and this is what we’ll show. First, observe that
\operatorname{gr}^{W}\Lambda
is trivial, since
R=\bigoplus_{k}H^{0}(\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{k}\Lambda(-k))
has a natural graded ring structure and we can identify
\operatorname{gr}^{W}\Lambda=\bigoplus_{k}R_{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(k)
as sheaves of algebras. Now, for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
\Lambda|_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\lambda}
has a splitting of the weight filtration compatible with the algebra structure thanks to
Lemma

4.15
, hence isomorphic to
\operatorname{gr}^{W}\Lambda|_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\lambda}
as a sheaf of algebras, and in particular trivial.
∎

Lemma 4.36
.

Let
\Lambda
be a local complete noetherian pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebra with
\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}=W_{-1}\Lambda
. Then the nilradical and minimal prime ideals are
\mathbb{C}
-MS-ideals.

Proof.

We first treat the Hodge case. First, by Deligne
[
35
]
there is a functorial splitting of
(W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
for any
\mathbb{C}
-MHS, and likewise for
(W_{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet})
. Observe that for a
\mathbb{C}
-MHS
V
and a subspace
U\subset V
, for
U
to be a sub-
\mathbb{C}
-MHS it suffices to show it is strict with respect to each splitting. By functoriality, the splittings for
\Lambda/\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}^{k}
are compatible with the algebra structure and the quotient maps, so in particular any element
x\in\Lambda
can be written as
x=\sum x^{p,q}
.

Now, take nonzero
x,y\in\Lambda
with splittings
x=\sum x^{p,q}
with respect to
(W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
, and likewise for
y
. Since
\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}=W_{-1}\Lambda
, there is a nonzero term
x^{p_{0},q_{0}}
with
(p_{0}+q_{0},p_{0})
lexicographically maximal. If
xy=0
, then it follows by induction that a power of
x^{p_{0}+q_{0},p_{0}}
annihilates each component of
y
. This first shows that if
x
is nilpotent, each component of
x
is nilpotent. Second, for any minimal prime
\mathfrak{p}
, if
y
is contained in the intersection of all other minimal primes and not in
\mathfrak{p}
while
x\in\mathfrak{p}
, then
xy
is nilpotent, and it follows that each component of
x
is contained in
\mathfrak{p}
. This completes the proof.

For the twistor case, by the same proof the nilradical and minimal prime ideals are graded with respect to the splittings of
Lemma

4.15
on two open sets
\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\lambda,\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\lambda^{\prime}
. Since the sheaf of algebras is Zariski locally trivial, each yields a subsheaf of ideals which is strictly compatible with the weight filtration. Moreover, each such ideal
I
maps isomorphically to an ideal of the corresponding type (either the nilradical or a minimal prime) of
\operatorname{gr}^{W}\Lambda
. The nilradical and minimal primes of the ring
R
from the proof of
Lemma

4.35
are graded, so the corresponding ideals of
\operatorname{gr}^{W}\Lambda
are twistor ideals, and this completes the proof.
∎

Definition 4.37
.

Let
X
a connected algebraic space and
\Lambda
a pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebra whose underlying algebra is a complete noetherian local
\mathbb{C}
-algebra. By a pro-
\Lambda
-AVMS on
X
we mean a pro-object of the category of
\mathbb{C}
-AVMS on
X
of the form
V=\lim V_{k}
where

(1)

V
has an underlying
\Lambda
-local system,

(2)

each
V_{k}:=V/\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}^{k+1}V
has the structure of a
\mathbb{C}
-AVMS for which the natural map
V_{k+1}\to V_{k}
is a morphism of
\mathbb{C}
-AVMS and for which
\Lambda/\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}^{k+1}\subset\End(V_{k})
is a sub-
\mathbb{C}
-MS.

We say a pro-
\Lambda
-AVMS is (free/miniversal) if the underlying
\Lambda
-local system is.

4.7.
Hodge/twistor enhancements of deformation categories

Let
\Lambda
be a complete local noetherian
\mathbb{C}
-algebra with residue field
\mathbb{C}
and let
\mathcal{D}
be a deformation category over
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
. An obstruction theory for
\mathcal{D}
consists of a complex vector space
\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{D}}
and an assignment, to each small extension
J\to A^{\prime}\to A
in
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
5
5

5

Meaning that
A^{\prime}\to A
is a surjective morphism in
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
whose kernel
J
is annihilated by
\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}
.
and
x\in\mathcal{D}(A)
, of an element
\mathrm{obs}_{A/A^{\prime}}(x)\in J\otimes\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{D}}
which detects obstructions in the sense that
\mathrm{obs}_{A/A^{\prime}}(x)=0
if and only if
x
lifts to
A^{\prime}
and which is functorial in the sense that for any morphism of small extensions
J_{1}\to A^{\prime}_{1}\to A_{1}
to
J_{2}\to A_{2}^{\prime}\to A_{2}
and an element
x_{1}\in\mathcal{D}(A_{1})
we have that
\mathrm{obs}_{A_{2}/A_{2}^{\prime}}(x_{2})
is the image of
\mathrm{obs}_{A_{1}/A_{1}^{\prime}}(x_{1})
under the natural map
J_{1}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{D}}\to J_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{D}}
, where
x_{2}
is the push-forward of
x_{1}
. See for example
[
48
, Definition 6.1.21]
for details.

Assume now
\Lambda
is further equipped with a
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebra structure and let
(\mathrm{MS}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
be the category of artinian local
\Lambda
-MS-algebras. Denote by
|-|:(\mathrm{MS}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)\to(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
the forgetful functor in the Hodge case, or the fiber at
1\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
in the twistor case, and likewise for
\Lambda
-MS-modules.

Let
A
be an object of
(\mathrm{MS}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
. Then
A=\mathbf{T}(0)\oplus\mathfrak{m}_{A}
as
\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MS}
, and the projection
A\to\mathfrak{m}_{A}
induces a natural group homomorphism
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathfrak{m}_{A},J)\to\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(A,J)
. It follows that via the natural group homomorphism
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}^{\vee},J)\to\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathfrak{m}_{A},J)
, the group
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}^{\vee},J)
acts on the set of extensions of
A
by
J
as
\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MS}
.

Lemma 4.38
.

For any small extension
J\to A^{\prime}\to A
in
(\mathrm{MS}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
, the group
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}^{\vee},J)
acts transitively on the set of small extensions
J\to B\to A
in
(\mathrm{MS}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
whose underlying small extension in
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
is
|J|\to|A^{\prime}|\to|A|
, up to isomorphisms of the diagram which are the identity on
J,A
.

Proof.

We have a commutative diagram with exact rows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J+\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}^{2}+\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{A}^{2}+\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

where the bottom row is the pullback extension. The bottom middle term is the image of
J\oplus(\mathfrak{m}_{A}\otimes_{A}\mathfrak{m}_{A})\oplus\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}A
under the natural maps associated to the
\Lambda
-algebra structure, and therefore has a fixed
\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MS}
. Moreover, the map from
J\oplus(\mathfrak{m}_{A}\otimes_{A}\mathfrak{m}_{A})\oplus\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}A
determines the
\Lambda
-algebra structure. Thus, the twist by an element of
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathfrak{m}_{A},J)
is compatible with the
\Lambda
-algebra structure if and only if the image in
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathfrak{m}_{A}^{2}+\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}A,J)
is trivial, which is the case if and only if it is in the image of the group
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}^{\vee},J)
.
∎

Proposition 4.39
.

Let
A
be an object of
(\mathrm{MS}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
with
\Lambda
-tangent space
\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}
. Let
S
be a pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebra with
|S|\cong\widehat{\Sym}^{*}_{|\Lambda|}|\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}^{\vee}|
admitting a quotient map
q:S\to A
of pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebras. Then for any small extension
J\to A^{\prime}\to A
in
(\mathrm{MS}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
with
\mathfrak{t}_{A^{\prime}/\Lambda}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}^{\vee}
, there is a possibly different pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebra structure
S^{\prime}
on
|S|
and a quotient map
q^{\prime}:S^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}
of pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebras lifting
|q|
.

Corollary 4.40
.

Any object
A
of
(\mathrm{MS}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
is a pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebra quotient of a pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebra
S
with
|S|\cong\widehat{\Sym}^{*}_{|\Lambda|}|\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}^{\vee}|
.

Proof of
Proposition

4.39
.

We first discuss pro-
\Lambda
-MHS-algebra structures on smooth
\Lambda
-algebras in the Hodge case. Let
\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}
be a
\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MHS}
and consider
S_{0}=\widehat{\Sym}^{*}_{|\Lambda|}|\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}|
. Given a lift
V\subset\mathfrak{m}_{S_{0}}
, by lifting either Deligne splitting of
\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}
we uniquely obtain a bigraded
\Lambda
-algebra structure on
S_{0}
(using the corresponding bigrading on
\Lambda
). Suppose
V,V^{\prime}
are two lifts; by lifting the Deligne splitting of
(W_{\bullet}\mathfrak{t}^{\vee},F^{\bullet}\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
(resp.
(W_{\bullet}\mathfrak{t}^{\vee},F^{\prime\bullet}\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
), we therefore obtain filtrations
(W_{\bullet}S_{0},F^{\bullet}S_{0})
(resp.
W_{\bullet}^{\prime}S_{0},F^{\prime\bullet}S_{0}
), giving
S_{0}
the structure of a bifiltered
\Lambda
-algebra, where
\Lambda
is given the filtrations
(W_{\bullet}\Lambda,F^{\bullet}\Lambda)
(resp.
(W_{\bullet}\Lambda,F^{\prime\bullet}\Lambda)
).

Claim
.

We have
W_{\bullet}V^{\prime}=W^{\prime}_{\bullet}V^{\prime}
if and only if
W_{\bullet}S_{0}=W_{\bullet}^{\prime}S_{0}
and
(W_{\bullet}S_{0},F^{\bullet}S_{0},F^{\prime\bullet}S_{0})
give
S_{0}
the structure of a pro-
\Lambda
-MHS-algebra.

Proof.

The reverse implication is clear. Consider the isomorphism
f:V\to V^{\prime}
given as the composition
V\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}\xleftarrow{\cong}V^{\prime}
, which maps
W_{\bullet}V
to
W_{\bullet}^{\prime}V^{\prime}
. There is a unique derivation
\delta\in\operatorname{Der}_{\Lambda}(S_{0},\mathfrak{m}_{S_{0}}^{2})^{\wedge}
such that
1+\delta
agrees with
f
on
V
. The resulting algebra automorphism
e^{\delta}:S_{0}\to S_{0}
clearly maps
W_{\bullet}S_{0}
to
W_{\bullet}^{\prime}S_{0}
. Since
e^{\delta}
is trivial on
\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}S_{0}
, it is nilpotent in each
S_{0}/\mathfrak{m}_{0}^{k}
, so
e^{\delta}
preserves
W_{\bullet}S_{0}
iff
\delta\in W_{0}\operatorname{Der}_{\Lambda}(S_{0},\mathfrak{m}_{S_{0}}^{2})^{\wedge}
iff
f\in W_{0}\Hom(V,V^{\prime})
(equipping
V^{\prime}
with
W_{\bullet}V^{\prime}
) iff
W_{\bullet}V^{\prime}=W_{\bullet}^{\prime}V^{\prime}
. Since the filtrations
(W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet},F^{\prime\bullet})
induce
\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MHS}
s on
\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}S_{0}
and are compatible with the algebra structure, it follows by
[
46
, Lemma 1.6]
that they give
S_{0}
the structure of a pro-
\Lambda
-MHS-algebra.
∎

We now handle the twistor case of the above claim, which is largely the same. Let
\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}
be a
\mathbb{C}
-MTS, choose
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\lambda_{0}
, and set
U^{(\prime)}=\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\lambda^{(\prime)}
. For lifts
V^{(\prime)}\subset\mathfrak{m}_{S_{0}}
of
|\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}|
, we obtain gradings on
S_{0}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{U^{(\prime)}}
(and filtrations
W_{\bullet},W^{\prime}_{\bullet}
) such that
e^{\delta}:S_{0}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{U\cap U^{\prime}}\to S_{0}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{U\cap U^{\prime}}
as in the previous proof maps
W_{\bullet}S_{0}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{U\cap U^{\prime}}
to
W_{\bullet}S_{0}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{U\cap U^{\prime}}
. If we have
W_{\bullet}(V^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{U\cap U^{\prime}})=W_{\bullet}^{\prime}V^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{U\cap U^{\prime}}
, then gluing the two filtrations produces a pro-
\Lambda
-MTS-algebra structure lifting
S_{0}
by the twistor version of
[
46
, Lemma 1.6]
using
Lemma

4.14
.

We now prove the proposition. Let
I
be the ideal of
A
. Any lift
q^{\prime}_{0}:|S|\to|A^{\prime}|
of
|q|
yields a map
|I|\to|J|
which is independent of the choice of lift. Since a lift can be chosen to be compatible with either Deligne splitting, it follows that
I\to J
is a morphism of pro-
\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MS}
. It therefore follows that the ideal
I^{\prime}
of
|A^{\prime}|
in
|S|
can be lifted to a pro-
\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MS}
-ideal, and the quotient
A^{\prime\prime}=S/I^{\prime}
equips
|A^{\prime}|
with a pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebra structure which is a small extension of
A
by
J
. By
Lemma

4.38
, the pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebra structures
A^{\prime\prime}
and
A^{\prime}
differ by an element of
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}^{\vee},J)
. Concretely, up to automorphisms, we can take this to mean in the Hodge case (resp. the twistor case) that there is an element
f\in W_{0}\Hom(\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}^{\vee},J)
(resp. a 1-cocycle) such that
A^{\prime\prime}
and
A^{\prime}
differ by the twist by
\operatorname{id}+f
as described after
Corollary

4.25
. Any such
f
can be lifted to an element
\tilde{f}\in W_{0}\Hom(\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}^{\vee},I)^{\wedge}
(resp. a 1-cocycle), and any lift
V^{\prime}
as above can be modified by
\operatorname{id}+\tilde{f}
. The claim (and its twistor version) then shows there is a pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebra structure
S^{\prime}
on
|S|
for which
q^{\prime}:S^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}
is a morphism of pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebras and which lifts
q
.
∎

Definition 4.41
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a deformation category over
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
equipped with an obstruction theory (with obstruction space
\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{D}}
). A
\mathrm{MS}
-enhancement consists of:

(1)

The structure of a
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebra on
\Lambda
.

(2)

A deformation category
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}
over
(\mbox{MS-}\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
, meaning a category cofibered in groupoids over the category
(\mbox{MS-}\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
for which
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0))
is equivalent to a point and such that the natural analog of the Rim–Schlessinger condition holds.

(3)

A commutative diagram of functors

(4.7.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mbox{MS-}\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Art}/|\Lambda|)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|-|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|-|}

where the vertical functors are the structural ones and the bottom functor is the forgetful functor.

(4)

An object
M
of
D^{b}(\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MS})
together with isomorphisms
\mathscr{H}^{1}(M)\cong\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathscr{H}^{2}(M)\cong\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{D}}
.

satisfying conditions (5) and (6) below:

(5)

The universal first order object in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\vee})
lifts to an object in
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0)\oplus\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\vee})
.

(6)

For
J\to A^{\prime}\to A
a small extension in
(\mbox{MS-}\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
together with an object
x\in\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}(A)
, there is an obstruction
\mathrm{obs}_{A/A^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{MS}}(x)\in\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}M[2])
, functorial with respect to morphisms of small extensions as above, such that:

(a)

\mathrm{obs}_{A/A^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{MS}}(x)=0
if and only if
x
lifts to an object
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MHS}}(A^{\prime})
.

(b)

In the natural short exact sequence

(4.7.2)

\displaystyle 0\to\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}})

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}M[2])

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{D}})\to 0,

the class
\mathrm{obs}_{A/A^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{MS}}(x)
maps to
\mathrm{obs}_{|A|/|A^{\prime}|}(|x|)
, via the inclusion
\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{D}})\to J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{D}}
.

(c)

The class
\mathrm{obs}_{A/A^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{MS}}(x)
is equivariant with respect to the natural action of
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda})
on the small extension
J\to A^{\prime}\to A
(by changing the
\mathbb{C}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{MS}
on
A^{\prime}
) and on the space
\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}M[2])
(via the natural map induced by the map on tangent spaces
\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}\to\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}}
).

We say the enhancement is
precise
if it furthermore satisfies:

(7)

There is an isomorphism
\mathscr{H}^{0}(M)\cong\mathfrak{a}_{\mathcal{D}}
.

(8)

Suppose given
J\to A^{\prime}\to A
a small extension in
(\mbox{MS-}\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
and
x\in\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MHS}}(A)
admitting a lift to
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}(A^{\prime})
. Then the set of isomorphism classes of the category of lifts
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}_{x}(A^{\prime})
admits a transitive action by
\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}M[1])
, functorially in
J
. The natural functor

\mathcal{D}_{x}^{\mathrm{MS}}(A^{\prime})\to\mathcal{D}_{x}(|A^{\prime}|)

on isomorphism classes is equivariant with respect to the two natural actions via the second map in the exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{a}_{\mathcal{D}})

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}M[1])

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}})\to 0

and the inclusion
\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}})\to J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}}
.

(9)

In the context of (8), the automorphisms of an object
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}_{x}(A^{\prime})
are identified with
\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{a}_{\mathcal{D}})
(functorially in
J
), and the natural map from the automorphisms of
x^{\prime}
to the automorphisms of
|x^{\prime}|\in\mathcal{D}_{|x|}(|A^{\prime}|)
is identified with the natural map
\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{a}_{\mathcal{D}})\to J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{a}_{\mathcal{D}}
.

We typically refer to the
\mathrm{MS}
-enhancement simply by
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}\to\mathcal{D}
. The following lemma follows immediately from the definitions and
Lemma

4.38
.

Lemma 4.42
.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}\to\mathcal{D}
be a
\mathrm{MS}
-enhancement. Let
(A,x)
be an element of
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}
,
J\to A^{\prime}\to A
a small extension in
(\mathrm{MS}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
, and
(|A^{\prime}|,y)
a lift of
(|A|,|x|)
in
\mathcal{D}
. Then:

(1)

The set of isomorphism classes of lifts of
|J|\to|A^{\prime}|\to|A|
to a small extension of
A
by
J
which admits a lift of
(A,x)
, if nonempty, is a torsor for

\ker\left(\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda})\to\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}})\right).

In particular, if
\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}}
, then there is at most one such lift.

If in addition
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}\to\mathcal{D}
is precise, then:

(2)

The set of isomorphism classes of common lifts of
(A,x)
and
(|A^{\prime}|,y)
to
A^{\prime}
, if nonempty, admits a transitive action by the inverse image of the stabilizer of
y
in
\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}})
in
\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}M[1])
. In particular, if
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathcal{D}}=0
, then there is at most one such lift.

Proposition 4.43
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a deformation category over
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
equipped with an obstruction theory and a
\mathrm{MS}
-enhancement
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}\to\mathcal{D}
. Then:

(1)

Any miniversal point
(\hat{\mathcal{O}},\hat{y})
of
\mathcal{D}
lifts to
(\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}},\hat{x})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}
. The lift
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}
is uniquely determined up to isomorphism.

(2)

If the enhancement is precise and
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathcal{D}}=0
, then
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}
is equivalent to the category
(\mathrm{MS}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Art}/\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}})
. In particular, any object
(A,x)
of
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{MS}}
is pulled back from
(\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}},\hat{x}^{\mathrm{MS}})
via a unique morphism
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}\to A
of pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebras.

Proof.

We begin with part (1). The uniqueness statement of (1) follows immediately from
Lemma

4.42
, so we need only show such a lift
(\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}},\hat{x})
exists. We prove that the miniversal element from Schlessinger’s construction described in the previous section lifts to each order. By condition (5) the first order object lifts, and we may take any such lift. Assume inductively there is a lift
(\mathcal{O}_{n}^{\mathrm{MS}},x_{n})
of the pair
(\mathcal{O}_{n},y_{n})
constructed in the
n
th step of the proof of
Theorem

4.33
. Assume also that we have equipped
\widehat{\Sym}^{*}_{|\Lambda|}|\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}^{\vee}|
with the structure of a pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebra
S
and that we have a quotient
S\to\mathcal{O}_{n}^{\mathrm{MS}}
of pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebras.

The resulting maximal small extension
A\to\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{MS}}_{n}
with ideal
J_{0}
from (
4.5.1
) is a small extension of
\Lambda
-MS-algebras, so the obstruction
\mathrm{obs}_{\mathcal{O}_{n}/|A|}(y_{n})\in J_{0}\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{D}}
is a Tate class, which we interpret as a morphism
J_{0}^{\vee}\to\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{D}}
of
\mathbb{C}
-MS. The image
N
of this map yields a sub-
\mathbb{C}
-MS
N^{\vee}\subset J_{0}
, and we define
B=A/N^{\vee}
. Let
J=J_{0}/N^{\vee}
be the ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{n}^{\mathrm{MS}}
in
B
. The resulting obstruction in
\mathcal{D}
vanishes, so by (
4.7.2
) the class
\mathrm{obs}^{\mathrm{MS}}_{\mathcal{O}_{n}^{\mathrm{MS}}/B}(x_{n})
is an element of
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}})
. Since the map on tangent spaces
\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{O}_{n}^{\mathrm{MS}}/\Lambda}\to\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}}
is an isomorphism, it follows that after changing the mixed structure on
B
(as a small extension in
(\mathrm{MS}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
without changing the underlying algebra structure) by an element of
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{O}_{n}^{\mathrm{MS}}/\Lambda})
, the obstruction vanishes and there is a lift
x_{n+1}
of
x_{n}
. We let
\mathcal{O}_{n+1}^{\mathrm{MS}}
be
B
with this new mixed structure and choose any such lift
x_{n+1}
, as we may take any lift of
y_{n}
to
\mathcal{O}_{n+1}
in Schlessinger’s construction. Finally, we must show that the pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebra structure on
S
can be changed to
S^{\prime}
so that the quotient
S^{\prime}\to\mathcal{O}_{n+1}^{\mathrm{MS}}
is a morphism of pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebras, and this is
Proposition

4.39
.

We turn now to the proof of part (2). It suffices to show that for a small extension
J\to A^{\prime}\to A
in
(\mathrm{MS}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
and an object
(A^{\prime},x^{\prime})
lifting
(A,x)
for which
x
is pulled back via
f:\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}\to A
from
\hat{x}^{\mathrm{MS}}
, there is a unique lift
f^{\prime}:\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}\to A^{\prime}
by which
x^{\prime}
is pulled back. The uniqueness follows from the representability of
\mathcal{D}
. Moreover, since
\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathbf{T}(0),J\otimes_{\mathbf{T}(0)}\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{D}})
acts simply transitively on the lifts of
(A,x)
to
A^{\prime}
, it is enough to construct a morphism
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}\to A^{\prime}
.

Let
S
be a smooth pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebra with a quotient
S\to\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}
of pro-
\Lambda
-MS-algebras that is an isomorphism on tangent spaces. We have the solid diagram with exact rows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

The lift
g
exists forgetting the mixed structures, as therefore does the left vertical map
h
. Note that
h
is independent of the choice of
g
. In particular, since
g
can be chosen to be compatible with either splitting, it follows that
I\to J
is a morphism of pro-MS-ideals. Taking the quotient of
S
by the kernel, we obtain a morphism
S\to A^{\prime\prime}
for some choice
A^{\prime\prime}
of
\Lambda
-MS-algebra structure on
|A^{\prime}|
. Moreover, since
S\to A
factors through a finite level quotient
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}_{n}\to A
, and since
\mathrm{obs}_{|A|/|A^{\prime}|}(x)=0
, it follows as in the usual proof of Schlessinger’s theorem (see
[
118
,
Tag 06IX
]
) by considering
S\to A^{\prime}\times_{A}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{n}^{\mathrm{MS}}
that there is a morphism
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}_{n+1}\to A^{\prime\prime}
lifting it. In particular, there is a lift
f^{\prime\prime}:\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}\to A^{\prime\prime}
of
f
.

The
\Lambda
-MS-algebra structure on
A^{\prime}
differs from that of
A^{\prime\prime}
by twisting by an element
\xi\in\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}^{\vee},J)
. Let
E
be the image of
\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}\to\mathfrak{t}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}/\Lambda}
and consider the short exact sequence

0\to E\to\mathfrak{t}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}/\Lambda}\to E^{\prime}\to 0

which leads to the exact sequence

\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(E^{\prime\vee},J)\to\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(E^{\vee},J)\to\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathfrak{t}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}/\Lambda}^{\vee},J).

Since both
A^{\prime}
and
A^{\prime\prime}
admit lifts of
x
, it follows that the image of
\xi
in
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(\mathfrak{t}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}/\Lambda}^{\vee},J)
vanishes. Thus, the image in
\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{MS}}(E^{\vee},J)
may be lifted to
c\in\Hom_{\mathrm{MS}}(E^{\prime\vee},J)
. In the Hodge case (the twistor case is similar), let
\tilde{c}
be a lift of
c
to
F^{0}W_{0}\Hom(\mathfrak{t}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}/\Lambda}^{\vee},J)
and
\tilde{c}^{\prime}
a lift to
F^{\prime 0}W_{0}\Hom(\mathfrak{t}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}/\Lambda}^{\vee},J)
. We can then think of
\xi
as a lift
b\in W_{0}\Hom(\mathfrak{t}_{A/\Lambda}^{\vee},J)/F^{0}W_{0}+F^{\prime 0}W_{0}
of
\tilde{c}-\tilde{c}^{\prime}\in W_{0}\Hom(E^{\prime\vee},J)/F^{0}W_{0}+F^{\prime 0}W_{0}
. It follows that by modifying the lift to
|f^{\prime\prime}|+c:|\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}|\to|A^{\prime\prime}|
, we obtain the required morphism
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}\to A^{\prime}
. Indeed,
|f^{\prime\prime}|+c
respects the weight filtration and
F^{\bullet}
, while

(|f^{\prime\prime}|+c)F^{\prime\bullet}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}=(|f^{\prime\prime}|+\tilde{c}^{\prime})(1+\tilde{c}-\tilde{c}^{\prime})F^{\prime\bullet}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{MS}}=(1+b)(|f^{\prime\prime}|+\tilde{c}^{\prime})F^{\prime\bullet}A^{\prime\prime}=(1+b)F^{\prime\bullet}A^{\prime\prime}.

∎

4.8.
Deformation theory of local systems

Let
X
be a topological space homeomorphic to a finite CW complex and
(\mathbb{C}_{X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Mod})
be the abelian category of contructible
\mathbb{C}_{X}
-modules on
X
. The deformation theory of the Betti stack
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
is well-known; we first briefly summarize the straightforward approach in terms of group cocycles.

Remark 4.44
.

Given a rank
r
local system
V_{0}\in\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
with monodromy representation
\rho_{V_{0},x}:\pi_{1}:=\pi_{1}(X,x)\to\GL(V_{0,x})
for a choice of basepoint
x\in X
and a small extension
J\to A^{\prime}\to A
of artinian
\mathbb{C}
-algebras, a deformation
V
of
V_{0}
over
A
will have monodromy representation
\rho_{V,x}:\pi_{1}\to\GL(V_{x})
. Identifying
V_{x}\cong A\otimes V_{0,x}
, we write
\rho_{V,x}=(1+f)\rho_{V_{0},x}
for
f\in\Hom_{\mathrm{Sets}}(\pi_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{A}\otimes\End(V_{0,x}))
. Then choosing an arbitrary lift
f^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes\End(V_{0,x})
, we have

(1+f^{\prime}(\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}))\rho_{V_{0},x}(\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2})=(1+f^{\prime}(\gamma_{1}))\rho_{V_{0},x}(\gamma_{1})(1+f^{\prime}(\gamma_{2}))\rho_{V_{0},x}(\gamma_{2})+o(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})

for a
2
-cocycle
o
with values in
J\otimes\End(V_{0,x})
, and the corresponding element of
J\otimes H^{2}(\pi_{1},\End(V_{0,x}))
is the obstruction for lifting both
V
and
\rho_{V,x}
to
A^{\prime}
-points of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
and
R_{B}(X,x,r)
. Note that since we have a natural injection
H^{2}(\pi_{1},\End(V_{0,x}))\to\Ext^{2}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(V_{0},V_{0})
, we may equally well use the image of
o
as an obstruction.

The first order deformations of
V_{0}
(resp.
\rho_{V_{0},x}
) are naturally identified with
H^{1}(\pi_{1},\End(V_{0,x}))=\Ext^{1}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(V_{0},V_{0})
(resp. the group
1
-cocycles
Z^{1}(\pi_{1},\End(V_{0,x}))=H^{1}((X,x),\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.99997pt\operatorname{nd}(V_{0}))
). The first order obstruction map is also easily computed to be the self-commutator

[\;,\;]:H^{1}(\pi_{1},\End(V_{0,x}))\to H^{2}(\pi_{1},\End(V_{0,x})).

We will instead need a treatment of the deformation theory from which the existence of Hodge enhancements will easily follow in the next section. Essentially this means we need to construct the obstruction classes functorially in terms of functors that lift to the category of mixed Hodge/twistor modules.

Let
J\to A^{\prime}\to A
be a small extension of artinian local
\mathbb{C}
-algebras. Let
V
be a free
A
-local system and set
V_{0}=V/\mathfrak{m}_{A}V
. By tensoring the sequence of
A
-modules

0\to J\to\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\to\mathfrak{m}_{A}\to 0

with
V
, we obtain an exact sequence of
A
-local systems

(4.8.1)

0\to J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0}\to\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}V\to\mathfrak{m}_{A}\otimes_{A}V\to 0.

By tensoring the sequence of
A
-modules

0\to\mathfrak{m}_{A}\to A\to\mathbb{C}\to 0

we also have an exact sequence of
A
-local systems

(4.8.2)

0\to\mathfrak{m}_{A}\otimes_{A}V\to V\xrightarrow{\pi}V_{0}\to 0

and therefore an exact sequence of
\mathbb{C}
-vector spaces

(4.8.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(V_{0},J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(V,J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(\mathfrak{m}_{A}\otimes_{A}V,J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ext^{1}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(V_{0},J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ext^{1}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(V,J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ext^{1}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(\mathfrak{m}_{A}\otimes_{A}V,J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ext^{2}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(V_{0},J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{2}}

Let
\eta_{V,A/A^{\prime}}\in\Ext^{1}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(\mathfrak{m}_{A}\otimes_{A}V,J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0})
be the class of the extension (
4.8.1
).

Lemma 4.45
.

The category of commutative diagrams of
\mathbb{C}
-local systems with exact rows and columns

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{A}\otimes_{A}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

where all maps but
\alpha,\beta,\mu
are the canonical ones and whose morphisms are morphisms of diagrams of
\mathbb{C}
-local systems which are the identity on all but the
V^{\prime}
factor is equivalent to the category of lifts
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)_{V}(A^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Given such a diagram,
\beta
induces a morphism
\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}V
, whose composition with the morphism
\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}V\xrightarrow{\mu}V^{\prime}
defines the structure of a
A^{\prime}
-local system on
V^{\prime}
for which
V^{\prime}\to V
is a surjective morphism of
A^{\prime}
-local systems with kernel
JV^{\prime}
. Thus,
V^{\prime}
is a free
A^{\prime}
-local system, and the adjoint
A\otimes_{A^{\prime}}V^{\prime}\to V
is an isomorphism. A morphism of diagrams in the above sense is clearly equivalent to a morphism of
A^{\prime}
-local systems which commutes with the projection to
V
, and any free
A^{\prime}
-local system
V^{\prime}
with an isomorphism
A\otimes_{A^{\prime}}V^{\prime}\to V
comes from such a diagram.
∎

Corollary 4.46
.

(1)

V
can be lifted to
A^{\prime}
if and only if
\partial_{2}\eta_{V,A/A^{\prime}}=0\in J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\Ext^{2}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(V_{0},V_{0})
.

(2)

The group
J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\Ext^{1}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(V_{0},V_{0})
acts transitively on the set of isomorphism classes of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)_{V}(A^{\prime})
, provided it is nonempty.

(3)

T_{V_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
is naturally identified with
\Ext^{1}_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(V_{0},V_{0})
.

(4)

The automorphisms of
V^{\prime}\to V
in
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)_{V}(A^{\prime})
are given by
\operatorname{id}+\alpha\sigma\pi
for
\sigma\in J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{X}}(V_{0},V_{0})
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

4.45
and the long exact sequence (
4.8.3
).
∎

We now give a slightly more flexible version of
Lemma

4.45
. Consider the natural two-term complex

N=[\overset{-1}{\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}V}\to\overset{0}{V}]

where the map is induced by the composition
\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\to\mathfrak{m}_{A}\to A
. We have a natural diagram whose rows and columns are distinguished triangles

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0}[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0}[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0}[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}V[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{A}\otimes_{A}V[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0}[2].}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V[1]}

Lemma 4.47
.

There is a lift
V^{\prime}\to V
if and only if
N
splits, and isomorphism classes in
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)_{V}(A^{\prime})
are naturally identified with splittings.

Proof.

A lift gives a splitting via the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}

and isomorphic lifts give the same splitting (as a morphism in the derived category).

A splitting
N\to J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0}[1]
gives a lift by taking cones, from the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}V[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}V[1].}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0}[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{0}[1]}

While the cone
V^{\prime}
is not unique, another choice of cone fits into an isomorphic diagram (via an isomorphism of diagrams which is the identity at every other node), and therefore gives a well-defined isomorphism class of lift. These two maps are easily seen to be mutually inverse.
∎

We now treat the absolute and relative deformation theory of some related stacks, including the framed moduli space, the pullback morphism, and the fixed local monodromy leaves, as this will give us the desired functoriality properties of the Hodge/twistor structures.

4.8.1.
Pullback

For a continuous map
f:X\to Y
of topological space (with the above finiteness assumptions), consider the pullback
f^{*}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
. Let
V_{0}\in\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)(\mathbb{C})
and let
(\Lambda,\hat{U})
be a
\Lambda
-local system for
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
with closed point
f^{*}V_{0}
. Let
\mathcal{D}
be the category cofibered in groupoids over
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
whose objects over a local artinian
\Lambda
-algebra
A
are pairs
(V,b)
where
V\in\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)(A)
and
b:\hat{U}\to f^{*}V
is a morphism of
\Lambda
-local systems which is an isomorphism over
A
. A morphism
(V_{1},b_{1})\to(V_{2},b_{2})
lying over a morphism
\alpha:A_{1}\to A_{2}
of
(\mathrm{Art}/\Lambda)
consists of a morphism
g:V_{1}\to V_{2}
of
A_{1}
-local systems which is an isomorphism over
A_{2}
and which form a commutative diagrams

(4.8.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\otimes_{\Lambda}\hat{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}V_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}\otimes_{\Lambda}\hat{U}}
