.

Then there exists a
\rho
-equivariant locally Lipschitz continuous map
u:\tilde{M}\to\Delta
of least (finite) energy. In particular,
u
is harmonic.

Proof.

When
\Delta
is a complete simply connected manifold with nonpositive sectional curvature, this is
[
28
, Theorem 2.2]
. When
\Delta
is a locally compact Euclidean Bruhat-Tits building, this is
[
58
, Theorem 7.1]
. In general, this is the conjonction of Theorem 2.1.3, Remark 2.1.5 and Theorem 2.2.1 in
[
72
]
.
∎

6.4.
Averaging harmonic maps

Proposition 6.9

(
[
71
, Lemma 2.5.1]
)
.

Let
(\Delta,d)
be a NPC complete metric space. Let
S\subset\Delta
be a finite subset. Then, there exists a unique point
c(S)
such that the number
\sum_{P\in S}d(c(S),P)^{2}
is minimal.

The point
c(S)
is called the center of mass of the finite set
S
. It belongs to the closed convex hull of
S

[
71
, Proposition 2.5.4]
.

Proposition 6.10

(
[
71
, Proposition 2.5.2]
)
.

Let
(\Delta,d)
be a NPC complete metric space. Let
n
be a positive integer. Let
P,Q\colon\{1,\ldots,n\}\to\Delta
be two functions. Let
c_{P}
and
c_{Q}
be the centers of mass of the sets
\{P(1),\ldots,P(n)\}
and
\{Q(1),\ldots,Q(n)\}
respectively. Then

d(c_{P},c_{Q})^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}d(P(i),Q(i))^{2}.

Proposition 6.11
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete Riemannian manifold,
(\Delta,d)
be a proper NPC space and
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(M)\to\mathrm{Isom}(\Delta)
a group homomorphism.
Let
u_{i}\colon\tilde{M}\to\Delta,1\leq i\leq n
, be
\rho
-equivariant locally Lipschitz continuous maps of finite energy. Then the map
u\colon x\mapsto c(\{u_{i}(x)\})
is a
\rho
-equivariant locally Lipschitz continuous map of finite energy. Moreover, if the
u_{i}
’s have least finite energy, then
u
has least finite energy.

Proof.

Since the maps
u_{i}
are locally Lipschitz continuous, the map
u
is locally Lipschitz continuous thanks to Proposition
6.10
. Thanks to Equation
2.5.iii,p.641
in
[
71
]
, the energy
E(u)
of the map
u
is finite and satisfies
E(u)\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E(u_{i})
.
∎

Proposition 6.12
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete Riemannian manifold,
(\Delta,d)
be a proper NPC space and
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(M)\to\mathrm{Isom}(\Delta)
a group homomorphism. Let
M^{\prime}\to M
be a finite Galois covering space and
\rho^{\prime}\colon\pi_{1}(M^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Isom}(\Delta)
be the induced homomorphism. If there exists a
\rho^{\prime}
-equivariant locally Lipschitz continuous map
\tilde{M}\to\Delta
of finite energy, then there exists a
\rho
-equivariant locally Lipschitz continuous map
\tilde{M}\to\Delta
of finite energy.

Proof.

Let
u\colon\tilde{M^{\prime}}\to\Delta
be a
\rho^{\prime}
-equivariant locally Lipschitz continuous map of finite energy. The center of mass of its Galois conjugates is a
\rho^{\prime}
-equivariant locally Lipschitz continuous map of finite energy by Proposition
6.11
. Since it is Galois invariant, it descends to a
\rho
-equivariant locally Lipschitz continuous map
\tilde{M}\to\Delta
of finite energy.
∎

6.5.
Constructing subharmonic functions

Proposition 6.13

(see
[
41
, Lemma 10.2]
)
.

Let
(\Delta,d)
be a NPC complete metric space. Let
\phi\colon\Delta\to\mathbb{R}
be a function which is convex when restricted to geodesics. If the map
u\colon M\to\Delta
is harmonic, then the function
\phi\circ u
is subharmonic.

Proposition 6.14
.

Let
(\Delta,d)
be a NPC complete metric space. Let
u,u^{\prime}\colon M\to\Delta
be two harmonic maps. Then the function
x\mapsto d(u(x),u^{\prime}(x))
is subharmonic.

Proof.

This is a consequence of the previous Proposition, since the map
(u,u^{\prime})\colon M\to\Delta\times\Delta
is harmonic and the function
(y,y^{\prime})\mapsto d(y,y^{\prime})
is convex, the space
\Delta
being NPC.
∎

6.6.
Limit of sequence of harmonic maps

Proposition 6.15
.

Let
(\Omega,g)
be a Lipschitz Riemannian domain and let
(\Delta,d)
be a NPC complete metric space. Let
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k}
be a sequence of harmonic maps in
W^{1,2}(\Omega,\Delta)
. Assume that the sequence of energy
\{E(\phi_{k})\}_{k}
is bounded. Assume also that the set
\{\phi_{k}(x)|k\geq 0\}
is relatively compact in
\Omega
for some
x\in\Omega
. Then
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k}
has a subsequence that converges to a harmonic map uniformly on every compact subset.

Proof.

A harmonic map is locally Lipschitz continuous. Moreover, since the
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k}
have uniformly bounded energy, the local Lipschitz constants are uniformly bounded thanks to
Theorem

6.7
. It follows from Arzelà-Ascoli theorem that a subsequence of
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k}
converges uniformly on every compact subset of
\Omega
. The limit
\phi
belongs to
W^{1,2}(\Omega,\Delta)
thanks to Theorem
6.3
. Let
u\in W^{1,2}(\Omega,\Delta)
be the unique map which minimizes the energy among all maps in
W^{1,2}(\Omega,\Delta)
with the same trace as
\phi
, see Theorem
6.5
. The function
d_{\Delta}(u,\phi)
is the uniform limit of (a subsequence of) the functions
d_{\Delta}(u,\phi_{k})
. Since the latter are subharmonic thanks to Proposition
6.14
,
d_{\Delta}(u,\phi)
is subharmonic. But it is zero on the boundary of
\Omega
, hence it is zero everywhere by the maximum principle.
∎

Corollary 6.16
.

Let
(\Omega,g)
be a Lipschitz Riemannian domain and let
(\Delta,d)
be a NPC complete metric space. Let
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k}
be a converging sequence of harmonic maps in
W^{1,2}(\Omega,\Delta)
. Then the limit is harmonic.

Proof.

Since the sequence converges in
W^{1,2}(\Omega,\Delta)
, the energy are uniformly bounded, so that one can apply the preceding Proposition.
∎

6.7.
Poincaré-type metric

Definition 6.17
.

Let
D
be a normal crossing divisor in a complex manifold
X
of dimension
n
. An admissible polydisk is an open subset
U\subset X
equipped with a biholomorphism
U\simeq\mathbb{D}^{n}
such that
D\cap U
corresponds to the union of some of the
\{z_{i}=0\}
.

We equip
\mathbb{D}
and
\mathbb{D}^{\ast}
with their Poincaré metrics
\omega_{P}
that are respectively given by

\frac{idz\wedge d\bar{z}}{\left(1-|z|^{2}\right)^{2}}\text{ and }\frac{idz\wedge d\bar{z}}{|z|^{2}\left(-\log|z|^{2}\right)^{2}}.

We denote also by
\omega_{P}
the induced metric on
\mathbb{D}^{l}\times(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{m}
for every
l,m\geq 0
.

Definition 6.18
.

Let
D
be a normal crossing divisor in a complex manifold
X
of dimension
n
. A metric
\omega
on
X\setminus D
is of Poincaré type if for every admissible polydisk
\mathbb{D}^{n}\subset X
there exists
C>0
such that the restriction of
\omega
to
\mathbb{D}^{n}\setminus D
satisfies
C^{-1}\omega_{P}\leq\omega\leq C\omega_{P}
in the neighborhood of
0
. (Here
\omega_{P}
denotes the Poincaré metric on
\mathbb{D}^{n}\setminus D
.)

As is well-known, if
D
is a normal crossing divisor in a compact Kähler manifold
\bar{X}
, then
\bar{X}\setminus D
admits a complete Kähler metric of Poincaré type.

6.8.
Euclidean buildings

Let
K
be a non-archimedean local field. Let
\mathbf{G}
be a connected, reductive group over
K
. We denote by
\Delta(\mathbf{G},K)
the extended Bruhat-Tits building of
\mathbf{G}
, as introduced in
[
15
,
16
]
. See also
[
73
]
. It is a (poly-)simplicial complex equipped with an action of
\mathbf{G}(K)
. One can define a metric
d
on
\Delta(\mathbf{G},K)
such that
(\Delta(\mathbf{G},K),d)
is a NPC complete metric space and
\mathbf{G}(K)
acts by isometries.

We will need the following functoriality results from
[
73
]
.

Proposition 6.19
.

Let
f\colon\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{H}
be a homomorphism of connected, reductive
K
-groups. Then there exists a map
f_{\ast}:\Delta(\mathbf{G},K)\to\Delta(\mathbf{H},K)
which is
\mathbf{G}(K)
-equivariant and, in case
f
is injective, an isometrical inclusion.

Proposition 6.20

(
[
73
, Proposition 2.1.6]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{G}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{G}_{n}
be connected, reductive
K
-groups. Then there is a canonical bijection,

\Delta(\mathbf{G}_{1},K)\times\ldots\times\Delta(\mathbf{G}_{n},K)\to\Delta(\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathbf{G}_{n},K)

which is
\mathbf{G}_{1}(K)\times\ldots\times\mathbf{G}_{n}(K)=(\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathbf{G}_{n})(K)
-equivariant. After a
suitable normalization of the metrics on
\Delta(\mathbf{G}_{i},K)
, for
i=1,\ldots,n
, this map becomes isometrical.

Proposition 6.21

(
[
73
, Theorem 2.2.1]
)
.

Let
i\colon\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{H}
be an inclusion of connected, reductive
K
-groups. Then, there exists an inclusion
f_{\ast}:\Delta(\mathbf{G},K)\to\Delta(\mathbf{H},K)
which is
\mathbf{G}(K)
-equivariant and isometrical (after a suitable normalization of the metric on
\Delta(\mathbf{G},K)
).

Proposition 6.22
.

Let
\pi\colon\mathbf{G}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{G}
be a central isogeny of connected, reductive
K
-groups. Then there is a canonical bijection
\Delta(\mathbf{G}^{\prime},K)\to\Delta(\mathbf{G},K)
which is compatible with the
\mathbf{G}^{\prime}(K)
-action on the left hand side and the
\mathbf{G}(K)
-action on the right hand side. After a suitable normalization of the metric on
\Delta(\mathbf{G}^{\prime},K)
, this map becomes isometrical.

Proof.

See
[
16
, 4.2.18]
or
[
73
, Proposition 2.1.7]
.
∎

6.9.
Harmonic maps towards Euclidean buildings

Definition 6.23
.

Let
(M,g)
be a Riemannian manifold. Let
\Delta
be a locally compact Euclidean building. Let
u\colon M\to\Delta
be a map. Its regular locus is by definition the open subset
\mathrm{Reg}(u)\subset M
consisting of points
x\in M
admitting a neighborhood
U\subset M
such that
u(U)\subset\Delta
is contained in a single apartment of
\Delta
. The complementary
\mathrm{Sing}(u):=M\setminus\mathrm{Reg}(u)
in
M
is called the singular locus of
u
.

Theorem 6.24

(
[
58
, Theorem 6.4]
)
.

Let
M
be a Riemannian manifold. Let
\Delta
be a locally compact Euclidean building. Let
u\colon M\to\Delta
be a harmonic map. Then the singular locus of
u
has Hausdorff codimension at least
2
.

Theorem 6.25
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a projective log smooth variety and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
K
be a nonarchimedean local field. Let
\mathbf{G}
be a connected reductive group over
K
. Let
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\mathbf{G}(K)
be a representation with Zariski dense image and quasiunipotent local monodromy. Let
\Delta(\mathbf{G},K)
be the (extended) Euclidean Bruhat-Tits building associated to
\mathbf{G}(K)
. Fix a Poincaré-type complete Kähler metric on
X
. Then there exists a
\rho
-equivariant harmonic map
\tilde{X}\to\Delta(\mathbf{G},K)
of finite energy.

When
\mathbf{G}
is simple, a proof of this result is given in
[
61
]
. For
\mathbf{G}=\mathrm{SL}_{2}
, a detailed proof appears in
[
29
]
. The case where
\mathbf{G}
is semisimple has been recently addressed in
[
31
,
14
]
, in the more general setting of harmonic maps with possibly infinite energy.

Proof.

When
\mathbf{G}
is semisimple, the existence of such a map follows from
[
14
]
. When
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{T}
is a torus, the building
\Delta(\mathbf{T},K)=\left(X_{K}^{\ast}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}\right)^{\ast}
is a finite-dimensional real vector space on which
\mathbf{T}(K)
acts via the translation action of the canonical lattice. Since the representation
\rho
has quasiunipotent local monodromy, it factorizes through the surjection
\pi_{1}(X)\to\pi_{1}(\bar{X})
. The existence of a
\rho
-equivariant harmonic map
\widetilde{\bar{X}}\to\Delta(\mathbf{T},K)
follows from classical Hodge theory for
H^{1}(\bar{X},\mathbb{Z})
. The map
\widetilde{\bar{X}}\to\Delta(\mathbf{T},K)
has finite energy since
\bar{X}
is compact, and its pull-back to
\tilde{X}
is therefore a
\rho
-equivariant harmonic map of finite energy.
For a general connected reductive group
\mathbf{G}
, let
Z(\mathbf{G})
and
\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{G})
denote the center and the derived subgroup of
\mathbf{G}
respectively. Since
\mathbf{G}
is reductive,
\mathbf{T}:=\mathbf{G}/\penalty\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{G})
is a torus,
\mathbf{G}^{ad}:=\mathbf{G}/\penalty Z(\mathbf{G})
is a semisimple group and the canonical morphism
\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{T}\times\mathbf{G}^{ad}
is a central isogeny. Thanks to
Proposition

6.20
and
Proposition

6.22
, this induces an isometrical bijection
\Delta(\mathbf{G},K)=\Delta(\mathbf{T},K)\times\Delta(\mathbf{G}^{ad},K)
which is compatible with respect to the
\mathbf{G}(K)
-action on the left hand side and the
\mathbf{T}(K)\times\mathbf{G}^{ad}(K)
-action on the right hand side.
Let
u_{1}\colon\tilde{X}\to\Delta(\mathbf{T},K)
(resp.
u_{2}\colon\tilde{X}\to\Delta(\mathbf{G}^{ad},K)
) be a harmonic map of finite energy which is
equivariant with respect to the induced representation
\pi_{1}(X)\to\mathbf{T}(K)
(resp.
\pi_{1}(X)\to\mathbf{G}^{ad}(K)
). Then
(u_{1},u_{2})\colon\tilde{X}\to\Delta(\mathbf{G},K)=\Delta(\mathbf{T},K)\times\Delta(\mathbf{G}^{ad},K)
is a

\rho
-equivariant harmonic map of finite energy.
∎

6.10.
Pluriharmonic maps

Definition 6.26
.

Let
M
be a complex analytic space and
(\Delta,d)
be a complete metric space. A map
u\colon M\to\Delta
is called pluriharmonic if for any Kähler manifold
(N,h)
equipped with a holomorphic map
f\colon N\to M
the composite map
u\circ f\colon N\to\Delta
is harmonic.

In particular, a pluriharmonic map
M\to\Delta
is harmonic for any choice of a Kähler metric on
M
.

Theorem 6.27
.

In the setting of Theorem
6.25
, every equivariant harmonic map
\tilde{X}\to\Delta
of finite energy is pluriharmonic.

Proof.

This is due to Gromov-Schoen
[
58
, Theorem 7.3]
. Note however that the definition of pluriharmonicity in
[
58
]
is a priori weaker than our Definition
6.26
, but turns out to be equivalent by
[
44
, Proposition 1.3.6]
or Theorem
6.29
below.
∎

Proposition 6.28

(See
[
44
, Proposition 1.3.3]
)
.

Let
M
be a complex manifold. Let
\Delta
be a locally compact Euclidean building. Let
u\colon M\to\Delta
be a pluriharmonic map. Then the singular locus of
u
is contained in a (strict) complex analytic subset of
M
.

Theorem 6.29
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complex manifold. Let
(\Delta,d)
be a NPC complete metric space. Let
Z\subset M
be a closed pluripolar subset. Let
u\colon M\to\Delta
be a locally Lipschitz continuous map. Then
u\colon M\to\Delta
is pluriharmonic as soon as it is pluriharmonic in restriction to
M\setminus Z
.

Proof.

Let
(N,h)
be a Kähler manifold equipped with a holomorphic map
f\colon N\to M
, and let us prove that the composite map
u\circ f\colon N\to\Delta
is harmonic. Since the map
u\circ f\colon N\to\Delta
is locally Lipschitz continuous, it is sufficient to check that every point in
N
admits an open neighborhood with Lipschitz boundary such that every locally Lipschitz continuous map
v\colon N\to\Delta
which agree with
u
outside of this neighborhood has no less energy. Therefore one can assume that
N
is a ball
\mathbb{B}^{r}
. Moreover, up to shrinking
\mathbb{B}^{r}
, one can assume that the holomorphic map
f\colon\mathbb{B}^{r}\to M
is a uniform limit of holomorphic maps
g_{k}\colon\mathbb{B}^{r}\to M
such that
g_{k}^{-1}(Z)\subsetneq\mathbb{B}^{r}
is a closed pluripolar subset. Since
u
is locally Lipschitz continuous, the maps
u\circ g_{k}
converges to
u\circ f
in
W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\Delta)
. Since the limit in
W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\Delta)
of a sequence of harmonic maps is harmonic (Corollary
6.16
), it is sufficient to treat the case where
f^{-1}(Z)\subsetneq\mathbb{B}^{r}
is a closed polar subset.

Let
h\colon\mathbb{B}^{r}\to\Delta
be the unique harmonic map that coincides with
u\circ f\colon\mathbb{B}^{r}\to\Delta
on
\partial\mathbb{B}^{r}
, see Theorem
6.5
. Then the function
z\mapsto d(h(z),u\circ f(z))
is locally Lipschitz continuous and its restriction to
\mathbb{B}^{r}\setminus f^{-1}(Z)
is subharmonic by Proposition
6.14
.

Therefore it is subharmonic on
\mathbb{B}^{r}
thanks to Brelot extension theorem
[
60
, Theorem 5.18]
. Since it is zero on
\partial\mathbb{B}^{r}
, it is zero everywhere by the maximum principle. Therefore, we have proved that
u\circ f\colon\mathbb{B}^{r}\to\Delta
is harmonic, and this finishes the proof.
∎

7.
The Betti–Dolbeault correspondence and the
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-action

Work of Corlette
[
27
]
and Mochizuki
[
84
, Part 5]
shows that every semisimple complex local system admits an essentially unique pluriharmonic metric. Similarly, Simpson
[
112
]
and Mochizuki
[
81
]
characterize the existence of pluriharmonic metrics on logarithmic Higgs bundles. This yields a correspondence between semisimple complex local systems and certain polystable logarithmic Higgs bundles. Strictly speaking parabolic structures are needed to make this precise, but in the case of unipotent local monodromy on the Betti side (and nilpotent residues of the Higgs field on the Dolbeault side) there is a canonical such choice. In this section, we show the bijection in this special case is a homeomorphism (for curves), and use it to transport the natural
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-action on the Dolbeault side to the unipotent local monodromy locus on the Betti side. This together with the density result of
Theorem

5.23
will allow us to produce
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-fixed points (and in particular variations of Hodge structures) in every irreducible component of a
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-stable Zariski closed
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-bialgebraic subset of
M_{B}(X)
.

7.1.
Moduli spaces of logarithmic Higgs bundles

Let
f\colon\bar{X}\to S
be a smooth projective morphism to a scheme of finite type over
\mathbb{C}
, and
D\subset\bar{X}
a relative normal crossing divisor. Let
\Omega_{\bar{X}/S}(\log D)
denote the sheaf of logarithmic differentials, and
T_{\bar{X}/S}(-\log D)\subset T_{\bar{X}/S}
be the subsheaf of the tangent sheaf dual to
\Omega_{\bar{X}/S}(\log D)
.

Definition 7.1
.

A logarithmic Higgs sheaf on
(\bar{X},D)
over
S
is a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}
-module
E
on
\bar{X}
together with a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}
-modules
\theta\colon E\to\Omega_{\bar{X}/S}(\log D)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}}E
such that
\theta\wedge\theta=0
. Equivalently, it is a (left)
\Sym T_{{\bar{X}}/S}(-\log D)
-module which is coherent as a
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}
-module. A Higgs bundle is a Higgs sheaf
(E,\theta)
such that
E
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}
-module.

Fix
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}(1)
a relatively very ample line bundle on
{\bar{X}}
. A logarithmic Higgs sheaf
(E,\theta)
on
({\bar{X}},D)
over
S
is Gieseker semistable if the underlying
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}
-module is flat over
S
, and if for every geometric point
s
of
S
the restriction of
E
to the fibre
{\bar{X}}_{s}
is pure and Gieseker semistable as a log-Higgs sheaf on
({\bar{X}}_{s},D_{s})
(the Hilbert polynomial, the rank, and the slope of
E_{s}
are defined to be those of the underlying sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{{\bar{X}}_{s}}
-module.) One defines similarly Gieseker stability, slope stability and slope semistability. It follows from
[
75
, Theorem 4.3]
that for logarithmic Higgs sheaves with vanishing Chern classes, Gieseker-semistability (resp. Gieseker-stability) is equivalent to slope-semistability (resp. slope-stability).
(Since
{\bar{X}}_{s}
is smooth, a coherent sheaf of pure dimension
d=\dim({\bar{X}}_{s})
is the same thing as a torsion-free coherent sheaf.)

Theorem 7.2
.

Let
f\colon{\bar{X}}\to S
be a smooth projective morphism to a scheme of finite type over
\mathbb{C}
, and
D\subset{\bar{X}}
a relative normal crossing divisor. Fix
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}(1)
a relatively very ample line bundle on
{\bar{X}}
, a section
\xi\colon S\to{\bar{X}}
and a positive integer
r
. Then,

(1)

The functor which associates to any
S
-scheme
T
the set of isomorphism classes
of pairs
((E,\theta),\beta)
, where
(E,\theta)
is a rank
r
Gieseker-semistable logarithmic Higgs bundle on
({\bar{X}}_{T},D_{T})
over
T
, such that the Chern classes
c_{i}(E_{t})
vanish in
H^{2i}({\bar{X}}_{t},\mathbb{C})
for all closed points
t\in T
, and
\beta\colon\xi^{\ast}E\simeq\mathcal{O}_{T}^{r}
is a framing, is representable by a quasiprojective complex algebraic variety
R_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,\xi,r)
.

(2)

Changing the framing
\beta
yields an action of the group
\mathbf{GL}_{r}
on
R_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,\xi,r)
, and every point is semistable for this action (with respect to the an appropriate linearized bundle obtained from
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}(1)
). The GIT quotient is a good quotient
M_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r):=\mathbf{GL}_{r}\backslash\hskip-1.99997pt\backslash R_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,\xi,r)
that universally corepresents the functor which to an
S
-scheme
T
associates the set of isomorphism classes of Gieseker semistable logarithmic Higgs bundles
(E,\theta)
on
{\bar{X}}_{T}
over
T
of Hilbert polynomial
rP_{0}
, such that the Chern classes
c_{i}(E_{t})
vanish in
H^{2i}({\bar{X}}_{t},\mathbb{C})
for all closed points
t\in T
.

(3)

The closed orbits in
R_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)
correspond to direct sums of stable logarithmic Higgs bundles with vanishing rational Chern classes.

Proof.

Let
P_{0}
denote the Hilbert polynomial of
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}
. By applying
[
114
, Theorem 4.10]
to the sheaf of rings of differential operators
\Lambda=\Sym T_{{\bar{X}}/S}(-\log D)
and the Hilbert polynomial
P=rP_{0}
, it follows that the functor which associates to any
S
-scheme
T
the set of isomorphism classes of pairs
((E,\theta),\beta)
, where
(E,\theta)
is a Gieseker-semistable logarithmic Higgs sheaf on
({\bar{X}}_{T},D_{T})
over
T
with Hilbert polynomial
P
, satisfying condition
LF(\xi)
, and
\beta\colon\xi^{\ast}E\simeq\mathcal{O}_{T}^{n}
is a framing, is representable by a quasiprojective complex algebraic variety
R_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,\xi,rP_{0})
. Let
R_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,\xi,r)
be the disjoint union of the connected components of
R_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,nP_{0})
such that the rational Chern classes of the universal family vanish in cohomology for all closed points. Since a slope semistable logarithmic Higgs sheaf with vanishing Chern classes is locally free
[
75
, Theorem 4.3]
, and Gieseker-semistability implies slope semistability, a Gieseker-semistable logarithmic Higgs sheaf
E
with vanishing rational Chern classes on
({\bar{X}}_{T},D_{T})
over
T
is necessarily a logarithmic Higgs bundle thanks to
[
114
, Lemma 1.27]
. It follows that the condition
LF(\xi)
is always satisfied, and this finishes the proof of part
(1)
. Parts
(2)
and
(3)
then follow from
[
114
, Theorem 4.10]
.
∎

Likewise, the stack quotient
\mathcal{M}_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r):=[\mathbf{GL}_{r}\backslash R_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,\xi,r)]
is naturally identified with the stack of rank
r
Gieseker-semistable logarithmic Higgs bundles with vanishing rational Chern classes. In particular, for
({\bar{X}},D)
a projective log smooth pair, taking
S=\Spec\mathbb{C}
we let
\mathcal{M}_{Dol}({\bar{X}},D,r)
be the stack of Gieseker-semistable logarithmic Higgs bundles with vanishing rational Chern classes, which has as good moduli space
M_{Dol}({\bar{X}},D,x,r)
. We denote the disjoint unions over all
r
by
R_{Dol}(\bar{X},D,x)
,
\mathcal{M}_{Dol}(\bar{X},D)
and
M_{Dol}(\bar{X},D)
.

The stack of Gieseker-semistable logarithmic Higgs bundles with vanishing rational Chern classes and whose Higgs field has nilpotent residues is easily seen to be a closed substack which we denote
\mathcal{M}_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)=[\mathbf{GL}_{r}\backslash R_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,\xi,r)]
, with good moduli space
M_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)=\mathbf{GL}_{r}\backslash\hskip-1.99997pt\backslash R_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,\xi,r)
, in the relative case, and
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{Dol}({\bar{X}},D,r)
and
M_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}({\bar{X}},D,r)
for
S=\Spec\mathbb{C}
.

7.1.1.
The
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-action

The algebraic group
\mathbf{G}_{m}
acts on the complex algebraic variety
R_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,\xi,r)
by scaling the Higgs field. This action commutes with the action of
\mathbf{GL}_{r}
on
R_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,\xi,r)
, hence it induces an action of
\mathbf{G}_{m}
on the moduli stack
\mathcal{M}_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)
and on its good moduli space
M_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)
such that the morphisms
R_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,\xi,r)\to\mathcal{M}_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)\to M_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)
are
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-equivariant. The closed subvarieties/substacks corresponding to nilpotent residues are
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-stable, so we also obtain
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-actions on
R_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,\xi,r),\mathcal{M}_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)
and
M_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)
.

The fixed points of the action of
\mathbf{G}_{m}
(or any infinite subgroup thereof) correspond to the logarithmic Higgs bundles which come from complex variations of Hodge structure, cf.
[
112
, Theorem 8]
and the proof of
[
82
, Proposition 10.3]
.

Proposition 7.3
.

For every
x\in M_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)
, the morphism

\mathbf{G}_{m}\to M_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r),t\mapsto t\cdot x

extends uniquely to a morphism
\mathbb{A}^{1}\to M_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)
. The image of
0\in\mathbb{A}^{1}
is fixed by the action of
\mathbf{G}_{m}
and therefore corresponds to a
\mathbb{C}
-VHS.

Proof.

The morphism
\mathbf{G}_{m}\to M_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)
lifts to a morphism
\mathbf{G}_{m}\to\mathcal{M}_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)
, and it is sufficient to prove that the later extends uniquely to a morphism
\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathcal{M}_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)
. Assume that
x\in M_{Dol}(({\bar{X}}_{s},D_{s}),r)
. It follows from Langton’s theory (see
[
109
, Proposition 10.1]
or
[
74
, Theorem 5.1]
) that there exists a rank
r
Gieseker-semistable logarithmic Higgs sheaf
(E,\theta)
on
({\bar{X}}_{s}\times\mathbb{A}_{1},D_{s}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, whose restriction to
\mathbf{G}_{m}
corresponds to the morphism
\mathbf{G}_{m}\to\mathcal{M}_{Dol}(({\bar{X}},D)/S,r)
. By flatness of
E
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, the rational Chern classes of
E_{0}
are also zero. Therefore
(E_{0},\theta_{0})
is a rank
r
Gieseker-semistable logarithmic Higgs bundle on
({\bar{X}}_{s},D_{s})
by
[
75
, Theorem 4.3]
, and
E
is locally-free by
[
114
, Lemma 1.27]
.
∎

7.2.
The Riemann–Hilbert correspondence revisited

As already described in
Section

5.5
, the Riemann–Hilbert correspondence does not provide an isomorphism between
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{an}}
and
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)^{\mathrm{an}}
in the non-proper case, but it will in restriction to the locus with unipotent local monodromy.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a proper log smooth algebraic space and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{good}}
denote the open substack of
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{an}}
consisting of logarithmic connections such that for each irreducible component
D_{i}
of
D
no two eigenvalues of the residue map differ by a nonzero integer
[
22
]
.

Observe that for every
\sigma\in\Aut(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Q})
, the good locus of
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{an}}
is sent bijectively to the good locus of
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X}^{\sigma},D^{\sigma})^{\mathrm{an}}
.

Proposition 7.4
.

The restriction of the Riemann-Hilbert morphism
RH_{(\bar{X},D)}^{\mathrm{good}}:\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{good}}\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)^{\mathrm{an}}
is a surjective local isomorphism of analytic stacks.

Proof.

The existence of the Deligne canonical extension gives surjectivity (in fact surjectivity if we restrict the eigenvalues to lie in a chosen fundamental domain for the exponential
\exp:\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}^{*}
).

To prove that that it is a local isomorphism, we analyze the relative obstruction theory. We first show the relative deformation functor is unramified. For a small extension
J\to A^{\prime}\to A
of artinian
\mathbb{C}
-algebras, suppose
V^{\prime}\to V
is an extension of
A^{\prime}
-local systems on
X^{\mathrm{an}}
lying over
A^{\prime}\to A
, and suppose
(E^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
is a lift of
V^{\prime}
to
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{good}}
. Letting
j:X:=\bar{X}\setminus D\to\bar{X}
be the open inclusion, we think of
E^{\prime}
as a subsheaf
E^{\prime}\subset E^{\prime}(*D)\subset j_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{an}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{X^{\mathrm{an}}}}V^{\prime})
of the meromorphic extension (as a
A^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}
-module), which is uniquely determined by
V^{\prime}
. It is straightforward to show that the set of lifts
(E^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
of
V^{\prime}
lying above a fixed lift of
(E,\nabla)
of
V
is naturally a torsor under global sections of the kernel of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.99997pt\operatorname{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}}(E_{0},E_{0}(*D)/E_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.99997pt\operatorname{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}}(E_{0},E_{0}(*D)/E_{0})\otimes\Omega_{\bar{X}}(\log D)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ad\nabla}

where
(E_{0},\nabla_{0})
is the closed point underlying
(E^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
. Locally near a smooth point of
D
where
q
is a local defining equation for
D
, a section of the kernel is represented as a principal part
\sum_{i\geq-N}q^{-i}f_{i}
for
f_{i}
a section of
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.99997pt\operatorname{nd}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}}}(E_{0})
with
f_{-N}\neq 0
. Further, we have

\displaystyle 0=(\ad\nabla)\sum_{i\geq-N}q^{-i}f_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\geq-N}\left(-iq^{-i}f_{i}\otimes\frac{dq}{q}+q^{-i}(\ad\nabla)f_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=(-N+\operatorname{Res}\ad\nabla)f_{i}\mod(q^{1-N})

so
\operatorname{Res}\ad\nabla
must have
N
as an eigenvalue, and therefore two of the eigenvalues of
\operatorname{Res}\nabla
differ by
N
, which is a contradiction. Thus, lifts are unique if they exist.

Now, for any
\mathbb{C}
-local system
V_{0}
, any lift
(E_{0},\nabla_{0})
to the good locus is obtained via the Deligne construction with respect to some choice of fundamental domain for the exponential. Moreover, for any artinian
A
and
A
-local system
V
, lifts of
V
with the fixed closed point
(E_{0},\nabla_{0})
exist locally by the Deligne construction (which works just as well over
A
), and are unique by the above, hence glue to a global lift. Thus, the map is a local isomorphism.
∎

Let
\mathcal{M}_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)\subset\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)
be the closed substack of logarithmic connections for which the residue of the connection is nilpotent. Clearly,
\mathcal{M}_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{an}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{good}}
. On the nilpotent substack Gieseker and slope (semi)stability are equivalent (with respect to any polarization) and semistability is automatic since the rational Chern classes of any logarithmic connection with nilpotent residues vanish
[
43
, Appendix B]
. Moreover every point is GIT-semistable by
[
114
, Theorem 4.10]
. Thus we may form the good moduli space
M_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)
.

Denote by
\mathcal{M}_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
the closed substack of local systems with unipotent local monodromy, and
M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)
the good moduli space. We also define the corresponding notions for the framed moduli space
R_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)
and
R_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X,x)
.

Corollary 7.5
.

The restriction of the Riemann–Hilbert morphism gives isomorphisms
RH^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{(\bar{X},D)}:\mathcal{M}_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{an}}\to\mathcal{M}_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)^{\mathrm{an}}
,
RH^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{(\bar{X},D)}:R_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)^{\mathrm{an}}\to R^{\mathrm{unip}}(X,x)^{\mathrm{an}}
, and
RH^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{(\bar{X},D)}:M_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{an}}\to M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)^{\mathrm{an}}
.

Proof.

The claim for the stacks follows from
Proposition

7.4
and the fact that the map is bijective on points. The claim for the framed moduli spaces follows similarly. By
[
114
, Proposition 5.5]
, the analytifications of the good moduli spaces are universal categorical quotient in the category of complex analytic spaces of the respective framed moduli spaces, from which the last claims follows.
∎

7.3.
Background on tame harmonic bundles

We recall the most important elements of the theory of tame harmonic bundles, due to Corlette
[
27
]
, Simpson in the proper case
[
113
]
and the case of non-proper curves
[
112
]
and Mochizuki
[
82
,
85
]
in general.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
-complex vector bundle on a complex manifold
X
equipped with a flat connection
\nabla
. The choice of a smooth hermitian metric
h
on
\mathcal{E}

induces a canonical decomposition
\nabla=\nabla_{u}+\Psi
, where
\nabla_{u}
is a unitary connection on
\mathcal{E}
with respect to
h
and
\Psi
is self-adjoint for
h
. Both decompose in turn in
their components of type
(1,0)
and
(0,1)
:
\nabla_{u}=\partial_{\mathcal{E}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{E}}
,
\Psi=\theta+\theta^{\ast}
.
By definition, the metric
h
is pluriharmonic if the operator
\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{E}}+\theta
is integrable, i.e. if the differentiable form
(\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{E}}+\theta)^{2}\in\mathcal{A}^{2}(\End(\mathcal{E}))
is zero.

Definition 7.6
.

A harmonic bundle
(\mathcal{E},\nabla,h)
(or equivalently
(\mathcal{E},\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{E}},\theta,h)
) on a complex manifold
X
is the data of a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
-complex vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
equipped with a flat connection
\nabla
and a pluriharmonic metric
h
.

If
(\mathcal{E},\nabla,h)
is a harmonic bundle, then the holomorphic bundle
E^{Dol}:=(\mathcal{E},\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{E}})
equipped with the one-form
\theta\in\mathcal{A}^{1}(\End(\mathcal{E}))
defines a Higgs bundle. By definition, this means that
\theta
is a holomorphic one-form with values in
\End(E^{Dol})
that satisfies
\theta\wedge\theta=0
.

Let
D
be a normal crossing divisor in a smooth projective complex algebraic variety
\bar{X}
. A harmonic bundle
(\mathcal{E},\nabla,h)
on
X:=\bar{X}\setminus D
is called tame if the associated Higgs bundle
(E^{Dol},\theta)
on
X
is the restriction of a logarithmic Higgs bundle
(\bar{E},\theta)
on
(\bar{X},D)
. (It is sufficient that the coefficients of the characteristic polynomial of the Higgs field
\theta
extends as logarithmic holomorphic symmetric forms.) A tame harmonic bundle
(\mathcal{E},\nabla,h)
on
X
is purely imaginary (resp. nilpotent) if the eigenvalues of the residues of
\theta
in a logarithmic extension
(\bar{E},\theta)
of
(E^{Dol},\theta)
are purely imaginary (resp. zero). One easily checks that these definitions do not depend on the choice of the log-compactification
(\bar{X},D)
and of the extension
(\bar{E},\theta)
.

In general the correspondence between flat bundles and polystable Higgs bundles involves parabolic structures on both sides. The details will not be important for us (see
[
18
]
for a more in-depth discussion), as there are canonical choices when the residues of the operators are nilpotent, but it is useful to recall the general idea. A parabolic sheaf on
(\bar{X},D)
extending an algebraic sheaf
E
on
X
is a
\mathbb{R}^{\pi_{0}(D^{\mathrm{reg}})}
-indexed decreasing filtration
\bar{E}^{\bullet}
of the associated meromorphic bundle
j_{*}E
on
\bar{X}
by coherent subsheaves satisfying a semicontinuity condition and such that
\bar{E}^{\alpha+e_{i}}=\bar{E}^{\alpha}(-D_{i})
. A parabolic bundle is a parabolic sheaf which is Zariski-locally isomorphic to a direct sum of parabolic line bundles (i.e. parabolic sheaves which
are locally-free of rank 1). A locally free coherent extension
\bar{E}
determines a parabolic bundle extending
E
by setting
\bar{E}^{\alpha}:=E(\sum-\lfloor\alpha_{i}\rfloor D_{i})
; such a parabolic bundle is said to be trivial. A tame pluriharmonic metric naturally induces a parabolic extension—the moderate growth extension—of the associated flat and Higgs bundles according to order of growth of the norm. Finally, a local system admits a natural parabolic bundle extension—called the Deligne–Manin parabolic extension—via the Deligne construction.

Theorem 7.7

(Mochizuki
[
85
, Theorem 1.1]
)
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a projective log smooth variety with ample bundle
L
and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
.

(1)

A flat filtered regular
\lambda
-connection bundle
(\bar{E}^{\bullet},D)
is
\mu_{L}
-polystable with vanishing rational parabolic chern classes if and only if its restriction to
X
admits a tame pluriharmonic metric for which the moderate growth parabolic extension agrees with
\bar{E}^{\bullet}
. The metric is unique up to flat automorphisms.

(2)

For a tame harmonic bundle
(E,\nabla,h)
on
X
, the following are true:

(a)

The parabolic structure on the associated filtered regular flat bundle is the Deligne–Manin extension if and only if the tame harmonic bundle is purely imaginary.

(b)

The parabolic structure on the associated filtered regular Higgs bundle is trivial if and only if the eigenvalues of the residues of the connection in the De Rham realization have integral real part, or equivalently if the local monodromy in the Betti realization has purely positive real eigenvalues.

Corollary 7.8
.

Fix
(\bar{X},D)
and
L
as above. There is an equivalence of categories via purely imaginary tame harmonic bundles with unipotent local monodromy between semisimple logarithmic flat vector bundles with nilpotent residues and
\mu_{L}
-polystable logarithmic Higgs bundles on
\bar{X}
with vanishing rational chern classes and nilpotent residues.

If
f\colon X\to Y
is an algebraic morphism between two smooth quasiprojective complex varieties, then the pull-back of a tame (resp. tame purely imaginary) harmonic bundle on
Y
is a tame (resp. tame purely imaginary) harmonic bundle on
X
, see
[
85
, Lemma 25.29]
. In particular, one gets the following result.

Theorem 7.9

(Corlette, Mochizuki
[
84
, Theorem 25.30]
)
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be an algebraic morphism between two smooth algebraic spaces. If
V
is a semisimple complex local system on
Y
, then its pull-back
f^{*}V
is a semisimple complex local system on
X
.

Proof.

The usual statement is for quasiprojective varieties. In general, there is an affine subspace of
X
mapping to an affine subspace of
Y

[
118
,
Tag 06NH
]
, and we apply the result to them.
∎

Remark 7.10
.

Let
X
be a smooth quasiprojective complex variety. If
(E,\nabla)
is a semisimple flat bundle on
X
, then thanks to Theorem
7.7
every choice of a tame purely imaginary pluriharmonic metric
h
on
(E,\nabla)
will yield the same (algebraic) Higgs bundle on
X
.

Proposition 7.11

(See (21.19) in
[
85
]
)
.

Let
(E,\theta,h)
be a harmonic bundle on a Kähler manifold
X
. Then the following equality between the energy density
e^{u}
of the corresponding twisted pluriharmonic map
u
and the norm of the Higgs field holds:

8\cdot|\theta|^{2}=e^{u}.

Proposition 7.12
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a projective log smooth variety and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Endow
X
with a Poincaré-type Kähler metric. Let
(E,\theta,h)
be a purely imaginary tame harmonic bundle on
X
. Then the following properties are equivalent:

(1)

the eigenvalues of the local monodromy have modulus one,

(2)

the residues of the Higgs field are nilpotent,

(3)

(E,\theta,h)
has finite energy.

We say that a harmonic bundle on a Kähler manifold
X
has finite energy if the associated twisted pluriharmonic map has finite energy (of course this depends on the choice of the metric on
X
).

Proof.

The equivalence between
(1)
and
(2)
is a consequence of the table in
[
112
, p.720]
. Assume that the residues of the Higgs field are nilpotent, so that
(E,\theta,h)
is a nilpotent tame harmonic bundle. By
[
112
, Theorem 1]
and
[
80
, Corollary 4.1]
, the norm of
\theta
is bounded with respect to the Poincaré-type metric on
X
, so that by
Proposition

7.11

(E,\theta,h)
has finite energy.
The implication
(3)\implies(2)
can be proved with arguments similar to those appearing in the proof of
Proposition

10.40
below. Since this implication is not used in this paper, we leave the details to the interested reader.
∎

7.4.
The nilpotent residue De Rham–Dolbeault comparison

Assume
(\bar{X},D)
is a projective log smooth variety. Recall that
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{Dol}(\bar{X},D)\subset\mathcal{M}_{Dol}(\bar{X},D)
is the closed substack of semistable logarithmic Higgs bundles whose Higgs fields has nilpotent residues and
M_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)
is its good moduli space, whose points correspond to polystable logarithmic Higgs bundles with nilpotent residues. According to
Corollary

7.8
, solving for the harmonic metric yields a bijective map of sets

(7.4.1)

SM^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{(\bar{X},D)}:M_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)(\mathbb{C})\to M_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)(\mathbb{C})

which is functorial with respect to pull-back along algebraic morphisms.

The main result of this subsection is the following:

Theorem 7.13
.

For a projective log smooth curve
(\bar{X},D)
, the comparison
SM^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{(\bar{X},D)}
is a homeomorphism in the euclidean topology.

Corollary 7.14
.

For any projective log smooth variety
(\bar{X},D)
,
SM^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{(\bar{X},D)}
is a continuous bijection. In particular, for any compact
K\subset M^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{Dol}(\bar{X},D)(\mathbb{C})
it induces a homeomorphism
K\to SM_{\bar{X},D}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(K)
.

Proof of
Corollary

7.14
assuming
Theorem

7.13
.

By an appropriate Lefschetz theorem (e.g.
[
53
, §II.5.1]
), for a general sufficiently ample curve
C\subset X
, the inclusion
i:C\to X
induces a surjection
i_{*}:\pi_{1}(C,x_{0})\to\pi_{1}(X,x_{0})
for
x_{0}\in C
. The pull-back
i^{*}_{B}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(C)
is therefore a closed immersion, as is the coarse map
i^{*}_{B}:M_{B}(X)\to M_{B}(C)
; likewise for the unipotent local monodromy loci, and the nilpotent residues locus in the De Rham realization, by
Corollary

7.5
. We have the commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{C},D_{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{C},D_{C}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}_{Dol}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SM^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{(\bar{X},D_{X})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SM^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{(\bar{C},D_{C})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}_{DR}}

Since
i_{DR}^{*}
is a closed immersion and both vertical maps are bijective on the level of sets, it follows from the theorem that the left vertical map is continuous.
∎

The main step of the proof of
Theorem

7.13
is the following:

Proposition 7.15
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a projective log smooth curve with basepoint
x\in X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
\mathbf{E}_{i}=(\mathcal{E}_{i},\bar{\partial}_{i},\theta_{i},h_{i},\phi_{i})
be a sequence of framed rank
r
tame nilpotent harmonic bundles on the curve
X
with unipotent local monodromy such that the coefficients of the characteristic polynomial
P(\theta_{i})
are uniformly bounded (in the space of global log symmetric forms) and such that the framing
\phi_{i}:(V_{0},h_{0})\xrightarrow{\cong}(\mathcal{E}_{i,x},h_{i,x})
respects the metric. Then there is a framed tame nilpotent harmonic bundle
(\mathcal{E}_{\infty},\bar{\partial}_{\infty},\theta_{\infty},h_{\infty},\phi_{\infty})
with unipotent local monodromy and whose framing respects the metric such that, up to passing to a subsequence, the associated sequence of framed logarithmic flat bundles
(\bar{E}^{DR}_{i},\nabla_{i},\phi_{i})
(resp. framed polystable logarithmic Higgs bundles
(\bar{E}_{i}^{Dol},\theta_{i},\phi_{i})
) converges in
R_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)
(resp.
R_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)
) to
(\bar{E}_{\infty}^{DR},\nabla_{\infty},\phi_{\infty})
(resp.
(\bar{E}_{\infty}^{Dol},\theta_{\infty},\phi_{\infty})
).

Throughout we use the following notation. For a complex manifold
M
, we say a complex-valued function on
M
is
L^{\infty}_{loc}
bounded if its restriction to any compact
K\subset M
is
L^{\infty}
-bounded. Likewise for
W^{1,2}_{loc}
. We also use the following elementary fact.

Lemma 7.16
.

A family of smooth complex-valued functions
f
on the disk
\mathbb{D}
for which both
f
and
\bar{\partial}f
are uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}_{loc}
has relatively compact restriction to
L^{\infty}(K)
for any compact
K\subset\mathbb{D}
.

Proof.

See for example
[
70
, Chapter 3, Exercise 3.6]
. This follows from the generalized Cauchy inequality, which shows that such a family is locally uniformly Hölder continuous with exponent
1-\epsilon
for any
0<\epsilon<1
, as well as the Arzelà–Ascoli theorem.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

7.15
.

We begin with some general remarks. Let
\Delta
be the symmetric space of positive definite hermitian forms on a fixed rank
r
vector space
V_{0}
and choose a basepoint
h_{0}
of
\Delta
. Given a harmonic bundle
\mathbf{V}:=(\mathcal{V},\bar{\partial},\theta,h)
on a complex manifold
M
, a basepoint
m\in M
, and a metric framing
\psi:(V_{0},h_{0})\xrightarrow{\cong}(\mathcal{V}_{m},h_{m})
, using the flat connection on
\mathbf{V}
we obtain a pluriharmonic map
f_{\mathbf{V},\psi}:\tilde{M}\to\Delta
which is equivariant with respect to the monodromy representation
\rho_{\mathbf{V},\psi}:\pi_{1}(M,m)\to\GL(V_{0})
.

Step 1.
Let
M
be a simply-connected complex manifold equipped with a Riemannian metric,
m_{0}\in M
a basepoint, and
\mathbf{V}_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\bar{\partial}_{i},\theta_{i},h_{i})
a sequence of harmonic bundles on
M
with framings
\psi_{i}:(V_{0},h_{0})\xrightarrow{\cong}(\mathcal{V}_{i,m},h_{m})
. Assume the associated pluriharmonic maps
f_{\mathbf{V}_{i},\psi_{i}}:M\to\Delta
have locally uniformly bounded energy. Then after passing to a subsequence, the
f_{\mathbf{V}_{i},\psi_{i}}
converge strongly in
W^{1,2}_{loc}
and
L_{loc}^{\infty}
to a (smooth) pluriharmonic map
f_{\infty}:M\to\Delta
. The resulting metrics
h_{i}
on the trivial
C^{\infty}
bundle
C^{\infty}_{M}\otimes V_{0}
converge strongly in
W^{1,2}_{loc}
and
L^{\infty}_{loc}
to
h_{\infty}
, and the operators
\bar{\partial}_{i},\theta_{i}
converge strongly in
L^{\infty}_{loc}
to
\bar{\partial}_{\infty},\theta_{\infty}
.

Proof.

The statement about the convergence of the maps follows from
Proposition

6.15
. As the harmonic metric is pulled back from
\Delta
, the convergence statements follow for
h_{i}
. The harmonic metric
h
uniquely determines the operators
\bar{\partial},\theta
, since the connection form of
\partial+\bar{\partial}
in the flat basis is
\frac{1}{2}h^{-T}dh
, which is also the matrix of
-(\theta+\theta^{*})
. Moreover,
\theta_{i}
(and therefore also the difference
A_{i}:=\bar{\partial}_{i}-\bar{\partial}\otimes\operatorname{id}
) is uniformly
L^{\infty}_{loc}
bounded since the coefficients of the
P(\theta_{i})
are uniformly bounded
[
113
, Lemma 2.7]
. Since
\bar{\partial}_{i}\theta_{i}=0
we have
(\bar{\partial}\otimes\operatorname{id})\theta_{i}=-[A_{i},\theta_{i}]
, which is uniformly
L^{\infty}_{loc}
bounded. Thus, by
Lemma

7.16
, after passing to a further subsequence, the operators
\bar{\partial}_{i},\theta_{i}
converge strongly in
L^{\infty}_{loc}
to
\bar{\partial}_{\infty},\theta_{\infty}
.
∎

We now return to the setting of the proposition.

Step 2.
After passing to a subsequence of the framed harmonic bundles in the proposition, the monodromy representations
\rho_{\mathbf{E}_{i},\phi_{i}}
converge to a limit representation
\rho_{\infty}:\pi_{1}(X,x)\to\GL(V_{0})
and there is a smooth pluriharmonic
\rho_{\infty}
-equivariant map
f_{\infty}:\tilde{X}\to\Delta
to which the
f_{\mathbf{E}_{i},\phi_{i}}
converges strongly in
W^{1,2}_{loc}
and
L^{\infty}_{loc}
. Moreover, the resulting harmonic bundle
(\mathcal{E}_{\infty},\bar{\partial}_{\infty},\theta_{\infty},h_{\infty})
is tame and purely imaginary. Finally, for any relatively compact
u\in U\subset X
, there are identifications
\alpha_{i}:\mathcal{E}_{i}|_{U}\to\mathcal{E}_{\infty}|_{U}
of
C^{\infty}_{U}
-bundles preserving the metric at
u
such that, up to passing to a subsequence,
\alpha_{i*}h_{i}
(resp.
\alpha_{i*}\bar{\partial}_{i}
resp.
\alpha_{i*}\theta_{i}
) converge strongly to
h_{\infty}
(resp.
\bar{\partial}_{\infty}
resp.
\theta_{\infty}
) in
W^{1,2}_{loc}
and
L^{\infty}_{loc}
(resp.
L^{\infty}_{loc}
resp.
L^{\infty}_{loc}
).

Proof.

As in the previous step, by
Proposition

7.11
the energies of the maps
f_{\mathbf{E}_{i},\phi_{i}}
are locally uniformly bounded. Choose loops
\gamma_{1}.\ldots,\gamma_{n}
generating
\pi_{1}(X,x)
. Applying Step 1 to a finite cover of each
\gamma_{i}
by simply-connected relatively compact open subsets
x\in U\subset X
, it follows that, after passing to a subsequence, the monodromy operators
\rho_{\mathbf{E}_{i},x}(\gamma_{j})
are constrained to lie in a compact subset of
\GL(V_{0})
, and therefore after passing to a further subsequence it follows that the limit representation
\rho_{\infty}
exists. For any
\gamma\in\pi_{1}(X,x)
, taking a finite cover of
X
by simply connected
x\in U\subset X
, we have that
f_{i}|_{\gamma\tilde{U}}=\rho_{\mathbf{E}_{i},x}(\gamma)\circ f_{i}|_{\tilde{U}}
. It follows that
\rho_{\infty}(\gamma)\circ f_{\infty}|_{\tilde{U}}
agrees with
f_{\infty}|_{\gamma\tilde{U}}
on overlaps, and since the pointwise limit of the
f_{i}
is unique if it exists, the first claim follows from Step 1. The boundedness of the characteristic polynomials implies the second claim. For the last claim, we may glue together local identifications of the
C^{\infty}
bundles via flat sections on finitely many relatively compact simply-connected open sets using a fixed partition of unity, and the metrics and operators will have the same convergence properties.
∎

Let
\mathbb{D}^{*}
be a disk neighborhood of a puncture of
X
with coordinate
q
and basepoint
u\in\mathbb{D}^{*}
. Choose framings
\chi_{i}:(V_{0},h_{0})\xrightarrow{\cong}(\mathcal{E}_{i,u},h_{i,u})
,
\chi_{\infty}:(V_{0},h_{0})\xrightarrow{\cong}(\mathcal{E}_{\infty,u},h_{\infty,u})
be the induced framings obtained via flat transport. It follows from Step 2 that the associated harmonic maps
f_{\mathbf{E}_{i}|_{\mathbb{D}^{*}},\chi_{i}}:\tilde{\mathbb{D}}^{*}\to\Delta
converge in
W^{1,2}_{loc}
and
L^{\infty}_{loc}
to
f_{\mathbf{E}_{\infty}|_{\mathbb{D}^{*}},\chi_{\infty}}
.

Let
V:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}}\otimes V_{0}
be the trivial holomorphic bundle. We have a canonical identification between
V
and the Deligne extension
\bar{E}^{DR}_{i}|_{\mathbb{D}}
(resp.
\bar{E}_{\infty}^{DR}|_{\mathbb{D}}
) of the flat bundle underlying each
\mathbf{E}_{i}|_{\mathbb{D}^{*}}
(resp.
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}|_{\mathbb{D}^{*}}
) by identifying, for each
v\in V_{0}
, the constant section
1\otimes v
of
V
with
e^{zN_{i}}\tilde{v}
(resp.
e^{zN_{\infty}}\tilde{v}
), where
\tilde{v}
is the flat continuation of
\chi_{i}(v)
(resp.
\chi_{\infty}(v)
),
z=\log q
and
N_{i},N_{\infty}
are the nilpotent local monodromy logarithms. Note that the
N_{i}
converge to
N_{\infty}
. Let
h_{i}^{V},\bar{\partial}_{i}^{V},\theta_{i}^{V}
be the transport of the metrics and operators to
C^{\infty}_{\mathbb{D}^{*}}\otimes V_{0}
via this identification. Note that these operators converge in the same way to their limits on
C^{\infty}_{\mathbb{D}^{*}}\otimes V_{0}
, since they differ from the flat trivialization by
e^{zN_{i}}
.

According to
[
82
, Prop. 7.4]
(taking
U_{0}=\{0\}\subset(a-1,a)
in the notation therein), there is a
\bar{\partial}^{V}_{i}
-holomorphic frame
\mathbf{e}_{i}
of
C_{\mathbb{D}^{*}}\otimes V_{0}
with the property that:

(1)

Under the above identification,
\mathbf{e}_{i}
is a holomorphic frame of
\bar{E}_{i}^{Dol}|_{\mathbb{D}}
.

(2)

h_{i}^{V}(\mathbf{e}_{i}(j))\leq C(\Im z)^{k}
for some
k
and some fixed constant
C>0
.

(3)

C^{\prime-1}\leq h_{i}^{V}(\det\mathbf{e}_{i})\leq C^{\prime}
for some fixed constant
C^{\prime}>0
, where
\det\mathbf{e}_{i}=\mathbf{e}_{i}(1)\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathbf{e}_{i}(r)
.

Step 3.
There is a
\bar{\partial}^{V}_{\infty}
-holomorphic frame
\mathbf{e}_{\infty}
of
C^{\infty}_{\mathbb{D}^{*}}\otimes V_{0}
to which the frames
\mathbf{e}_{i}
converge strongly in
L^{\infty}_{loc}
after passing to a subsequence. The limit frame
\mathbf{e}_{\infty}
satisfies properties (1)-(3) above (with
i=\infty
).

Proof.

The frames
\mathbf{e}_{i}
are uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}_{loc}
, hence converge weakly to an
r
-tuple of sections
\mathbf{e}_{\infty}
in
L^{q}_{loc}
for all
q<\infty
. Since
\bar{\partial}_{i}^{V}
converges strongly in
L_{loc}^{\infty}
to
\bar{\partial}_{\infty}^{V}
, it follows that
\bar{\partial}_{\infty}^{V}\mathbf{e}_{\infty}=0
weakly, so by
\bar{\partial}
-regularity it follows that
\mathbf{e}_{\infty}
is in fact comprised of
\bar{\partial}^{V}_{\infty}
-holomorphic sections. Let
g_{i}
be the change of basis matrix from
\mathbf{e_{i}}
to
\mathbf{e}_{\infty}
, which is
L^{\infty}_{loc}
bounded.

Claim
.

The
g_{i}
converge strongly in
L^{\infty}_{loc}
to
\operatorname{id}
after passing to a subsequence.

Proof.

Let
\bar{\partial}^{V}_{i}=\bar{\partial}^{V}_{\infty}+B_{i}
, so
B_{i}\to 0
strongly in
L^{\infty}_{loc}
; we have
\bar{\partial}_{\infty}^{V}g_{i}=-g_{i}B_{i}
which is uniformly
L^{\infty}_{loc}
bounded. Thus, by
Lemma

7.16
the
g_{i}
converge strongly in
L^{\infty}_{loc}
after passing to a subsequence.
∎

Thus, the norms
h_{\infty}^{V}(\mathbf{e}_{\infty}(j))
satisfy inequalities (2) and (3). In particular,
h_{\infty}^{V}(\det\mathbf{e}_{\infty})
is nowhere vanishing on
\mathbb{D}^{*}
, and neither vanishes nor goes to infinity faster than a power of
\Im z
at the puncture. The extension
\bar{E}_{\infty}^{Dol}
is the moderate growth extension with respect to
h_{\infty}
, so property (1) follows.
∎

By mapping the
\mathbf{e}_{i}
frame to
\mathbf{e}_{\infty}
, we obtain holomorphic identifications
\beta_{i}^{Dol}:\bar{E}_{i}^{Dol}|_{\mathbb{D}}\to\bar{E}_{\infty}^{Dol}|_{\mathbb{D}}
. From Steps 2 and 3, it follows that
\beta^{Dol}_{i*}h_{i}
(resp.
\beta^{Dol}_{i*}\theta_{i}
) converges to
h_{\infty}
(resp.
\theta_{\infty}
) strongly in
L^{\infty}_{loc}
.

Step 4.
With respect to any metric on
\bar{E}_{\infty}^{Dol}|_{\mathbb{D}}
,
\beta_{i*}\theta_{i}
converges to
\theta_{\infty}
in
L^{\infty}(\End(\bar{E}_{\infty}^{Dol}|_{\mathbb{D}})\otimes\omega_{\mathbb{D}}(0))
, possibly after shrinking
\mathbb{D}
.

Proof.

The
\beta_{i*}^{Dol}\theta_{i}
are a sequence of sections of a holomorphic vector bundle which converge strongly in
L^{\infty}_{loc}
to a holomorphic section. The Cauchy integral formula implies both uniform boundedness and equicontinuity on
\mathbb{D}
, hence uniform convergence.
∎

Step 5.
End of proof.

Using a fixed partition of unity, we may glue together the identifications
\alpha_{i}:\mathcal{E}_{i}|_{U}\to\mathcal{E}_{\infty}|_{U}
of the
C^{\infty}
bundles of Step 2 on the complement
U
of small disk neighborhoods of the punctures with the holomorphic identifications
\beta_{i}^{Dol}:\bar{E}_{i}^{Dol}|_{\mathbb{D}}\to\bar{E}_{\infty}^{Dol}|_{\mathbb{D}}
from Step 4 to obtain identifications
\eta_{i}:C^{\infty}_{\bar{X}}\otimes\bar{E}_{i}^{Dol}\to C^{\infty}_{\bar{X}}\otimes\bar{E}_{\infty}^{Dol}
which preserve the metric at a chosen basepoint
x
. We will therefore consider the operators
\bar{\partial}_{i},\theta_{i}
on a fixed
C^{\infty}_{\bar{X}}
bundle
\bar{\mathcal{E}}
; from Steps 2 and 4 we deduce that they both converge to
\bar{\partial}_{\infty},\theta_{\infty}
in
L^{\infty}
with respect to any choice of metric, which in particular implies they converge in operator norm as operators
W^{1,\infty}(\bar{E}^{Dol}_{\infty})\to L^{\infty}(\bar{E}^{Dol}_{\infty}\otimes\omega_{\bar{X}}(D))
. According to
[
114
, Lemma 5.12]
, this implies the corresponding sequence of framed polystable logarithmic Higgs bundles
(\bar{E}_{i}^{Dol},\theta_{i},\phi_{i})
converges to
(\bar{E}_{\infty}^{Dol},\theta_{\infty},\phi_{\infty})
. The convergence in the De Rham realization follows from the convergence of the monodromy representations, by Riemann–Hilbert.
∎

Let
(V_{0},h_{0})
be a fixed vector space with a hermitian form of the relevant rank. As in
[
115
, §7]
, we denote by
R_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})^{(V_{0},h_{0})}\subset R_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})
(resp.
R_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})^{(V_{0},h_{0})}\subset R_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})
) the subspace of semisimple logarithmic connections with nilpotent residues (resp. polystable logarithmic Higgs bundles with nilpotent residues and trivial Chern class) which are framed at
x
and which admit a tame purely imaginary harmonic metric which is identified with
h_{0}
at
x
via the framing.

As in
[
115
, §7]
we deduce from
Proposition

7.15
:

Corollary 7.17
.

The correspondence

RSM^{\mathrm{nilp},(V_{0},h_{0})}_{(\bar{X},D)}:R_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})^{(V_{0},h_{0})}\to R_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})^{(V_{0},h_{0})}

is a homeomorphism.

Proof.

To show a bijection
f:X\to Y
of metric spaces is continuous, it suffices to show any convergent sequence in the source
x_{i}\to x_{\infty}
has a subsequence such that
f(x_{i})\to f(x_{\infty})
. For any sequence of points
(\bar{E}_{i}^{Dol},\theta_{i},\phi_{i})
in
R_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})^{(V_{0},h_{0})}
converging to
(\bar{E}_{\infty}^{Dol},\theta_{\infty},\phi_{\infty})
in
R_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})
, the image in
M_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)
converges, hence the corresponding sequence of framed harmonic bundles satisfies the condition of the proposition, and the images
RSM^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{\bar{X},D}(\bar{E}_{i}^{Dol},\theta_{i},\phi_{i})
converge to
RSM^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{\bar{X},D}(\bar{E}_{\infty}^{Dol},\theta_{\infty},\phi_{\infty})
up to passing to a subsequence. This shows continuity of
SM^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{\bar{X},D}
.

To show continuity of the inverse, observe that one can construct harmonic maps by minimizing the energy (see Theorem
6.8
). Since for
K\subset R_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X,x,r)
compact there are
\rho
-equivariant maps
f_{\rho}:\tilde{X}\to\Delta
with uniformly bounded energy for all
\rho\in K
, for example by the construction of an initial metric as in
[
62
, section 2.4]
, it follows that a given convergent sequence of points in the target
(\bar{E}_{i}^{DR},\nabla_{i},\phi_{i})\to(\bar{E}_{\infty}^{DR},\nabla_{\infty},\phi_{\infty})
can be lifted to a sequence of harmonic bundles satisfying the conditions of the proposition, hence converges in the Dolbeault realization.
∎

Corollary 7.18
.

R_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})^{(V_{0},h_{0})}\to M_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)(\mathbb{C})
and
R_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})^{(V_{0},h_{0})}\to M_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)(\mathbb{C})
are proper.

Proof.

The Dolbeault part is immediate from
Proposition

7.15
.
For the De Rham part, according to the Kempf–Ness theorem (specifically in the form
[
103
, (4.7) Corollary]
), there is a subset
S\subset R_{B}(X,x,r)(\mathbb{C})
such that
S\to M_{B}(X,r)(\mathbb{C})
is proper with respect to the euclidean topology. Thus, by lifting each simple factor and passing to a subsequence there is a convergent lift
(\bar{E}_{i}^{DR},\nabla,\phi^{\prime}_{i})
of
(\bar{E}_{i}^{DR},\nabla_{i})
to
R_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}
. By the argument of the previous corollary, the sequence of tame nilpotent harmonic bundles associated to
(\bar{E}_{i}^{DR},\nabla,\phi^{\prime}_{i})
has uniformly bounded energy, and the same will be true after modifying the framings to get a lift
(\bar{E}_{i}^{DR},\nabla,\phi_{i})
to
R_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})^{(V_{0},h_{0})}
. Passing to a subsequence, there is then a limit by the proposition.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

7.13
.

The previous two corollaries imply that any convergent sequence on either side of (
7.4.1
) corresponds to a convergent sequence on the other side after passing to a subsequence.
∎

7.5.
The
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-action on the Betti realization

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a projective log smooth variety and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
L
be an ample line bundle on
\bar{X}
. If
(E_{\ast},\theta)
is a
\mu_{L}
-polystable regular filtered Higgs bundle on
(\bar{X},D)
with vanishing first and second rational parabolic Chern classes, then
(E_{\ast},t\cdot\theta)
is a
\mu_{L}
-polystable regular filtered Higgs bundle on
(X,D)
with vanishing first and second rational parabolic Chern classes for every
t\in\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
. Moreover, if
t\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
,
(E_{\ast},\theta)
is purely imaginary if and only if
(E_{\ast},t\cdot\theta)
is purely imaginary. Therefore, using the correspondence between semisimple complex local systems on
X
and purely imaginary
\mu_{L}
-polystable regular filtered Higgs bundle on
(\bar{X},D)
with vanishing first and second rational parabolic Chern classes (which follows from
Theorem

7.7
), we get a set-theoretic action of
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
on the points of the good moduli space
M_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
. Moreover, this action extends to a
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action on
M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)(\mathbb{C})
by
Corollary

7.8
.

Lemma 7.19

(Simpson, Mochizuki)
.

A semisimple complex local system
V
underlies a complex variation of pure Hodge structures if and only if the corresponding point of
M_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is fixed by
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
(or any infinite subgroup thereof).

Proof.

The same argument as in
[
112
, Theorem 8]
works given the correspondence
Theorem

7.7
.
∎

Remark 7.20
.

For a
\mu_{L}
-polystable regular filtered Higgs bundle
(E_{*},\theta)
(with no condition on the residues) we can still associate a local system to
(E_{*},t\cdot\theta)
for
t\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
. Choosing a hermitian metric
h_{0}
on a framing space
V_{0}
and framing compatibly with the harmonic metric, Mochizuki proves
[
82
, Theorem 10.1]
that the resulting map
\mathbb{C}^{*}\to U(V_{0},h_{0})\backslash R_{B}(X,x)(\mathbb{C})^{(V_{0},h_{0})}
(which is independent of choices) is continuous. In particular, the map to
M_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is continuous. Moreover, the image of
\mathbb{D}^{*}
is relatively compact
[
82
, Lemma 10.2]
. This does not yield an action on
M_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
however, since the family depends on the choice of parabolic structure on the initial local system, and the resulting parabolic structure on the translate may not be the Deligne–Manin parabolic structure.

It follows from the functoriality of the Simpson–Mochizuki correspondence that the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
action (i) is independent of the choice of compactification, and therefore (ii) can be defined functorially for connected normal algebraic spaces:

Lemma 7.21
.

There is a unique extension of the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-action to
M_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
for any connected normal algebraic space
X
which is functorial with respect to pullback and agrees with the above description for
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
where
(\bar{X},D)
is projective log smooth.

Proof.

Functoriality with respect to morphisms
f:X\to Y
of smooth varieties is standard. For
X
a connected normal algebraic space, we must define the action on
M_{B}(X)
via the embedding
M_{B}(X)\subset M_{B}(U)
induced by restriction to any smooth affine
U\subset X
. It remains to check compatibility with respect to pullback. Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of connected normal algebraic spaces, let
\pi:\tilde{Y}\to Y
be a resolution. There is a proper generically finite dominant morphism
g:\tilde{X}\to X
with
\tilde{X}
smooth such that
\tilde{X}\to Y
lifts to
\tilde{f}:\tilde{X}\to\tilde{Y}
, and there is a dense open smooth subset
U\subset X
for which the base-change
g_{U}:\tilde{U}\to U
of
g
is finite étale. It suffices to check that in the pullback diagram on the right induced by the commutative diagram on the left, the bottom map must be
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-equivariant.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{B}(\tilde{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{B}(\tilde{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{B}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{B}(Y).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}_{U}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{U}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{U}^{*}}

Since
M_{B}(U)\to M_{B}(\tilde{U})
is quasifinite and each
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbit is continuous, it follows that the inverse image of any
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbit in
M_{B}(\tilde{U})
is a disjoint union of
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbits in
M_{B}(U)
each of which maps isomorphically and equivariantly onto its image. For
V\in M_{B}(Y)(\mathbb{C})
, the image
f_{U}^{*}\mathbb{R}_{>0}V
is thus one of the components of the preimage of
\tilde{f}_{U}^{*}\pi^{*}\mathbb{R}_{>0}V
, hence
f_{U}^{*}
is equivariant.
∎

The similarity with the compact case is restored if we restrict our attention to local systems with unipotent local monodromy using
Theorem

7.13
: for a projective log smooth curve
(\bar{X},D)
, we obtain a continuous action of
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
on
M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)
. Our main use of
Theorem

7.13
will be for the following:

Theorem 7.22
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space. Let
\Sigma
be a
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-stable
\mathbb{C}
-constructible subset of
M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)(\mathbb{C})
. Then

(1)

\overline{\Sigma}
is
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-stable.

(2)

Each irreducible component of
\overline{\Sigma}
is
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-stable.

(3)

For any
V\in\overline{\Sigma}
, the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbit of
V
completes to a continuous map
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\to\overline{\Sigma}
and the image of 0 is a
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-fixed point.

In particular, every irreducible component of
\Sigma
contains a
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-fixed point.

Before the proof we establish some preliminary facts.

Lemma 7.23
.

Let
X
be a reduced algebraic space and
X^{\mathrm{ball}}\subset X^{\mathrm{an}}
the open subset of points of
X^{\mathrm{an}}
with a neighborhood homeomorphic to a ball. Then taking closure gives a bijection from connected components of
X^{\mathrm{ball}}
to irreducible components of
X
.

Proof.

We first claim that the inclusion
X^{\mathrm{reg}}\subset X^{\mathrm{ball}}
of the regular locus induces a bijection on connected components. Observe that the dimension of a ball neighborhood of a point is a locally constant function on
X^{\mathrm{ball}}
, hence constant on connected components, so we may assume it is constant and equal to
n
. Since
X^{\mathrm{ball}}
can be given a countable locally finite finite-dimensional triangulation such that
X^{\mathrm{ball}}\setminus X^{\mathrm{reg}}
is a subcomplex of dimension
\leq n-2
, from the long exact sequence in Borel–Moore homology we have

0=H_{n}^{BM}(X^{\mathrm{ball}}\setminus X^{\mathrm{reg}})\to H_{n}^{BM}(X^{\mathrm{ball}})\to H_{n}^{BM}(X^{\mathrm{reg}})\to H_{n-1}^{BM}(X^{\mathrm{ball}}\setminus X^{\mathrm{reg}})=0

whence the claim.

To finish, it remains to observe that since
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
is dense in
X^{\mathrm{ball}}
, each connected component of
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
has the same closure as the corresponding connected component of
X^{\mathrm{ball}}
.
∎

Corollary 7.24
.

Let
X
be a reduced algebraic space. Let
f
be a homeomorphism of the set
X(\mathbb{C})
equipped with the euclidean topology. Then
f
sends every irreducible component of
X(\mathbb{C})
homeomorphically onto a (possibly different) irreducible component of
X(\mathbb{C})
.

Proposition 7.25
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a proper log smooth algebraic space and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
\Sigma
be a
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-stable (resp.
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-stable) Zariski closed subset of
M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)(\mathbb{C})
. Then, every irreducible component of
\Sigma
is
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-stable (resp.
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-stable).

Proof.

By taking a projective modification, Lefschetz, and functoriality of the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action, we may assume
X
is a curve. By
Theorem

7.13
, the map
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\times M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)(\mathbb{C})\to M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)(\mathbb{C})
defining the action of
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
on
M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is continuous. Therefore, the induced map
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\times\Sigma\to\Sigma
is also continuous, so each
t\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
acts as a homeomorphism on
\Sigma
. By
Corollary

7.24
,
t
sends each irreducible component of
\Sigma
to an irreducible component of
\Sigma
. Therefore we get an action of
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
on the set of irreducible components of
\Sigma
. Since the map
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\times\Sigma\to\Sigma
is continuous, this action is locally constant. Since
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
is connected and
1\in\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
acts by the identity, we get that the action of
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
stabilizes every irreducible component of
\Sigma
. The proof for the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-stable case is the same.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

7.22
.

Again by taking a projective modification and Lefschetz we may assume
X
is a curve. Then (1) and (2) follow immediately from
Theorem

7.13
and
Proposition

7.25
. For (3) we may then assume that
\Sigma
is Zariski closed and irreducible. Thanks to
Corollary

7.5
and
Theorem

7.13
, the comparison map
M_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)(\mathbb{C})\to M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is a homeomorphism. For any
(E,\theta)\in M_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)(\mathbb{C})
, the limit of
(E,t\cdot\theta)
when
t
goes to zero exists in
M_{Dol}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)(\mathbb{C})
by Proposition
7.3
and it is a
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
fixed point. If
(E,\theta)
corresponds to a point in
\Sigma
, since
\Sigma
is closed and
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-stable, the limit is a
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
-fixed point in
\Sigma
.
∎

Corollary 7.26
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space and
\Sigma\subset M_{B}(X)
a nonempty
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-stable subset of semisimple local systems with quasiunipotent local monodromy. Then
\overline{\Sigma}
contains a
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-fixed point.

Proof.

By the functoriality of the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-action, we may assume
X
is smooth and quasiprojective. For any
V\in\Sigma
, there is a finite étale cover
\pi:X^{\prime}\to X
(see
Proposition

8.2
) such that
\pi^{*}V\in\mathcal{M}_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X^{\prime})(\mathbb{C})
. The
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbit of
\pi^{*}V
has a limit at
t\to 0
in
\overline{\pi^{*}\Sigma}
, by
Theorem

7.22
. By
[
82
, Lemma 10.2]
(see
Remark

7.20
), the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbit of
V
has an accumulation point in
\overline{\Sigma}
as
t\to 0
. Since any lift of an
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-fixed point of
M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X^{\prime})
to
M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)
is
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-fixed (since variations of Hodge structures push forward under finite étale maps and factors of local systems underlying variations underlie variations
[
33
, Proposition 1.13]
), it follows that
\overline{\Sigma}
contains a
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-fixed point.
∎

8.
The Deligne–Hitchin space

In this section we use the Deligne–Hitchin space equipped with its
\mathbf{G}_{m}
to get around the lack of a
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-action on the Betti stack. See the works of Simpson
[
109
,
111
,
107
]
for related discussions and results.

8.1.
From quasiunipotent to unipotent

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
R\subset\mathbb{C}
be a finitely generated
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra. Let
N
be a positive integer. Then there exists a maximal ideal
I\subset R
such that the reduction morphism
R\to R/\penalty I
is injective in restriction to the
N
-roots of unity.

Proof.

The integral closure
S
of
\mathbb{Z}
in
R
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra which contains the roots of unity of
R
. If such an ideal
J\subset S
exists for
S
then we may take
I
to be any maximal ideal containing
J
. Thus we may assume
R\subset\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
. The fraction field
K\subset\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
of
R
is a number field, and
R
differs from
\mathcal{O}_{K}
only at finitely many places. Therefore one can assume from the beginning that
R=\mathcal{O}_{K}
. If
a\in R\setminus\{1\}
satisfies
a-1\in I
, then the norm of
I
divides the norm of
1-a
. The result follows by letting
a
being any
N
-root of unity.
∎

Proposition 8.2
.

Let
X
be a connected complex algebraic variety. Let
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\GL(n,\mathbb{C})
be a representation with quasi-unipotent local monodromy. Then there exists a finite étale cover
X^{\prime}\to X
such that the corresponding finite index subgroup
\pi_{1}(X^{\prime})
of
\pi_{1}(X)
contains
\ker\rho
and
\rho_{|\pi_{1}(X^{\prime})}
has unipotent local monodromy.

Proof.

Since
\pi_{1}(X)
is finitely generated,
\rho
takes values in
\GL(n,R)
for
R\subset\mathbb{C}
a finitely generated
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra. Let
N
be a positive integer such that the eigenvalues of the monodromies at infinity of
\rho
are all
N
-roots of unity. Thanks to the Proposition
8.1
, there exists a maximal ideal
I\subset R
such that the reduction morphism
R\to R/\penalty I
is injective in restriction to the
N
-roots of unity. Then one can take for
X^{\prime}\to X
the finite étale cover corresponding to the kernel of the composed morphism
\pi_{1}(X)\to\GL(n,R)\to\GL(n,R/I)
.
∎

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
X
be a connected complex algebraic variety and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
a constructible subset containing only local systems with quasiunipotent local monodromy. Then there exists a finite étale cover
X^{\prime}\to X
corresponding to a finite quotient of the image of the monodromy representation of
\bigoplus_{V\in\Sigma}V

such that the image of
\Sigma
in
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X^{\prime})(\mathbb{C})
consists only of elements with unipotent (rather than quasiunipotent) local monodromy.

Proof.

It is sufficient to consider the case where
\Sigma
is an irreducible locally closed subset of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
. For every finite étale cover
X^{\prime}\to X
, consider the closed algebraic subset
\Sigma^{\prime}
of
\Sigma
consisting of the elements whose pull-back to
X^{\prime}
have unipotent (rather than quasiunipotent) local monodromy. It follows from the assumption and
Proposition

8.2
that the union of all those subsets is equal to
\Sigma
. It follows from Baire’s category theorem that there exists a finite étale cover
X^{\prime}\to X
such that
\Sigma^{\prime}
has non-empty interior in
\Sigma
(for the euclidean topology). Since
\Sigma
is irreducible and
\Sigma^{\prime}
is a closed algebraic subset of
\Sigma
, it follows that
\Sigma^{\prime}=\Sigma
.
∎

8.2.
Construction of the Deligne–Hitchin space

The construction here is largely based on Simpson’s description
[
109
]
; see
[
111
,
107
]
for some related discussion.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a connected log smooth algebraic space with
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D)
the stack of logarithmic
\lambda
-connections. Precisely, an
S
-point of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D)
consists of a triple
(\lambda,\bar{E},\nabla)
where
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}_{S}(S)
and
\bar{E}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}\times S}
-module on
\bar{X}_{S}:=\bar{X}\times S
equipped with a flat logarithmic
\lambda
-connection
\nabla
—that is, an operator
\nabla:E\to E\otimes\Omega_{\bar{X}_{S}/S}(\log D_{S})
satisfying
\nabla(fs)=\lambda s\otimes d_{\bar{X}}f+f\nabla s
and
\nabla^{2}=0
. As for the De Rham stack,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D)
is a countably finite type algebraic stack (see
5.4
for the definition of this notion, and
[
111
, §2]
for general references on representability). There is a natural morphism
\lambda:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D)\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
, as well as a
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D)
by scaling the connection which covers the scaling action on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

It will often be simpler to consider the framed space
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D,x)
for
x\in X
, whose
S
-points are
S
-points
(\lambda,\bar{E},\nabla)
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D)
together with an isomorphism
\phi:\bar{E}|_{x\times S}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\rk\bar{E}}
. Then
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D,x)
is a countably finite type algebraic space which comes with a morphism
\lambda:R_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D,x)\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
and a
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-action. Clearly, the rank
r
substack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D,r)
is identified with the quotient
[\mathbf{GL}_{r}\backslash R_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D,x,r)]
of the framed rank
r
space.

The symmetry offered by the
C^{\infty}
perspective motivates the construction of the Deligne–Hitchin space, so we describe it informally. An
S
-point of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{an}}
in particular gives a
\mathscr{A}_{\bar{X}_{S}}=C^{\infty}_{\bar{X}}\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module
\bar{\mathcal{E}}
on
\bar{X}_{S}^{\mathrm{an}}
together with a flat
\lambda\partial_{X^{\mathrm{an}}}+\bar{\partial}_{X^{\mathrm{an}}}
connection
\mathscr{D}=\nabla+\bar{\partial}_{\bar{E}}
on
\mathcal{E}:=\bar{\mathcal{E}}|_{X^{\mathrm{an}}}
, where
\bar{\partial}_{\bar{E}}
is the holomorphic structure on
\bar{E}
. Note that
\nabla=\mathscr{D}^{1,0}
and
\bar{\partial}_{\bar{E}}=\mathscr{D}^{0,1}
. These are the same types of operators that define a variation of mixed twistor structures, restricted to
\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\infty
. As realized by Simpson and Sabbah
[
112
,
98
]
, the eigenvalues of the residues of the
\lambda
-connection associated to a variation of mixed twistor structures behave in a regular way, although it depends on the parabolic structures as well. For
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
, define the bijection (see
[
98
]
)
\mathfrak{k}_{\lambda}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}
by

(8.2.1)

\mathfrak{k}_{\lambda}(a,\alpha)=(\mathfrak{p}_{\lambda}(a,\alpha),\mathfrak{e}_{\lambda}(a,\alpha))\hskip 36.135pt\begin{cases}\mathfrak{p}_{\lambda}(a,\alpha)&:=a+2\Re(\lambda\bar{\alpha})\\
\mathfrak{e}_{\lambda}(a,\alpha)&:=\alpha-a\lambda-\bar{\alpha}\lambda^{2}.\end{cases}

For any
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
the set of pairs
(a,\alpha)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}
where
\alpha
is an eigenvalue of the residue of the logarithmic extension occurring in the
a
th graded piece of the parabolic structure in the
\lambda
specialization is called the KMS-spectrum at
\lambda
. The KMS-spectrum at
\lambda
is then the image under
\mathfrak{k}_{\lambda}
of the KMS-spectrum of the
\lambda=0
specialization.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, denote by
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(\bar{X},D,x)\subset R_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D,x)
the closed subspace of
\lambda
-connections with residues contained in
\lambda\cdot(\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{Z}\cap(-1,0])\subset\mathbb{C}
. Let
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)
be the germ of an open neighborhood of
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(\bar{X},D,x)^{\mathrm{an}}
in
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D,x)^{\mathrm{an}}
. It is useful for example to keep in mind the open neighborhoods for which the
\lambda
-connection has residual eigenvalues in
\lambda\cdot B_{\epsilon}(\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{Z}\cap(-1,0])
, where for
\Xi\subset\mathbb{C}
we let
B_{\epsilon}(\Xi)
be the union of radius
\epsilon
balls centered at points of
\Xi
and
\epsilon<\frac{1}{2n}
is sufficiently small.

Let
(\bar{X}^{c},\bar{D}^{c})
be the complex conjugate variety. There is a natural holomorphic isomorphism
15
15

15

The construction is usually described via an isomorphism
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)|_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}\cong R_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)^{c}|_{\mathbf{G}_{m}^{c}}
given as
(\lambda,\mathcal{E},\mathscr{D},\phi)\mapsto(-\bar{\lambda}^{-1},(\mathcal{E}^{\vee})^{c},-\bar{\lambda}^{-1}(\mathscr{D}^{0,1\vee})^{c}+\bar{\lambda}^{-1}(\mathscr{D}^{1,0\vee})^{c})
, but as
(\lambda,\mathcal{E},\mathscr{D})\mapsto(-\bar{\lambda},(\mathcal{E}^{\vee})^{c},-(\mathscr{D}^{0,1\vee})^{c}+(\mathscr{D}^{1,0\vee})^{c})
gives an identification
R^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X}^{c},D^{c},x)\xrightarrow{\cong}R^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)^{c}
these give the same space. Note however with this description we would then take the residual eigenvalues to lie in a neighborhood of
\lambda\cdot\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,1)
in the target.
