R_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)|_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}\cong R_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X}^{c},D^{c},x)|_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
covering the involution
\lambda\mapsto\lambda^{-1}
and locally equivariant with respect to the
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-action after twisting by
t\mapsto t^{-1}
on the target which is described as follows. For an
S
-point of
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)|_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
given by
(\lambda,\bar{E},\nabla,\phi)=(\lambda,\bar{\mathcal{E}},\mathscr{D},\phi)
, the rescaled framed connection
(\bar{\mathcal{E}},\lambda^{-1}\mathscr{D}^{1,0}+\mathscr{D}^{0,1},\phi)
is a family of flat connections whose residues have eigenvalues contained in
(-1+\epsilon,\epsilon]+i\mathbb{R}
. It follows that the extension is the Deligne extension of the underlying family of framed local systems
S\to R_{B}(X,x)^{\mathrm{an}}
, which works in families since the eigenvalues of the local monodromy admit a continuous logarithm. We can form the antiholomorphic Deligne extension
(\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime},\mathscr{D}^{0,1}+\lambda^{-1}\mathscr{D}^{1,0},\phi)
with eigenvalues in
(-1+\epsilon,\epsilon]+i\mathbb{R}
, and rescaling
(\lambda^{-1},\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime},\lambda^{-1}\mathscr{D},\phi)
provides the required
S
-point of
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X}^{c},D^{c},x)|_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
. Gluing
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)
to
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X}^{c},D^{c},x)
via this identification we obtain a countably finite type complex analytic space
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)
with an analytic map
\pi:R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
, restricting to the corresponding structure on the two
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}
spaces. We similarly construct
\mathcal{M}_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D)
by gluing, and identify
\mathcal{M}_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D)
with the quotient of
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)
by
\mathbf{GL}_{r}(\mathbb{C})
. The derivation
\Theta
associated to the
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-action glues to give a natural global derivation
\Theta
compatibly on
\mathcal{M}_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D)
and
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)
. In fact, the
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-action on
R_{DH}(\bar{X},D,x)
stabilizes
R^{\mathrm{qu}|n}_{DH}(\bar{X},D,x)
, so there is a well-defined
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-action on
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)
as a germ of an analytic space containing
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(\bar{X},D,x)
as a closed subspace.

The moduli functor of
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x,r)
is described as follows. For an analytic space
S
, an
S
-point of
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x,r)
yields a tuple
(\pi,\mathcal{E},\bar{E},\bar{E}^{\prime},\mathscr{D},\phi)
where:

(1)

\pi:S\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
is a morphism;

(2)

\mathcal{E}
is a
C^{\infty}_{X}\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module on
X_{S}
;

(3)

\mathscr{D}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega_{X_{S}/S}(1)
is a flat
x\partial_{X_{S}/S}+y\bar{\partial}_{X_{S}/S}
connection, where
x,y
are fixed sections of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
vanishing at
0,\infty
. We require that:

(a)

Using the trivialization
y
of
\pi^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
on
S_{0}:=\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus 0)
,
(\mathcal{E},\mathscr{D}^{0,1})
is a rank
r
locally free holomorphic vector bundle on
X_{S_{0}}
and
\bar{E}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}_{S}}
-module extension (meaning it comes equipped with an isomorphism
j_{S_{0}}^{*}\bar{E}\to(\mathcal{E},\mathscr{D}^{0,1})
), where
j_{S_{0}}:X_{S_{0}}\to\bar{X}_{S_{0}}
is the inclusion) to which
\mathscr{D}^{1,0}
extends as a flat logarithmic connection.

(b)

Using the trivialization
x
of
\pi^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
on
S_{\infty}:=\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\infty)
,
(\mathcal{E},\mathscr{D}^{1,0})
is a rank
r
locally free holomorphic vector bundle on
X^{c}_{S_{0}}
and
\bar{E}^{\prime}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{X}^{c}_{S}}
-module extension to which
\mathscr{D}^{0,1}
extends as a flat logarithmic connection.

(4)

\phi:\mathcal{E}|_{x\times S}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{O}_{S}^{r}
is a framing.

Moreover, any such
(\pi,\mathcal{E},\bar{E},\bar{E}^{\prime},\mathscr{D},\phi)
on
S
arises from a morphism
S\to R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x,r)
after shrinking to a sufficiently small neighborhood of the closed analytic space
S^{\mathrm{qu}|n}\subset S
where the residual eigenvalues of
(\bar{E},\mathscr{D}^{1,0})
(resp.
(\bar{E}^{\prime},\mathscr{D}^{0,1})
) are contained in
\lambda\cdot(\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{Z}\cap(-1,0])
(resp.
-\lambda^{-1}\cdot(\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{Z}\cap(-1,0])
).

Remark 8.4
.

Note that
S
-points of
\mathcal{M}_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D)
are tuples
(\pi,\mathcal{E},\bar{E},\bar{E}^{\prime},\mathscr{D})
that are trivializable over
x
, which is a nontrivial condition. In fact, we could have twisted the above gluing to produce a version of
R_{DH}
and
\mathcal{M}_{DH}
where
\mathcal{E}|_{x\times S}\cong\pi^{*}F
where
F
is a fixed locally free sheaf on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Remark 8.5
.

The global construction of the Deligne–Hitchin space is complicated by both the monodromy of the residual eigenvalues if they are allowed to roam freely, and “resonant” phenomena when they differ by integers (as in
Proposition

7.4
). This is more seriously contended with in recent work of Simpson
[
111
,
107
]
, although there the “generic” case is assumed. By the results of
Section

5
, bialgebraic substacks of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
are determined by their germ around the quasiunipotent local monodromy locus, and neither issue arises in the germ of the Deligne–Hitchin space around this locus.

The following is straightforward; we leave the proof to the reader.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
f:(\bar{X},D)\to(\bar{Y},E)
be a morphism of log smooth pairs compatible with a choice of basepoints. Then there are pullback morphisms
f^{*}:\mathcal{M}_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{Y},E)\to\mathcal{M}_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D)
and
f^{*}:R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{Y},E,y)\to R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)
of analytic stacks (up to shrinking).

8.3.
Preferred sections and twistor germs

Variations of twistor structures yield sections of the Deligne–Hitchin space:

Lemma 8.7
.

To every
\mathbb{C}
-AVMTS
(\mathcal{E},W_{\bullet}\mathcal{E},\mathscr{D})
with quasiunipotent local monodromy on
X
, there is a functorially associated filtered logarithmic locally free extension
(\mathcal{E},W_{\bullet}\mathcal{E},\bar{E},\bar{E}^{\prime},\mathscr{D})
on
\bar{X}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
in the above sense whose residues have eigenvalues in
\lambda\cdot(\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{Z}\cap(-1,0])
.

Proof.

By the above discussion, the family of flat connections
\lambda^{-1}\mathscr{D}^{1,0}
on
(\mathcal{E},\mathscr{D}^{0,1})
has constant residual eigenvalues in
(-1,0]
and the Deligne extension provides such extensions
\bar{E},\bar{E}^{\prime}
outside of the codimension 2 subset
D\times 0\cup D\times\infty
in
\bar{X}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
. The existence of the claimed extensions
\bar{E},\bar{E}^{\prime}
therefore reduces to the local existence, which is by assumption the case for a mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module (see
[
86
, §9]
).
∎

Corollary 8.8
.

Let
A
an artinian
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MTS-algebra and
\mathscr{E}=(\mathcal{E},W_{\bullet}\mathcal{E},\mathscr{D})
an
A
-AVMTS on
X
with quasiunipotent local monodromy whose eigenvalues have order dividing
n
. Then
\mathscr{E}
is pulled back via a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-morphism
\underline{\mathrm{Spec}}\,A\to\mathcal{M}_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D)
.
If in addition there is a framing
\phi\colon\mathscr{E}_{x}\xrightarrow{\cong}A^{r}
, then
(\mathscr{E},\phi)
is pulled back via a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-morphism
\underline{\mathrm{Spec}}\,A\to R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)
.

Proof.

We can interpret the extension
(\mathcal{E},W_{\bullet}\mathcal{E},\bar{E},\bar{E}^{\prime},\mathscr{D})
on
\bar{X}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
given by applying the lemma to the underlying
\mathbb{C}
-AVMTS together with its action by the sheaf of rings
A
as such a tuple on
\bar{X}_{\underline{\mathrm{Spec}}\,A}
.
∎

Definition 8.9
.

A
quasiunipotent preferred section
is a section of
\mathcal{M}_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D)\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
or
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
as in
Corollary

8.8
resulting from a weight 0
\mathbb{C}
-VTS (that is, a tame purely imaginary harmonic bundle) with quasiunipotent local monodromy.

Note the image of a quasiunipotent preferred section is contained in
\mathcal{M}_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(X)
or
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(\bar{X},D,x)
. We now specialize to the framed spaces, but there are versions of the following discussion for
\mathcal{M}_{DH}
, provided we replace all formal isomorphism claims with a miniversality claim.

Corollary 8.10
.

For any
(V,\phi)\in R_{B}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(X,x)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}
we have:

(1)

The associated framed Deligne extension
(V_{DR},\phi)\in R_{DR}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})
(with residual eigenvalues in
(-1,0]
).

(2)

A framed quasiunipotent preferred section
s_{(V,\phi)}
of
\pi\colon R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
specializing to
(V_{DR},\phi)
at
\lambda=x/y=1
.

(3)

A uniquely determined pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MTS-algebra
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}
and a morphism

\hat{s}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}\colon\underline{\mathrm{Spec}}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}\to R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)

fitting into a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Spec}}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{(V,\phi)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{s}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

Moreover, the specialization to
\lambda=1
of
\hat{s}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}
is a formal isomorphism to the formal completion of
R^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}_{DR}(\bar{X},D,x)
at
(V_{DR},\phi)
.

Proof.

By
Theorem

4.2
and
Proposition

7.4
.
∎

Lemma 8.11
.

In the notation of the previous corollary, for any
(V,\phi)\in R_{B}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(X,x)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}
, the morphism

(8.3.1)

\hat{s}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}\colon\underline{\mathrm{Spec}}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}\to R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)

is a formal isomorphism to the formal completion of
R^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}_{B}(\bar{X},D,x)
along
s_{(V,\phi)}
over
\mathbf{G}_{m}
. If
X
is a curve and
n=1
, the same is true over all of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Proof.

We first reduce to the claim assuming
X
is a curve. If
i:C\to X
is a morphism from a smooth affine curve such that
i_{*}:\pi_{1}(C,c)\to\pi_{1}(X,x)
is surjective (which exists by
Lemma

4.9
), we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\Spec}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\Spec}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(C,c),(i^{*}V,i^{*}\phi)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{C},D_{C},c)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{s}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{s}_{R_{B}(C,c),(i^{*}V,i^{*}\phi)}}

The pullback
i^{*}
is a closed immersion in the Betti realization, hence also in the De Rham realization (locally around the chosen component of the quasiunipotent locus). By
Lemma

4.35
, the left morphism is a closed immersion, while by the
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-action on
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)|_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
the right morphism is a closed immersion over
\mathbf{G}_{m}
. Thus, if the bottom morphism is an isomorphism on completions over
\mathbf{G}_{m}
, the top morphism is a closed immersion on completions over
\mathbf{G}_{m}
. For any
t\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
, taking
V^{\prime}=tV
we may consider a framed quasiunipotent preferred section
s^{\prime}:=s_{(V^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})}
such that
t^{-1}s(t)=s^{\prime}(1)
, where
s=s_{(V,\phi)}
. It then follows by acting by
t
that there is a surjection
of the
\lambda=1
specialization of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})}
onto the
\lambda=t
specialization
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}
, so in particular we have an inequality of the dimensions of the graded pieces with respect to the maximal ideal. By
Lemma

4.35
the same is true for the
\lambda=1
specializations of both, and swapping
(V,\phi)
and
(V^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
we find the top morphism is an isomorphism on completions for all
\lambda\neq 0,\infty
.

It remains therefore to take
X
to be a smooth affine curve. In this case both
\underline{\mathrm{Spec}}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}
and
R^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}_{DH}(X,x)|_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
are smooth over
\mathbf{G}_{m}
, so for the claim over
\mathbf{G}_{m}
it is enough to show the map (
8.3.1
) is an isomorphism to first order in the fiber direction at every
\lambda\in\mathbf{G}_{m}
. This follows from the fact that the specialization to
\lambda\in\mathbf{G}_{m}
of the (derived) pushforward of a tame nilpotent mixed twistor module (specifically,
\End(V)
) computes the cohomology of the De Rham complex of the associated
\lambda
-connection
[
86
, Proposition 14.1.20]
, which in turn computes the deformation space of the logarithmic connection by
Proposition

7.4
.

For
\lambda=0
(and
\lambda=\infty
is the same, after passing to the conjugate), we have:

Lemma 8.12
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a log smooth projective curve with
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
, let
j:X\to\bar{X}
be the inclusion, and let
E
be a smooth tame nilpotent (algebraic) pure twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module on
X
with trivial parabolic structure. Then the cohomology of
\mathrm{sp}_{0}\mathrm{pt}_{*}j_{*}E
is canonically isomorphic to the cohomology of the log de Rham complex of the
\lambda=0
specialization of the logarithmic extension
(\bar{E},\theta)
from above.

Proof.

We thank Claude Sabbah for communicating the following argument. Let
x
be a local defining equation for
D
. Let
\mathcal{M}[*D]
(see
[
86
, §3.1.2]
) be the
\mathscr{R}_{X}
-module underlying
j_{*}E
away from
\lambda=\infty
. Then locally
\mathcal{M}[*D]
has a
V
-filtration for which
\lambda\partial_{x}:\operatorname{gr}^{V}_{b}\mathcal{M}[*D]\to\operatorname{gr}^{V}_{b+1}\mathcal{M}[*D]
is an isomorphism for
b>-1
and
x:V_{0}\mathcal{M}[*D]\to V_{-1}\mathcal{M}[*D]
is an isomorphism. We therefore have quasi-isomorphisms of two-term complexes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{-1}\mathcal{M}[*D]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{-1}\mathcal{M}[*D]\otimes\omega_{\bar{X}}(D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{-1}\mathcal{M}[*D]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{0}\mathcal{M}[*D]\otimes\omega_{\bar{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}[*D]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}[*D]\otimes\omega_{\bar{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda\partial+\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda\partial+\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x\otimes x^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda\partial+\theta}

The bottom row is the De Rham complex of
\mathcal{M}[*D]
and the top row specializes to the log Higgs complex of
V_{-1}\mathcal{M}[*D]
, which in this case is
\bar{E}
. Since the terms of the complexes are flat over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
and the cohomology of the push-forward to the point is locally free, its formation commutes with specialization.
∎

We now claim that (
8.3.1
) is an isomorphism on tangent spaces at
\lambda=0
. Since
V
has unipotent local monodromy, the same is true for
\End(V)
. Thus taking endomorphism objects commutes with the Deligne extension for
V
, and the same is true for the extensions in the sense of
Lemma

8.7
. The tangent space of the
\lambda=0
fiber of
\mathcal{M}_{DH}(\bar{X},D)
is the first cohomology of the log De Rham complex of
(\overline{\End(V)}|_{0},\ad\theta)
, which is by the lemma identified with the relative tangent space of
\underline{\Spec}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{M}_{B}(X),V}
at
\lambda=0
. The same is then true for the framed space, so (
8.3.1
) is an isomorphism on relative tangent spaces at
\lambda=0
, and because of the presence of the section this implies the map on tangent spaces is an isomorphism.

Finally, any local morphism
A\to B
of complete noetherian local rings which is an isomorphism on tangent spaces and for which
B
is smooth is an isomorphism, and this completes the proof.
∎

Remark 8.13
.

We could have directly used the formalism of
Section

4
to show that for
\lambda\in\mathbf{G}_{m}
the deformation theory of a
\lambda
-connection with residual eigenvalues in
\lambda\cdot\frac{1}{n}(-1,0]
admits an enhancement by twistor structures. This follows from
[
86
, Proposition 14.1.20]
, which implies that the second part of
Theorem

4.28
holds for
\lambda\in\mathbf{G}_{m}
. For
\lambda=0,\infty
, our version of
R^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}_{DH}(\bar{X},D,x)
is not the correct moduli space since it ignores parabolic structures.
If one keeps track of this, then the same argument can likely be made to work using that the
\lambda=0
specialization of the push-forward of the endomorphism object computes the cohomology of the log De Rham complex.

For any
(V,\phi)\in R_{B}(X,x)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss},\mathrm{qu}}
, let
i\colon C\to X
be a Lefschetz curve (see
Lemma

4.9
)and
p\colon C^{\prime}\to C
a finite étale cover for which
V^{\prime}:=f^{*}V
has unipotent local monodromy, where
f=i\circ p
. Then
f^{*}\colon\underline{\Spec}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}\to\underline{\Spec}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(C^{\prime},c^{\prime}),(V^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})}
is a closed immersion of formal spaces by
Lemma

4.62
and
Lemma

4.35
.

Corollary 8.14
.

The canonical derivation
\Theta
lifts functorially to each
\underline{\Spec}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}
for
(V,\phi)\in R_{B}(X,x)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{qu},\mathrm{ss}}
.

Proof.

The derivation
\Theta
lifts canonically over
\mathbf{G}_{m}
so we just need to check it extends to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
and this holds on
\underline{\Spec}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(C^{\prime},c^{\prime}),(V^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})}
by
Lemma

8.11
.
∎

For a subspace
Z\subset R_{B}(X,x)
which is formally twistor at
(V,\phi)\in Z^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}
we define
\hat{s}_{Z,(V,\phi)}
as the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\Spec}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{Z,(V,\phi)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\Spec}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{s}_{Z,(V,\phi)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{s}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}}

Since
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{Z,(V,\phi)}
is a quotient pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MTS-algebra of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}
, it follows from
Lemma

8.11
that
\hat{s}_{Z,(V,\phi)}
is a closed immersion, as this is true after post-composition with
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)\xrightarrow{i^{*}}R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{C},E,c^{\prime})\xrightarrow{p^{*}}R_{DH}^{\mathrm{unip},\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{C}^{\prime},E^{\prime},c^{\prime})
.

We denote by
Z_{\mathrm{Hod}}(V,\phi)
(resp.
Z_{\overline{\mathrm{Hod}}}(V,\phi)
) the Zariski closure of the image of
\hat{s}_{Z,(V,\phi)}|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
(resp.
\hat{s}_{Z,(V,\phi)}|_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus 0}
) in
R_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D,x)
(resp.
R_{\overline{\mathrm{Hod}}}(\bar{X}^{c},D^{c},x)
).

Definition 8.15
.

In the above situation, we say
\hat{s}_{Z,(V,\phi)}
is
algebraic
over
\mathbf{G}_{m}
if:

(1)

\hat{s}_{Z,(V,\phi)}|_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
induces an isomorphism onto the completion of
Z_{\mathrm{Hod}}(V,\phi)|_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
along the image of
s_{(V,\phi)}
.

(2)

The same is true for
\hat{s}_{Z,(V,\phi)}|_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
and
Z_{\overline{\mathrm{Hod}}}(V,\phi)|_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
.

Note that if
\hat{s}_{Z,(V,\phi)}
is algebraic, then the algebraic germs
Z_{\mathrm{Hod}|\mathbf{G}_{m}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(V,\phi):=Z_{\mathrm{Hod}|\mathbf{G}_{m}}(V,\phi)\cap R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)
and
Z_{\overline{\mathrm{Hod}}|\mathbf{G}_{m}}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(V,\phi)
(defined similarly) are identified as analytic germs via the gluing.

Corollary 8.16
.

Let
Z\subset R_{B}(X,x)
a subspace which is formally twistor at
(V,\phi)\in Z^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}
such that
\hat{s}_{Z,(V,\phi)}
is algebraic. Then:

(1)

\hat{s}_{Z,(V,\phi)}|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
induces an isomorphism onto the the completion of
Z_{\mathrm{Hod}}(V,\phi)
along the image of
s_{(V,\phi)}|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
. The same is true for
Z_{\overline{\mathrm{Hod}}}(V,\phi)
over
\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus 0
.

(2)

There is a germ of a closed analytic subspace
Z_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(V,\phi)\subset R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)
for which
\hat{s}_{Z,(V,\phi)}
induces an isomorphism onto the completion of
Z_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(V,\phi)
along the image of
s_{(V,\phi)}
.

Proof.

The second assertion is a direct consequence of the first.
For simplicity write
W=Z_{\mathrm{Hod}}(V,\phi)
with completion
\hat{W}
along the image of
s_{(V,\phi)}|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
. First observe that the morphism
\mathcal{O}_{\hat{W}}\to\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{Z,(V,\phi)}
is injective. Indeed, by
Lemma

4.35
, the localization
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{Z,(V,\phi)}\to\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{Z,(V,\phi)}[\lambda^{-1}]
is injective, but since
\mathcal{O}_{\hat{W}}\to\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{Z,(V,\phi)}
is an isomorphism over
\mathbf{G}_{m}
by assumption, its ideal is exactly the kernel of
\mathcal{O}_{\hat{W}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\hat{W}}[\lambda^{-1}]
. By noetherianity and flatness of completion, this ideal is the completion of the corresponding ideal in
\mathcal{O}_{W}
, hence must vanish be zero since
W
is the Zariski closure.

We therefore need only show the surjectivity. As in
Lemma

8.11
, by Lefschetz we may reduce to the case
X
is an affine curve, since this induces a closed immersion of formal twistor spaces. By
Proposition

8.2
there is a finite étale cover
p:X^{\prime}\to X
such that
p^{*}V
has unipotent local monodromy. Since
p^{*}:R_{B}(X,x)\to R_{B}(X^{\prime},x^{\prime})
is immersive by
Lemma

4.62
, by the same argument we reduce to the case that
V
has unipotent local monodromy, and further that
Z=R_{B}(X,x)
, which is
Lemma

8.11
.
∎

Corollary 8.17
.

With the setup of the previous corollary, the formal deformation theory of
s_{(V,\phi)}
as a section of
Z_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(V,\phi)\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
is naturally equivalent to the formal deformation theory of the zero section of
\underline{\Spec}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{Z,(V,\phi)}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

8.4.
Moduli of sections

Let
\operatorname{Sect}(R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)/\mathbb{P}^{1})
denote the space of sections of the morphism
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
, which is an open subspace of the Douady space and in particular has the structure of a second countable analytic space. Since
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)
is only defined as a germ of a neighborhood of
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(\bar{X},D,x)
, the space
\operatorname{Sect}(R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)/\mathbb{P}^{1})
is well-defined as a germ of a neighborhood of the locus of sections contained in
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)
. For any
\lambda\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
there is an evaluation morphism

\operatorname{ev}_{\lambda}:\operatorname{Sect}(R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)/\mathbb{P}^{1})\to R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)_{\lambda}

which is compatible with the
\mathbf{GL}_{r}
-action (with
r
interpreted as the locally constant rank function). Note also that for any morphism
f:(\bar{X},D)\to(\bar{Y},E)
of log smooth projective pairs respecting the choice of basepoints, there is an analytic map

(8.4.1)

f^{*}:\operatorname{Sect}(R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{Y},E,y)/\mathbb{P}^{1})\to\operatorname{Sect}(R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)/\mathbb{P}^{1})

which is also compatible with the
\mathbf{GL}_{r}
-action.

We denote by
\operatorname{PrefSect}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}_{DH}(\bar{X},D,x)\subset\operatorname{Sect}(R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)/\mathbb{P}^{1})
the set of quasiunipotent preferred sections with the subspace topology, which is well-defined as they are contained in
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X},D,x)
. Pullback as in (
8.4.1
) maps preferred sections to preferred sections. The evaluation at
\lambda=1
restricts to a bijection

(8.4.2)

\operatorname{ev}_{1}:\operatorname{PrefSect}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}_{DH}(\bar{X},D,x)\to R_{DR}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})^{ss}.

There is a natural action of
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
on
\operatorname{PrefSect}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}_{DH}(\bar{X},D,x)
coming from the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-action on
R_{DR}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}
.

As in
Section

7.4
, for a fixed a vector space
(V_{0},h_{0})
with hermitian metric of the relevant rank, we define
\operatorname{PrefSect}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}_{DH}(\bar{X},D,x)^{(V_{0},h_{0})}\subset\operatorname{PrefSect}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}_{DH}(\bar{X},D,x)
to be the subspace of preferred sections passing through
R_{DR}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(\bar{X},D,x)(\mathbb{C})^{(V_{0},h_{0})}
over
\lambda=1
, or equivalently which are framed compatibly with the metric for all
\lambda
.

Proposition 8.18
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a log smooth projective curve. Then the evaluation

\operatorname{ev}_{1}:\operatorname{PrefSect}^{\mathrm{unip}}_{DH}(\bar{X},D,x)^{(V_{0},h_{0})}\to R_{DR}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X,x)(\mathbb{C})^{(V_{0},h_{0})}

is a homeomorphism.

Proof.

The evaluation map is continuous, so we need only show the inverse is continuous. As in the proof of
Corollary

7.17
, it suffices to show any convergent sequence in the target has a subsequence which converges on the source to the point corresponding to the limit.

The claim is essentially a restatement of
Proposition

7.15
. Indeed,
[
114
, Theorem 5.12]
implies that a metric on
R^{\mathrm{nilp}}_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\bar{X},D,x)
is given by

\displaystyle d((\lambda_{1},\bar{E}_{1},\nabla_{1},\phi_{1}),

\displaystyle(\lambda_{2},\bar{E}_{2},\nabla_{2},\phi_{2}))

\displaystyle=\inf_{\psi}|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|^{2}+|\phi_{1}-\psi_{*}\phi_{2}|^{2}+\|\nabla_{1}-\psi_{*}\nabla_{2}\|^{2}+\|\bar{\partial}_{\bar{E}_{1}}-\psi_{*}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{E}_{1}}\|^{2}

where the infimum is over all
C^{\infty}
isomorphisms
\psi:C^{\infty}\otimes\bar{E}_{1}\to C^{\infty}\otimes\bar{E}_{2}
and the norms are operator norms as operators
L_{1}^{q}(\bar{E}_{1})\to L^{q}(\bar{E}_{1}\otimes\omega_{\bar{X}}(D))
. The proof of
Proposition

7.15
implies if
(V_{i},\phi_{i})
converges to
(V_{\infty},\phi_{\infty})
and has framings which are compatible with the metric, then after passing to a subsequence, the associated preferred sections converge uniformly as maps
\mathbb{P}^{1}\to R_{DH}^{\mathrm{unip}}(\bar{X},D,x)
to the section corresponding to
(V_{\infty},\phi_{\infty})
.
∎

Corollary 8.19
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a connected log smooth proper algebraic space. For any
(V,\phi)\in R_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X,x)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}
, there is a continuous map
\mathbb{R}_{>0}\to\operatorname{PrefSect}^{\mathrm{unip}}_{DH}(\bar{X},D,x)
such that the composition with

\operatorname{PrefSect}^{\mathrm{unip}}_{DH}(\bar{X},D,x)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{ev}_{1}}R_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X,x)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}\to M^{\mathrm{unip}}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})

is the continuous map
\mathbb{R}_{>0}\to M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)(\mathbb{C})
given by the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbit of
V
.

Proof.

Pullback to a Lefschetz curve
f:C\to X
induces a closed immersion on section spaces, so we may assume
X
is a curve. By
Corollary

7.18
,
R^{\mathrm{unip}}_{B}(X,x)^{(V_{0},h_{0})}\to M^{\mathrm{unip}}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is proper, and in corestriction to the locally closed stratification of
M^{\mathrm{unip}}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
by Jordan–Hölder type it is a locally trivial fiber bundle (by the existence of a slice
[
89
, Theorem 2.1]
). The
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbit of
V
in
M_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is contained in one of these strata, hence a lift to
R^{\mathrm{unip}}_{B}(X,x)^{(V_{0},h_{0})}
exists through any point above
V
, and by
Proposition

8.18
this yields a lift to
\operatorname{PrefSect}^{\mathrm{unip}}_{DH}(\bar{X},D,x)^{(V_{0},h_{0})}
. Acting by
\mathbf{GL}_{r}(\mathbb{C})
, we obtain the claim for any framing.
∎

8.5.
Hodge substacks

Definition 8.20
.

Let
X
be a connected smooth algebraic space with a log smooth compactification
(\bar{X},D)
. A locally closed substack
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
is
Hodge
at
V\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{qu},\mathrm{ss}}
if the following conditions are satisfied. Let
R\mathcal{Z}\subset R_{B}(X,x)
be the base-change to the framed space and let
\phi
be a framing of
V
. Then we have:

(1)

R\mathcal{Z}
is formally twistor at
(V,\phi)
, and formally Hodge if
V
underlies a
\mathbb{C}-\mathrm{VHS}
.

(2)

The formal twistor subspace
\underline{\Spec}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R\mathcal{Z},(V,\phi)}\subset\underline{\Spec}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{B}(X,x),(V,\phi)}
is tangent to
\Theta
.

(3)

The formal twistor germ
\hat{s}_{R\mathcal{Z},(V,\phi)}
is algebraic.

A locally closed substack
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
is a
Hodge substack
if it is closed under semisimplification and Hodge at every
V\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{qu},\mathrm{ss}}
.

If
X
is a connected normal algebraic space we define the corresponding notion for
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
if the restriction
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(U)
is Hodge for any smooth affine
U\subset X
.

The definition is easily seen to not depend on the log smooth compactification. Note that all of the conditions only involve the Deligne–Hitchin space over
\mathbf{G}_{m}
, where its functorial behavior is controlled by the Betti stack. Nonetheless, each of the three structures extend to the full Deligne–Hitchin space, and in particular by
Corollary

8.16
the algebraic germ
Z_{\mathrm{Hod}}(V,\phi)
(resp.
Z_{\overline{\mathrm{Hod}}}(V,\phi)
) will extend over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
(resp.
\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus 0
) and agree with the twistor germ
\hat{s}_{R\mathcal{Z},(V,\phi)}
.

The following functorial properties follow from the definitions and what we’ve proven.

Proposition 8.21
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space.

(1)

For any
r
,
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X,r)
and
\{\mathrm{triv}_{r}\}
are Hodge substacks of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
.

(2)

Assume
X
is a curve. For any
V\in\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{qu},\mathrm{ss}}
, the fixed local monodromy leaf
FM(V)\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
is Hodge at
V
. For any
V\in\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{qu},\mathrm{ss}}
, the fixed residual eigenvalues leaf
FE(V)\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
is a closed Hodge substack.

(3)

Intersections and reductions of Hodge substacks are Hodge substacks. Irreducible components of reduced Hodge substacks are Hodge substacks. Finite unions of closed Hodge substacks are Hodge substacks.

(4)

Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of connected normal algebraic spaces and
f^{*}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
the pullback morphism. Then:

(a)

For any Hodge substack
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
,
(f^{*})^{-1}(\mathcal{Z})\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)
is a Hodge substack.

(b)

For any Hodge substack
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)
,
f^{*}\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
is a Hodge substack provided
f
is dominant or a Lefschetz curve (see
Definition

4.8
).

(5)

Inverse images of Hodge substacks under the direct sum and tensor product morphisms
\oplus,\otimes:\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)^{2}\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
are Hodge substacks
16
16

With the obvious definition of Hodge substack of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)^{2}
.
.

Proof.

.
For

. For the rest, the

The following will be essential, especially

Theorem 8.22
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
be a Hodge substack, and
Z\subset M_{B}(x)
the image in the good moduli space. Then for any
n
, any irreducible component
Z_{0}
of

satisfies the following property. For any

, a germ of the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbit of
V
around

Z_{0}
.

Proof.

By Lefschetz (see

) we may assume

(\bar{X},D)
. We may also replace
\mathcal{Z}
with an irreducible component of the reduction of

Z
is irreducible. We will need to use the following:

Lemma 8.23
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
be a locally closed substack which is Hodge at each of its semisimple points. Then the singular locus
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{sing}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
is also Hodge at each of its semisimple points.

Proof.

The only condition to check is the formally twistor condition (the proof of the formally Hodge condition will be the same), as then the other conditions in
Definition

8.20
are clear, since the algebraic germ will be the singular locus of the algebraic germ of
\mathcal{Z}
, which is clearly
\Theta
-tangent. Let
R\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{sing}}
be the base-change to

(and likewise for
R\mathcal{Z}
); we have
(R\mathcal{Z})^{\mathrm{sing}}=R\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{sing}}
. For a point
(V,\phi)\in R\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}
, let
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R}
be the deformation pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-algebra of
R_{B}(X,x)
at

and

be the ideal of

.
By

4.14
(and the argument of
[

, Lemma 1.6]
),
\operatorname{Der}_{\mathbf{T}(0)}(\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R},\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R})^{\wedge}
has a natural pro-
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R}
-MS-module structure: there is a natural equivariant structure and an equivariant filtration by
\operatorname{Der}_{\mathbf{T}(0)}(\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R},\mathfrak{m}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R}}^{\ell})^{\wedge}
whose graded pieces
\Hom_{\mathbf{T}(0)}(\mathfrak{m}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R}}^{2},\mathfrak{m}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R}}^{\ell}/\mathfrak{m}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R}}^{\ell+1})
are twistor structures. Since

is smooth, this puts a natural pro-
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R}
-MS-module structure on
\hat{\Omega}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R}/\mathbf{T}(0)}
, and the canonical morphism

I/I^{2}\to\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R\mathcal{Z}}\otimes_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R}}\hat{\Omega}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R}/\mathbf{T}(0)}

is a morphism of pro-
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R}
-MS-modules. The singular locus of
\mathcal{Z}
is cut out by the minors of this morphism, which generate a
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MS-ideal.
∎

By
Proposition

8.3
, there is a finite étale cover
p:X^{\prime}\to X

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{D}}

Indeed, the reduced intersections

It suffices to prove the statement of the theorem for each

Claim
.

Suppose
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)

Proof.

Let
V\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}
and equip it with a framing
\phi

(V^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}):=p^{*}(V,\phi)

p^{*}:(R\mathcal{Z},(V,\phi))\to(R_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X,x),(V^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}))

p^{*}:R\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}_{DH}(V,\phi)\to R_{DH}^{\mathrm{unip},\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime},x^{\prime})(V^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),

where we recall that the source (resp. target) is the germ of the Zariski closure of
\hat{s}_{R\mathcal{Z},(V,\phi)}
(resp.
\hat{s}_{R_{B}(X^{\prime},x^{\prime}),(V^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})}

According to

, there is a continuous family
(\boldsymbol{s}^{\prime}_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}}
of preferred sections of

\mathbb{R}_{>0}

. These sections satisfy the following properties:

(1)

Since
\underline{\Spec}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{R\mathcal{Z},(V,\phi)}
is
\Theta
-tangent,
\boldsymbol{s}^{\prime}_{t}(0),\boldsymbol{s}^{\prime}_{t}(\infty)\in p^{*}R\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}_{DH}(V,\phi)
for

(2)

By

(see also the discussion in

RFM_{DH}^{\mathrm{unip},\mathrm{loc}}(V^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})

The space of sections satisfying these two conditions is an analytic subspace of
\operatorname{Sect}(R_{DH}^{\mathrm{unip},\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime},x^{\prime})/\mathbb{P}^{1})

is a continuous family of sections, so it follows that there is an analytic family of sections
\sigma^{\prime}_{a}
over a connected analytic germ

which satisfies these two conditions and such that there is a continuous map
\gamma:((1-\epsilon,1+\epsilon),1)\to(A,a_{0})
for

.

Sub-Claim
.

The family

is contained in
p^{*}R\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}_{DH}(V,\phi)
.

Proof.

Denote by
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{R}^{\prime}},\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{F}}
the pro-
\mathbf{T}(0)
-MTS-algebras of
R_{B}(X^{\prime},x^{\prime}),RFM(V^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
at
(V^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
, and
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{Z}}
that of
R\mathcal{Z}
at
(V,\phi)
. Let
\mathscr{R}^{\prime},\mathscr{F},\mathscr{Z}
be the associated formal spaces obtained by applying
\underline{\Spec}\,
, with
\mathscr{Z}
embedded in
\mathscr{R}^{\prime}
via
p^{*}
. By

8.17
, the deformation theory of
\sigma_{a_{0}}
in
R^{\mathrm{unip},\mathrm{loc}}_{DH}(\bar{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime},x^{\prime})
is equivalent to the deformation theory of the zero section in
\mathscr{R}^{\prime}
. The relative obstruction space of
\mathscr{Z}
in
\mathscr{F}
is given by
(I_{\mathscr{Z}/\mathscr{F}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{F}}I_{\mathscr{Z}/\mathscr{F}})^{\vee}
, where

is the ideal of
\mathcal{Z}
in
\mathcal{F}
, and we have a commutative diagram with exact rows and columns

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\mathscr{F}/\mathscr{R}^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{R}^{\prime}}I_{\mathscr{F}/\mathscr{R}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\mathscr{F}/\mathscr{R}^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{R}^{\prime}}I_{\mathscr{F}/\mathscr{R}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\mathscr{Z}/\mathscr{R}^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{R}^{\prime}}I_{\mathscr{Z}/\mathscr{R}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{R}^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{R}^{\prime}}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{Z}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{Z}}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\mathscr{Z}/\mathscr{F}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{F}}I_{\mathscr{Z}/\mathscr{F}}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{F}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{F}}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{Z}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{Z}}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

where the injection comes from the smoothness of

and

. By a diagram chase, we obtain exactness including the dashed arrow.

According to
Lemma

4.50
and
Lemma

4.51
,
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{F}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{F}}^{2}
has weights 0 and -1, so
(I_{\mathscr{Z}/\mathscr{F}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathscr{F}}I_{\mathscr{Z}/\mathscr{F}})^{\vee}
has weights 0 and 1. In particular, any section which vanishes at two distinct points vanishes. Since
\sigma_{a}^{\prime}

\mathscr{F}
and contained in

∎

Thus, there is a continuous lift of the family of preferred sections
s^{\prime}_{t}
of
R_{DH}^{\mathrm{unip},\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime},x^{\prime})
to a family of sections
s_{t}
of
R\mathcal{Z}_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(V,\phi)
. Note that any lift of a preferred section
s_{E^{\prime}}
is preferred, since such a section yields a flat subbundle of
p_{*}E^{\prime}
, which is therefore a sub-harmonic bundle. Thus,
s_{t}
is a continuous family of preferred sections of
R\mathcal{Z}_{DH}^{\mathrm{qu}|n,\mathrm{loc}}(V,\phi)
through
(V,\phi)
whose image in
M_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is a lift of an arc of the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbit of
V^{\prime}
. Since
p^{*}:M_{B}(X)\to M_{B}(X^{\prime})
is quasi-finite (in fact finite
[
97
, Lemma 1]
), any continuous lift of a germ of an
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbit is a germ of a
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbit, and this completes the proof of the claim, and therefore also of the theorem.
∎

∎

Corollary 8.24
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
be a closed Hodge substack and
Z
its image in
M_{B}(X)
. Then for any
n
, each irreducible component of
Z^{\mathrm{qu}|n}
is
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-stable.

Corollary

8.24
will allow us to produce points underlying
\mathbb{C}-\mathrm{VHS}
in Hodge substacks provided
Z^{\mathrm{qu}}
is nonempty, which will always be the case by the results of
Section

5
if we require bialgebraicity.

Remark 8.25
.

In the proper case, Simpson shows there is an
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-action on
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
lifting the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
action on
M_{B}(X)

[
113
]
; by pre- and post-composing with complex conjugation, we obtain another action. The resulting
\mathbb{R}_{>0}*\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-action is expected to have as fixed points the local systems underlying
\mathbb{C}
-VMHSs
[
113
, Remark, p. 45]
, and perhaps the induced action of
\mathbb{R}_{>0}*\mathbb{R}_{>0}
on the formal germ at a fixed point would yield the
\mathbb{C}
-MHS of
Theorem

4.3
. Thus, the formally twistor condition might be related to a
\mathbb{R}_{>0}*\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-stability condition, and it is conceivable the notion of a Hodge substack could be equivalent to the notion of a
\mathbb{R}_{>0}*\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-stable substack.

9.
Properties of constructible subsets of local systems

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space.
We begin with some general remarks about various properties of constructible subsets of the Betti stack
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
, and eventually formulate a notion of absolute Hodge subsets. It will be important to work with points of the stack as opposed to the coarse space since we will ultimately want to allow non-semisimple local systems.

9.1.
Nonextendability

We briefly review some of the notions from
[
20
]
.

Definition 9.1
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
a subset.

(1)

For
X\subset\bar{X}
a partial compactification by a connected normal algebraic space (meaning an inclusion as an open subset of a connected normal algebraic space), we say
\Sigma
extends to
\bar{X}
if
\Sigma
is contained in the image of the restriction
\mathcal{M}_{B}(\bar{X})(\mathbb{C})\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
.

(2)

If
X
is smooth we say
\Sigma
is
nonextendable
if the following condition is met. For any finite étale cover
f:X^{\prime}\to X
and any partial log smooth compactification
X^{\prime}\subset\bar{X}^{\prime}
, if
f^{*}\Sigma
extends to
\bar{X}^{\prime}
then
X^{\prime}=\bar{X}^{\prime}
.

(3)

In general, we say
\Sigma
is
nonextendable
if for some (hence any) resolution
f:Y\to X
,
f^{*}\Sigma
is nonextendable.

Note that any
\Sigma
is nonextendable if
X
is proper.

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
. The following are equivalent.

(1)

\Sigma
is nonextendable.

(2)

For every proper morphism
f:C\to X
from a smooth quasiprojective curve
C
,
f^{*}\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(C)(\mathbb{C})
is nonextendable.

(3)

For every morphism
f:\mathbb{D}^{*}\to X^{\mathrm{an}}
from the punctured disk which completes to a morphism from
\mathbb{D}
to some partial compactification of
X^{\mathrm{an}}
,
f^{*}\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(\mathbb{D}^{*})(\mathbb{C})
contains a non-trivial local system.

Proof.

Nonextendability is unaffected by passing to a finite étale cover, and maps from disks and curves will lift to a resolution up to an étale cover of the source, so we may assume
X
is smooth. In the log smooth situation, a local system extends if and only if its local monodromy around every divisor is trivial, which can clearly be checked on curves and disks.
∎

Corollary 9.3
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a proper morphism of connected normal algebraic spaces and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)(\mathbb{C})
. Then if
\Sigma
is nonextendable, so is
f^{*}\Sigma
. If
f
is in addition dominant, then
\Sigma
is nonextendable if and only if
f^{*}\Sigma
is.

Proposition 9.4
.

Let
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
a subset, and
\Sigma^{\mathrm{Zar}}
the
\mathbb{Q}
-Zariski closure. Then
\Sigma
is nonextendable if and only if
\Sigma^{\mathrm{Zar}}
is.

Proof.

The subset
\Sigma
is extendable if and only if there is a resolution
Y\to X
and a finite étale cover
Y^{\prime}\to Y
with composition
f:Y^{\prime}\to X
and a partial log smooth compactification
i:Y^{\prime}\to\bar{Y}^{\prime}
such that
f^{*}\Sigma\subset i^{*}\mathcal{M}_{B}(\bar{Y}^{\prime})(\mathbb{C})
. Since
i^{*}\mathcal{M}_{B}(\bar{Y}^{\prime})(\mathbb{C})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Zariski closed in
\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y^{\prime})(\mathbb{C})
the claim follows.
∎

Proposition 9.5
.

Let
X
be a smooth algebraic space and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
of bounded rank. Then there is a finite étale cover
\pi:X^{\prime}\to X
arising from a finite quotient of the image of the monodromy of
\bigoplus_{V\in\Sigma}V
, a partial log smooth compactification
i:X^{\prime}\to\bar{X}^{\prime}
, and a nonextendable
\bar{\Sigma}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(\bar{X}^{\prime})
such that
i^{*}\bar{\Sigma}^{\prime}=\pi^{*}\Sigma
.

Proof.

Taking Zariski closure by
Proposition

9.4
and the sum of local systems corresponding to generic points of each irreducible component we may reduce to a single local system and this is
[
20
, Proposition 3.5]
.
∎

Note that in the previous proposition we can do without the finite étale cover if we allow a partial compactification by a Deligne–Mumford stack. In fact, as a consequence of
Theorem

1.3
such a partial compactification exists even when
X
is only assumed to be normal.

Let
X
be the complement of a normal crossing divisor in a smooth algebraic space
\bar{X}
. For every
x\in\bar{X}
, the local fundamental group of
X
at
x
(inside
\bar{X}
) is by definition the group
\pi_{1}(X,\bar{X},x):=\pi_{1}(B\cap X,x^{\prime})
,
where
B
is a small euclidean ball in
\bar{X}
around
x
and
x^{\prime}\in B\cap X
(since
\pi_{1}(B\cap X,x^{\prime})
is abelian, it does not depend of the choice of
x^{\prime}
).

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
X
be a smooth algebraic space and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
. Let
\bar{X}\supset X
be a log smooth compactification of
X
. Then,
\Sigma
is nonextendable if and only if the induced representations of the local fundamental group
\pi_{1}(X,\bar{X},x)
at each point
x
of
\bar{X}\setminus X
are collectively injective.

Proof.

Indeed, for every
x\in\bar{X}
, every element of the local fundamental group
\pi_{1}(X,\bar{X},x)
is obtained as the image of a generator of
\pi_{1}(\Delta^{\ast})
for a holomorphic map
f:\mathbb{D}^{\ast}\to X^{\mathrm{an}}
from the punctured disk which completes to a holomorphic map from
\mathbb{D}
to
\bar{X}
with
x=f(0)\in\bar{X}
.
∎

Proposition 9.7
.

Let
X
be a smooth algebraic space and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
. Let
\bar{X}\supset X
be a log smooth compactification of
X
. Assume that
\Sigma
contains only local systems with unipotent local monodromy. If
\Sigma
is nonextendable, then there exists a finite subset
\Sigma_{0}\subset\Sigma
which is nonextendable.

Observe that
Proposition

9.7
is not true without any assumption on
\Sigma
, as shown by the example where
X=\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
consists of the torsion rank one local systems.

Proof.

Thanks to Proposition
9.6
, it is sufficient to find a finite subset
\Sigma_{0}\subset\Sigma
such that the induced representations of the local fundamental group at each point of
\bar{X}\setminus X
are collectively injective. Since there are only finitely many strata, it is sufficient to look at what happen in the neighborhood of one point
x\in\bar{X}\setminus X
. For every
V\in\Sigma
, let
K^{x}_{V}
be the kernel of the induced representation of the free abelian group of finite type
\pi_{1}(X,\bar{X},x)
. Since the image of this representation is made of commuting unipotent matrices,
K^{x}_{V}
is a saturated subgroup of
\pi_{1}(X,\bar{X},x)
, i.e. the quotient group is torsion-free. By assumption, the intersection
\cap_{V\in\Sigma}K^{x}_{V}=\{0\}
. Since the
K^{x}_{V}
’s are saturated, it follows that there is a finite subset
\Sigma_{0}\subset\Sigma
such that
\cap_{V\in\Sigma_{0}}K^{x}_{V}=\{0\}
, finishing the proof.
∎

9.2.
Absolute constructible subsets

In this section we show that the notion of bialgebraicity with respect to the Riemann–Hilbert correspondence as in
Section

5
is essentially equivalent to absoluteness, which is intrinsic to the Betti side.

Let
X
be a smooth complex algebraic space. Let
LS(X)
be the abelian category of complex local systems on
X^{\mathrm{an}}
. Let
\mathrm{Conn}(X)^{\mathrm{reg}}
be the abelian category of algebraic flat vector bundles on
X
with regular singularities. Deligne’s version of the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence states that the functor
\mathrm{Conn}(X)^{\mathrm{reg}}\to LS(X)
which associates to an algebraic flat vector bundle with regular singularities
(V,\nabla)
the locally constant sheaf of
\nabla
-flat sections of
V^{\mathrm{an}}
is an equivalence of abelian categories
[
34
]
. For any field automorphism
\sigma\in\Aut(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Q})
and any complex scheme
Y
, let
Y^{\sigma}\to Y
denote the base change of
Y
via
\sigma
(it is a morphism of schemes but not of complex schemes). By pulling-back along the morphism of
X^{\sigma}\to X
, we get an equivalence of categories
\mathrm{Conn}(X)^{\mathrm{reg}}\simeq\mathrm{Conn}(X^{\sigma})^{\mathrm{reg}}
, and therefore a bijection between the isomorphism classes of objects in both categories. Therefore, any automorphism
\sigma\in\Aut(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Q})
induces a canonical bijection
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathcal{M}_{B}(X^{\sigma})(\mathbb{C})
. Given a subset
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
, we denote by
\Sigma^{\sigma}
its image in
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X^{\sigma})(\mathbb{C})
through this canonical bijection.

Definition 9.8

(Simpson
[
106
]
, Budur-Wang
[
23
]
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth complex algebraic space. Let
K\subset\mathbb{C}
be a subfield. A subset
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is absolute
K
-constructible (resp. absolute
K
-closed or absolute
K
-locally closed) if for any automorphism
\sigma\in\Aut(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Q})
the subset
\Sigma^{\sigma}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X^{\sigma})(\mathbb{C})
defined above is
K
-constructible (resp.
K
-closed or
K
-locally closed).

If
X
is merely a normal complex algebraic space, the open immersion
X^{\mathrm{reg}}\subset X
induces a surjective homomorphism between their fundamental groups. Therefore, the induced morphism of algebraic stacks
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X^{\mathrm{reg}})
is a closed immersion.

Definition 9.9
.

Let
X
be a normal complex algebraic space. Let
K\subset\mathbb{C}
be a subfield.

(1)

A subset
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is absolute
K
-constructible (resp. absolute
K
-closed or absolute
K
-locally closed) if its image by the injective map
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X^{\mathrm{reg}})(\mathbb{C})
is absolute
K
-constructible (resp. absolute
K
-closed or absolute
K
-locally closed).

(2)

A morphism between (products of) Betti stacks is an absolute
K
-morphism if its graph is absolute
K
-constructible.

Proposition 9.10
.

Let
X
be a normal complex algebraic space. Let
K\subset\mathbb{C}
be a subfield. Then

(1)

\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is an absolute
K
-closed subset of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
.

(2)

Absolute
K
-constructible subsets of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
form a Boolean subalgebra.

(3)

Pullback under algebraic morphisms, tensor products, and direct sums are absolute
K
-morphisms.

(4)

Images and preimages of absolute
K
-constructible subsets under absolute
K
-constructible morphisms are absolute
K
-constructible.

Proof.

Immediate.
∎

Lemma 9.11
.

(1)

The semisimple locus
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is absolute
\mathbb{Q}
-constructible.

(2)

The maps
c_{X}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})\to M_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
,
p_{X}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(C)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}\to M_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
and
\mathrm{ss}_{X}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
are absolute
\mathbb{Q}
-morphisms and commute with pull-back under any algebraic map
f:X\to Y
.

Proof.

For (1), a local system is semisimple if and only if the associated flat bundle is semisimple, and semisimplicity is preserved under the Galois action. For (2), clearly the morphism
c_{X}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})\to M_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is an absolute
\mathbb{Q}
-morphism, and the absoluteness of
p_{X}
and
\mathrm{ss}_{X}
follows. The final claim is obvious for
c_{X}
, and by the harmonic theory (see
Theorem

7.9
) the pull-back of a semisimple local systems is semisimple, whence the remainder of the claim.
∎

Proposition 9.12

(Compare with
[
106
, Theorem 6.3]
; see also
[
23
, Proposition 7.4.6]
)
.

Let
X
be a complex algebraic space defined over a countable field
L\subset\mathbb{C}
. Let
S\subset X(\mathbb{C})
be a subset such that as
\sigma
runs through
\Aut(\mathbb{C}/L)
,
S^{\sigma}
runs through countably many subsets of
X(\mathbb{C})
. Suppose that
S
is an analytically constructible subset of
X^{\mathrm{an}}
.
Then
S
is algebraically constructible in
X(\mathbb{C})
.

Recall that an analytically constructible subset of a complex analytic variety is an element of the Boolean algebra generated by closed analytic subsets.

Proof.

We follow closely the proof of
[
106
, Theorem 6.3]
.
Note that it is sufficient by induction on dimension to prove that the Euclidean closure
\bar{S}
is Zariski closed in
X(\mathbb{C})
, and that is what we will prove.

Let us show that, up to enlarging
L
, one can assume that
S
is stable by
\Aut(\mathbb{C}/L)
. Let
\{S_{j}\}
denote the set of subsets of
X(\mathbb{C})
which occur as
S^{\sigma}
. For each pair
(j,k)
with
S_{j}\neq S_{k}
, choose a point
x_{jk}
in one of
S_{j}
or
S_{k}
but not the other. Then, among all the subsets, a given one is determined by the information of whether
x_{jk}
is in it or not for all
j,k
. On the other hand, there are countably many points
x_{jk}
, so we may choose a countable field extension
L^{\prime}/L
so that
x_{jk}^{\sigma}=x_{jk}
for every
\sigma\in\Aut(\mathbb{C}/L^{\prime})
. Then
S^{\sigma}=S
for every
\sigma\in\Aut(\mathbb{C}/L^{\prime})
.

For every
x\in X(\mathbb{C})
, let
\overline{\{x\}}^{L}
be the smallest closed algebraic subset of
X
defined over
L
and containing
x
. For every
y\in\overline{\{x\}}^{L}(\mathbb{C})
, we have
\overline{\{y\}}^{L}\subset\overline{\{x\}}^{L}
with equality if and only if
\overline{\{y\}}^{L}(\mathbb{C})
meets the orbit
\Aut(\mathbb{C}/L)\cdot x
. Since
L
is countable, there are only countably many strict suvarieties defined over
L
, so that their union is a meager subset of
\overline{\{x\}}^{L}(\mathbb{C})
. By the Baire category theorem, its complement
\Aut(\mathbb{C}/L)\cdot x
is therefore dense in
\overline{\{x\}}^{L}(\mathbb{C})
for the euclidean topology. Taking
x\in S
, one has
\Aut(\mathbb{C}/L)\cdot x\subset S
, hence
\overline{\{x\}}^{L}
is contained in the euclidean closure
\bar{S}
of
S
.

Let
T
be an irreducible component of
\bar{S}
. Then
T\cap S
is covered by the closed subsets
\overline{\{x\}}^{L}\cap T\cap S
for
x\in T\cap S
. Since there are countably many of those, it follows from Baire category theorem again that there exists
x\in T\cap S
such that
\overline{\{x\}}^{L}
contains a non-empty open subset of
T
. By analytic continuation, we get that
T
is equal to a geometric component of
\overline{\{x\}}^{L}
.
∎

Corollary 9.13

(cf.
[
23
, Proposition 7.4.5]
)
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a proper log smooth algebraic space and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
K\subset\mathbb{C}
be a countable subfield. If
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is an absolute
K
-constructible subset, then any analytic irreducible component of the Euclidean closure of
RH^{-1}_{(\bar{X},D)}(\Sigma)
is a Zariski closed subset of
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)(\mathbb{C})
.

Proof.

Let
L
be a countable field of definition of
\bar{X}
and
D
, so that
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)
is also defined over
L
. Therefore there exists
U
a
L
-scheme and
U\to\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)
a smooth surjective morphism defined over
L
. Let
S=RH^{-1}_{(\bar{X},D)}(\Sigma)
and
T
be the preimage of
S
in
U(\mathbb{C})
. Since
\Sigma
is absolute
K
-constructible and
K
is countable,
T^{\sigma}
runs through countably many subsets of
U(\mathbb{C})
as
\sigma
runs through
\Aut(\mathbb{C}/L)
. Moreover,
T
is the preimage by an analytic map of an algebraic (hence analytic) constructible subset of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
, hence it is an analytic constructive subset of
U^{\mathrm{an}}
. Therefore, it follows from
Proposition

9.12
that any analytic irreducible component of the Euclidean closure of
T
in
U^{\mathrm{an}}
is Zariski closed in
U(\mathbb{C})
. Conclude using the analytic version of
[
118
,
Tag 0DR5
]
.
∎

Proposition 9.14
.

Let
X
be a normal complex algebraic space,
K\subset\mathbb{C}
be a countable subfield, and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
an absolute
K
-constructible. Then

(1)

For every
\sigma\in\Aut(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Q})
we have
\overline{\Sigma^{\sigma}}=\overline{\Sigma}^{\sigma}
(where
\overline{(\cdot)}
denotes euclidean closure).
In particular,
\bar{\Sigma}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is absolute
K
-closed.

(2)

If
\Sigma
is absolute
K
-locally closed, then for every
\sigma\in\Aut(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Q})
the geometric irreducible components of
\Sigma^{\sigma}
are the
\sigma
-translates of the geometric irreducible components of
\Sigma
. In particular, each geometric irreducible component of
\Sigma
is absolute
\bar{K}
-locally closed.

Proof.

Replacing
X
with
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
, one can assume that
X
is smooth. Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a proper log smooth algebraic space with
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
(which we may even assume is projective, but this is not necessary). Let

RH_{(\bar{X},D)}^{\mathrm{good}}:\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{good}}\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)^{\mathrm{an}}

be the restriction of the Riemann–Hilbert map, which is surjective and locally (on the source) an isomorphism by
Proposition

7.4
. Note also that:

(i)

Pull-back under
RH_{(\bar{X},D)}^{\mathrm{good}}
commutes with euclidean closure.

(ii)

For any analytically constructible subset of
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{good}}
whose euclidean closure in
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)
is Zariski closed, the Galois action commutes with euclidean closure (in
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{good}}
). This is because
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{good}}
is Galois-stable and open, so inherits this property from the corresponding one on
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)
, which is clear.

(iii)

For any analytically closed subset of
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{good}}
whose euclidean closure in
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)
is Zariski closed, the
\sigma
-translates of components are precisely the components of the
\sigma
-translate.

Thanks to
Corollary

9.13
, (i) and (ii) imply part (1).

For part (2), the geometric components of
\Sigma
are the geometric components of
\bar{\Sigma}
minus
\bar{\Sigma}\setminus\Sigma
, so by (1) we may assume
\Sigma
is absolute
K
-closed. For
\Sigma_{0}
a geometric component of
\Sigma
,
(RH_{(\bar{X},D)}^{\mathrm{good}})^{-1}(\Sigma_{0})
is (globally) pure-dimensional and a union of components of
(RH_{(\bar{X},D)}^{\mathrm{good}})^{-1}(\Sigma)
. Thus, by (iii) every component of
(RH_{(\bar{X},D)}^{\mathrm{good}})^{-1}(\Sigma_{0})^{\sigma}=(RH_{(\bar{X}^{\sigma},D^{\sigma})}^{\mathrm{good}})^{-1}(\Sigma_{0}^{\sigma})
is (globally) pure-dimensional and a union of components of
(RH_{(\bar{X},D)}^{\mathrm{good}})^{-1}(\Sigma^{\sigma})
. In particular, each component of
(RH_{(\bar{X},D)}^{\mathrm{good}})^{-1}(\Sigma_{0})^{\sigma}
dominates a component of
\Sigma^{\sigma}
. In fact, every component of
(RH_{(\bar{X},D)}^{\mathrm{good}})^{-1}(\Sigma_{0})^{\sigma}
must dominate a single component
T_{0}
of
\Sigma^{\sigma}
, since for any component
T_{1}\subset\Sigma^{\sigma}
, the locus of points
x\in\Sigma_{0}
admitting a neighborhood
x\in U\subset\Sigma_{0}
for which
U^{\sigma}\subset T_{1}
is open, and such sets give an open partition of
T_{0}
. Thus,
\Sigma_{0}^{\sigma}\subset T_{0}
, and applying the same argument to
\sigma^{-1}
yields
T_{0}^{\sigma^{-1}}\subset\Sigma_{0}
, so
\Sigma_{0}^{\sigma}
is closed.
∎

Corollary 9.15
.

Let
X
be a normal complex algebraic space. Let
K\subset\mathbb{C}
be a countable subfield.
Every absolute
K
-constructible subset in
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is a Boolean combination of absolute
K
-closed subsets in
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
.

Proof.

If
S
is an absolute
K
-constructible subset, then its closure
\bar{S}
is an absolute

K
-closed subset and the complement
\bar{S}\setminus S
is an absolute
K
-constructible subset of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
of smaller dimension, hence the result follows by induction.
∎

Corollary 9.16
.

Let
X
be a normal complex algebraic space and
K\subset\mathbb{C}
a countable field. A
K
-Zariski closed subset
\Sigma_{B,1}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is absolute
K
-closed if and only if it can be completed to a
K
-bialgebraic pair
(\Sigma_{DR},\{\Sigma_{B,\sigma}\})
of the Riemann–Hilbert
\mathbb{Q}
-correspondence stack
\mathcal{M}_{RH,\mathbb{Q}}(\bar{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime},E)
for some (hence any) choice of log smooth resolution/compactification.

Proof.

By replacing
X
with
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
we may assume
X
is smooth.
On both sides of the implication, by definition
\Sigma_{B,1}
is
K
-constructible. We may assume
\Sigma_{B,1}
is closed by
Corollary

9.15
. If
\Sigma_{B,1}
is absolute
K
-closed, then taking
\Sigma_{DR}
to be any component of
RH_{(\bar{X},D)}^{-1}(\Sigma)
meeting
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)^{\mathrm{good}}
,
(\Sigma_{DR},\{(\Sigma_{B,1})^{\sigma}\})
is
K
-bialgebraic. Conversely, if
(\Sigma_{DR},\{\Sigma_{B,\sigma}\})
is
K
-bialgebraic, we may assume it is
K
-bialgebraic irreducible, and by taking Zariski closure we may assume all of
\Sigma_{DR},\Sigma_{B,\sigma}
are closed and irreducible. Then for each
\sigma
,
RH_{(\bar{X}^{\sigma},D^{\sigma})}(\Sigma_{DR}^{\sigma})
contains a euclidean open set
U_{\sigma}\subset\Sigma_{B,\sigma}
which has nonempty interior. Since the saturation
RH_{(\bar{X}^{\sigma},D^{\sigma})}^{-1}RH_{(\bar{X}^{\sigma},D^{\sigma})}(\Sigma^{\sigma}_{DR})
is a countable union of algebraically constructible sets (as it is the set of all log connections whose meromorphic connection is isomorphic to the meromorphic connection of a point of
\Sigma_{DR}^{\sigma}
), it follows that for every
\sigma
the euclidean closure in
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X}^{\sigma},D^{\sigma})
of every component of
(RH_{(\bar{X}^{\sigma},D^{\sigma})}^{\mathrm{good}})^{-1}(\Sigma_{B,\sigma})
is algebraic. Since
\Sigma_{DR}
is a component of
RH_{(\bar{X},D)}^{-1}(\Sigma_{B,1})
and
\Sigma_{DR}^{\sigma}
is a component of
RH_{(\bar{X}^{\sigma},D^{\sigma})}^{-1}(\Sigma_{B,\sigma})
it follows that thet saturations are equal
RH_{(\bar{X},D)}^{-1}(\Sigma_{B,1})=RH_{(\bar{X}^{\sigma},D^{\sigma})}^{-1}(\Sigma_{B,\sigma})
. Thus,
\Sigma_{B,\sigma}=(\Sigma_{B,1})^{\sigma}
and
\Sigma_{B,1}
is absolute
K
-closed.
∎

Corollary 9.17
.

Let
X
be a connected normal complex algebraic space. Let
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
be an absolute
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-closed subset. Then

(1)

The locus of points
\Sigma^{\mathrm{qu}}\subset\Sigma
with quasiunipotent local monodromy is Zariski dense in
\Sigma
.

(2)

For each
n
, the locus
\Sigma^{\mathrm{qu}|n}\subset\Sigma
with eigenvalues of local monodromy of order dividing
n
is absolute
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-closed.

Proof.

The first part is by
Theorem

5.23
, and the second part follows from
Lemma

5.20
or can be seen directly.
∎

9.3.
Graded nearby cycle functors

9.3.1.
The Betti side

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a log smooth complex manifold and set
X:=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
D_{k}
be a (smooth) irreducible component of
D
. Let
D^{k}
denotes
\cup_{j\neq k}D_{j}
. Then
D_{k}\cap D^{k}
is a simple normal crossing divisor in
D_{k}
. Let
U_{k}
be a tubular neighborhood of
D_{k}
in
\bar{X}
. A fortiori,
U_{k}\setminus D^{k}
is a tubular neighborhood of
D_{k}\setminus D^{k}
, and we have an exact sequence of groups:

1\to K\to\pi_{1}(U_{k}\setminus D)\to\pi_{1}(U_{k}\setminus D^{k})\to 1.

The group
K
is canonically isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}
and we denote by
\gamma_{k}\in\pi_{1}(U_{k}\setminus D)
its canonical generator (which corresponds geometrically to the class of a simple loop going around
D_{k}
counterclockwise). Moreover,
K
is central in
\pi_{1}(U_{k}\setminus D)
, hence it acts on any local system defined on
U_{k}\setminus D
. Thanks to Deligne’s version of the Riemann–Hilbert correspondence, the group
K
acts also on any regular meromorphic connection on the pair
(U_{k},U_{k}\cap D)
.

Recall the following well-known result.

Lemma 9.18

(see
[
37
, Proposition 1.6.1]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an abelian category. For each object

Z
of
\mathcal{A}
equipped with a nilpotent endomorphism
N
, there exists a unique finite increasing filtration
W_{\bullet}=W(N)_{\bullet}
of
Z
satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

N(W_{k})\subset W_{k-2}
for every
k
,

(2)

N^{k}
induces an isomorphism
\operatorname{gr}_{k}^{W}Z\simeq\operatorname{gr}_{-k}^{W}Z
for every
k\geq 0
.

Moreover, the association
(Z,N)\mapsto(Z,W(N)_{\bullet})
defines a functor between the category of objects
Z
of
\mathcal{A}
equipped with a nilpotent endomorphism
N
, a morphism between
(Z_{1},N_{1})

and
(Z_{2},N_{2})
being a morphism
f\colon Z_{1}\to Z_{2}
in
\mathcal{A}
such that
f\circ N_{1}=N_{2}\circ f
, and the category of objects
Z
of
\mathcal{A}
equipped with a finite increasing filtration
W_{\bullet}
, the morphisms being the morphisms preserving the filtrations.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)
denote the closed substack of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
consisting in local systems with unipotent local monodromy. For every
k
, we have the graded nearby cycle functor

\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{k}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)\to\mathcal{M}_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(D_{k}\setminus D^{k})

defined as follows. If
A
is a ring and
V
is a
A
-local system on
X
with unipotent local monodromy, then
\gamma_{k}
induces a unipotent automorphism
T
of its restriction
V_{|U_{k}\setminus D}
to
U_{k}\setminus D
. Let
N:=T-\mathrm{Id}
be the induced nilpotent endomorphism of
V_{|U_{k}\setminus D}
and
W_{\bullet}
be the associated filtration in the abelian category
\mathcal{M}_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(U_{k}\setminus D)(A)
. Then
\operatorname{gr}^{W}V_{|U_{k}\setminus D}
is a
A
-local system on
U_{k}\setminus D
with no monodromy around
D_{k}
. Therefore it extends uniquely as a
A
-local system on
U_{k}\setminus D^{k}
, whose restriction to
D_{k}\setminus D^{k}
is by definition the graded nearby cycles
\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{k}}(V)
along
D_{k}
. Note that
\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{k}}(V)
has indeed unipotent local monodromies around the irreducible components of
D_{k}\cap D^{k}
.

Theorem 9.19

(Mochizuki)
.

Assume that
(\bar{X},D)
is a projective log smooth variety and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
K
be a field of characteristic zero. Let
V
be a
K
-local system on
X
with unipotent local monodromy. If
V
is semisimple, then the
K
-local system
\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{k}}(V)
on
D_{k}\setminus D^{k}
is semisimple.

Proof.

Since the fundamental group of
X
is finitely generated, there exists a finitely generated field
F
and a
F
-local system
V_{F}
such that
V_{F}\otimes_{F}K=V
. Since on the one hand the property of being semisimple is invariant by changing coefficients, and on the other hand every finitely generated field can be embedded in
\mathbb{C}
, one can assume that
K=\mathbb{C}
. When
K=\mathbb{C}
, this is
[
84
, Theorem 19.49]
. See also
[
98
]
.
∎

9.3.2.
The De Rham side

We briefly recall the construction of a graded nearby cycle functor for logarithmic connections and refer to
[
21
,
75
]
for the details.

Let
\bar{X}
be a smooth projective variety defined over an algebraically closed
field
k
. Let
D
be a simple normal crossing divisor on
\bar{X}
and let
Y
be an irreducible component of
D
. Let
\iota\colon Y\to\bar{X}
be the canonical embedding.

Let
\mathbb{L}_{Y}=\left(L,[,],\alpha\right)
be the Lie algebroid on
Y
, where
L=\iota^{\ast}T_{X}(-\log D)
is equipped with the Lie algebra structure induced from the standard Lie algebra structure on
T_{\bar{X}}
and the anchor map
\alpha\colon L\to\operatorname{Der}_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{Y})=T_{Y}
is the canonical map induced by
\iota
. The restriction to
Y
defines an obvious functor
(E,\nabla)\mapsto\iota^{\ast}(E,\nabla)
from the abelian category
\mathcal{M}_{DR}(\bar{X},D)(k)
to the category
\mathbb{L}_{Y}
-Mod of coherent
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules with an
\mathbb{L}_{Y}
-module structure. Moreover, the residue of
(E,\nabla)
along
Y
yields an endomorphism of
\iota^{\ast}(E,\nabla)
seen as an element of
\mathbb{L}_{Y}
-Mod. When the residue is nilpotent, we get from Lemma
9.18
an increasing filtration
W_{\bullet}
of
\iota^{\ast}(E,\nabla)
. By definition, the graded nearby functor of
(E,\nabla)\in\mathcal{M}_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)(k)
is
\operatorname{gr}\psi_{Y}(E,\nabla):=\operatorname{gr}^{W}\iota^{\ast}(E,\nabla)
. It is an element of
\mathcal{M}_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(Y,D_{Y})
.

9.3.3.
Compatibilities

The following proposition is a straightforward consequence of the constructions.

Proposition 9.20
.

Let
D
be a simple normal crossing divisor in a smooth projective complex algebraic variety
\bar{X}
. Let
D_{k}
be a (smooth) irreducible component of
D
.

(1)

The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)(\mathbb{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(D_{k},D^{k})(\mathbb{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)(\mathbb{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(D_{k}\setminus D^{k})(\mathbb{C})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces RH}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces RH}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{k}}}

is commutative.

(2)

For every field automorphism
\sigma\in\Aut(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Q})
and every
(E,\nabla)\in\mathcal{M}_{DR}^{\mathrm{nilp}}(\bar{X},D)(\mathbb{C})
, one has

\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{k}^{\sigma}}((E,\nabla)^{\sigma})=\left(\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{k}}((E,\nabla))\right)^{\sigma}.

Corollary 9.21
.

In the above setup,
\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{k}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(X)\to\mathcal{M}_{B}^{\mathrm{unip}}(D_{k}\setminus D^{k})
is an absolute
\mathbb{Q}
-morphism.

9.4.
Absolute Hodge subsets

Definition 9.22
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space. An
absolute Hodge substack

\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
is a Hodge substack for which
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{C})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-locally closed and absolute
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-locally closed. An
absolute Hodge subset

\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is a set of points
\Sigma=\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{C})
underlying an absolute Hodge substack
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
.

By
Proposition

9.10
, absolute Hodge substacks of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
satisfy the same functoriality properties as Hodge substacks, see
Proposition

8.21
:

Proposition 9.23
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space.

(1)

For any
r
,
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X,r)
and
\{\mathrm{triv}_{r}\}
are absolute Hodge substacks of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
.

(2)

Assume
X
is a curve. For any
V\in\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{qu},\mathrm{ss}}
, the fixed residual eigenvalues leaf
FE(V)\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
is a closed absolute Hodge substack.

(3)

Intersections and reductions of absolute Hodge substacks are absolute Hodge substacks.
\mathbb{Q}
-irreducible components of reduced absolute Hodge substacks are absolute Hodge substacks. Finite unions of closed absolute Hodge substacks are absolute Hodge substacks.

(4)

Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of connected normal algebraic spaces and
f^{*}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
the pullback morphism. Then:

(a)

For any absolute Hodge substack
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
,
(f^{*})^{-1}(\mathcal{Z})\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)
is an absolute Hodge substack.

(b)

For any absolute Hodge substack
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)
,
f^{*}\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
is an absolute Hodge substack provided
f
is dominant or a Lefschetz curve (see
Definition

4.8
)

(5)

Inverse images of absolute Hodge substacks under the direct sum and tensor product morphisms
\oplus,\otimes:\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)^{2}\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
are absolute Hodge substacks.

Corollary 9.24
.

Every irreducible component of a closed absolute Hodge substack contains a point underlying a
\mathbb{C}-\mathrm{VHS}
.

Proof.

By
Corollary

9.17
,
Corollary

8.24
, and
Corollary

7.26
.
∎

For completeness we give a notion of absolute Hodge subset suited to the semisimple quasiunipotent local monodromy case, which is substantially easier since we do not have to deal with the miniversal families.

Definition 9.25
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space. A
coarse absolute Hodge subset

\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss},\mathrm{qu}}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-constructible absolute
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-constructible
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-stable subset.

We sometimes refer to subsets of
M_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
being coarse absolute Hodge subsets, by which we mean the corresponding set of semisimple local systems is coarse absolute Hodge.

Proposition 9.26
.

Let
X
be connected normal algebraic space.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
be a closed absolute Hodge substack. Then for each
n
,
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{qu}|n}(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}\subset M_{B}(X)
is a coarse absolute Hodge subset.

(2)

Intersections and finite unions of coarse absolute Hodge subsets are coarse absolute Hodge subsets.

(3)

Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of connected normal algebraic spaces and
f^{*}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
the pullback morphism. Then:

(a)

For any coarse absolute Hodge subset
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
,
(f^{*})^{-1}(\Sigma)^{\mathrm{ss},\mathrm{qu}|n}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)(\mathbb{C})
is a coarse absolute Hodge subset.

(b)

For any coarse absolute Hodge subset
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss},\mathrm{qu}}
,
f^{*}\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is a coarse absolute Hodge subset.

Proof.

Immediate from
Corollary

8.24
,
Proposition

9.10
,
Theorem

7.9
, and the functoriality of the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-action.
∎

Finally, we give an ad hoc definition that is not particularly well-behaved, but collects the properties we will need for the proofs in
Section

13
and
Section

14
.

Definition 9.27
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space. A
weak absolute Hodge subset

\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-constructible absolute
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-constructible subset such that:

(1)

Each geometric irreducible component of
\Sigma^{\mathrm{Zar}}
contains a point underlying a
\mathbb{C}
-VHS with quasiunipotent local monodromy.

(2)

For each geometric irreducible component
\Sigma_{0}
of
\Sigma^{\mathrm{Zar}}
which is not generically semisimple with quasiunipotent local monodromy, let
\mathcal{M}_{\Sigma_{0}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
be the reduced substack with underlying set of points
\Sigma_{0}
. Then
\mathcal{M}_{\Sigma_{0}}
has a point underlying a
\mathbb{C}
-VHS with quasiunipotent local monodromy at which it is formally Hodge as in
Definition

4.58
.

(3)

There exists a
\mathbb{Q}
-constructible absolute
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-constructible subset
\Sigma_{0}\subset\Sigma^{\mathrm{qu},\mathrm{ss}}
satisfying (1) and containing the
\mathbb{C}
-VHS points of
\Sigma
from (2).

Note that by definition a weak absolute Hodge subset has bounded rank. Note also that a weak absolute Hodge subset
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{qu},\mathrm{ss}}
is just a
\mathbb{Q}
-constructible absolute
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-constructible subset satisfying (1).

Proposition 9.28
.

(1)

For any coarse absolute Hodge subset
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
, there is a finite étale cover
p:X^{\prime}\to X
corresponding to a finite quotient of the image of the monodromy representation of
\bigoplus_{V\in\Sigma}V
such that
p^{*}\Sigma
is a weak absolute Hodge subset.

(2)

For any closed absolute Hodge substack
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
,
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{C})
is a weak absolute Hodge subset.

Proof.

The first claim is immediate from by the density of the quasiunipotent locus (
Corollary

9.17
) and
Theorem

7.22
combined with
Proposition

8.3
. The second part follows from
Corollary

9.17
,
Proposition

9.23
, and
Corollary

9.24
.
∎

Corollary 9.29
.

For each
r
,
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X,r)(\mathbb{C})
is a weak absolute Hodge subset of
\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
.

Proposition 9.30
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space.

(1)

Finite unions and closures of weak absolute Hodge subsets are weak absolute Hodge.

(2)

\mathbb{Q}
-irreducible components of weak absolute Hodge subsets are weak absolute Hodge.

(3)

Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of connected normal algebraic spaces and
f^{*}:\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)\to\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)
the pullback morphism. Then for any weak absolute Hodge subset
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)(\mathbb{C})
,
f^{*}\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
is a weak absolute Hodge subset.

Proof.

Immediate given
9.10
,
Proposition

4.61
, the functoriality of the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-action, and the fact that all operations are defined over
\mathbb{Q}
.
∎

9.5.
Generalities on Shafarevich morphisms

Definition 9.31
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space (or more generally a connected generically inertia-free normal Deligne–Mumford stack) and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
a set of complex local systems. We say
\Sigma
is large if for every non-constant morphism
g:Z\to X
from a connected normal algebraic space
Z
,
g^{*}\Sigma
contains a nontrivial local system.

Lemma 9.32
.

Let
X
be a connected generically inertia-free normal Deligne–Mumford stack and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
a set of complex local systems. Assume that for every point
x\in X(\mathbb{C})
the inertia of
x
acts faithfully on
\bigoplus_{V\in\Sigma}i^{*}_{x}V
. Then there exists a connected finite Galois étale cover
p:X^{\prime}\to X
with Galois group
G
arising from a finite quotient
\pi_{1}(X,x)\to\operatorname{img}\rho_{\Sigma}\to G
such that
X^{\prime}
is an algebraic space.

Proof.

For any point
x\in X
, there is a finite set of local systems
\Sigma_{x}\subset\Sigma
such that the inertia of
x
acts faithfully on
\bigoplus_{V\in\Sigma_{x}}V
. By Selberg’s lemma
17
17

17

Selberg’s lemma states that any finitely generated subgroup of
\mathbf{GL}_{r}(\mathbb{C})
has a torsion-free finite-index subgroup.
, we obtain a cover of the required form trivializing the inertia at points above
x
. By noetherianity (since the inertia sheaf is constructible), such a cover exists trivializing all of the inertia.
∎

We say that a set of complex local systems
\Sigma
on a connected generically inertia-free normal Deligne–Mumford stack
X
