is nonextendable if there exists a connected finite Galois étale cover by an algebraic space
p:X^{\prime}\to X
such that
p^{*}\Sigma
is not extendable. This generalizes the definition for algebraic spaces thanks to
Corollary

9.3
.

Definition 9.33
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
a set of complex local systems. A representable morphism
f:X\to Y
to a connected generically inertia-free normal Deligne–Mumford stack
Y
is an
(algebraic)
\Sigma
-Shafarevich morphism
if:

(1)

s:X\to Y
is dominant and
K(Y)
is algebraically closed in
K(X)
.

(2)

\Sigma
is the pull back of a large nonextendable
\Sigma_{Y}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(Y)(\mathbb{C})
and for every point
y\in Y(\mathbb{C})
the inertia of
y
acts faithfully on
\bigoplus_{V\in\Sigma_{Y}}i^{*}_{y}V
.

(3)

If a morphism
g:Z\to X
from a connected
Z
has the property that
g^{*}V
is trivial for every
V\in\Sigma
, then the composition
Z\to X\to Y
factors through
Z\to\Spec\mathbb{C}
.

In particular, a
\Sigma
-Shafarevich morphism contracts exactly those subvarieties on which the local systems in
\Sigma
have uniformly finite monodromy. Also, there is always a natural étale cover
\tilde{Y}^{\Sigma_{Y}}\to Y
by an analytic space and a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{X}^{\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Y}^{\Sigma_{Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{an}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\mathrm{an}}}

where the top horizontal map is equivariant with respect to the action of the natural deck transformation groups.

Provided they exist,
\Sigma
-Shafarevich morphisms are functorial in the following sense:

Proposition 9.34
.

Let
f:X\to X^{\prime}
be a morphism between two normal connected algebraic spaces,
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
and
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X^{\prime})(\mathbb{C})
two subsets such that
f^{*}\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Sigma
. Suppose
s:X\to Y
(resp.
s^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
) is a
\Sigma
-Shafarevich morphism (resp.
\Sigma^{\prime}
-Shafarevich morphism). Then, there exists a unique morphism
g:Y\to Y^{\prime}
such that we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

In particular, it follows that a Shafarevich morphism is unique up to unique isomorphism provided it exists. Before proving this, we state the following lemma, which says that the existence of a Shafarevich morphism can be checked on certain finite étale covers, and in fact there is always such a cover where the target is an algebraic space. For any
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
, define
\rho_{\Sigma}
to be the monodromy representation of
\bigoplus_{V\in\Sigma}V
.

Lemma 9.35
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
.

(1)

Suppose an algebraic
\Sigma
-Shafarevich morphism
s:X\to Y
exists. Given a connected finite Galois étale cover
q:Y^{\prime}\to Y
coming from a finite quotient
\pi_{1}(Y,y)\to\operatorname{img}\rho_{\Sigma_{Y}}\to G
, the base-change
s^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
of
s
is an algebraic
p^{*}\Sigma
-Shafarevich morphism, where
p:X^{\prime}\to X
is the base-change of
q
.

(2)

Given a connected finite Galois étale cover
p:X^{\prime}\to X
with Galois group
G
arising from a finite quotient
\pi_{1}(X,x)\to\operatorname{img}\rho_{\Sigma}\to G
, and a
p^{*}\Sigma
-Shafarevich morphism
s^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
, then
s^{\prime}
is equivariant with respect to a
G
-action on
Y^{\prime}
, and the quotient
s:X\to[G\backslash Y^{\prime}]
is a
\Sigma
-Shafarevich morphism.

Proof of
Proposition

9.34
and
Lemma

9.35
.

Part (1) of the lemma is straightforward.

We prove part (2) of the lemma and the proposition together in a sequence of more general cases. We first prove
Proposition

9.34
in the case
f
is an open embedding,
\Sigma=f^{*}\Sigma^{\prime}
, and
Y,Y^{\prime}
are algebraic spaces; we will prove that
g
exists and is an isomorphism. Let
X\to Z\to Y
be a factorization of
s
for which
X\to Z
is an open embedding and
Z\to Y
is proper; likewise
X\to Z^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
. If
Z^{\prime\prime}
is the normalization of the closure of
X
in
Z\times Z^{\prime}
via the diagonal map, then since the composition
Z^{\prime\prime}\to Z\times Z^{\prime}\to Y\times Y^{\prime}
is proper, the pullback of
\Sigma_{Y}\boxtimes\Sigma_{Y^{\prime}}
is nonextendable. But this pullback is the pullback of
\Sigma_{Y}
(resp.
\Sigma_{Y^{\prime}}
) along
Z^{\prime\prime}\to Y
(resp.
Z^{\prime\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
), so it follows that both of these maps are proper. On the other hand,
Z^{\prime\prime}\to Y
and
Z^{\prime\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
have the same fibers, hence by the rigidity lemma
[
32
, Lemma 1.15]
there is a unique isomorphism
g:Y\to Y^{\prime}
making the obvious diagram commute.

We now prove
Proposition

9.34
when
Y,Y^{\prime}
are algebraic spaces. Let
X\to X_{1}\to Y^{\prime}
be a relative compactification of the composition
s^{\prime}\circ f
and
X_{1}\to Z\to Y^{\prime}
the Stein factorization. Then
X\to Z
is a
f^{*}\Sigma
-Shafarevich morphism. Thus we may assume
X=X^{\prime}
and
f
is the identity. Let
X\to X_{2}\to Y\times Y^{\prime}
be a relative compactification of
s\times s^{\prime}:X\to Y\times Y^{\prime}
with
X_{2}
normal. As in the previous paragraph,
X_{2}\to Y
is proper, and
X_{2}\to Y^{\prime}
contracts the fibers of
X_{2}\to Y
, so by the rigidity lemma there is a factorization
X_{2}\to Y\to Y^{\prime}
.

We now prove
Lemma

9.35
(2) provided
Y^{\prime}
is an algebraic space. By the algebraic space case of
Proposition

9.34
proven above,
G
acts on the map
s^{\prime}
, and the stack quotient is easily checked to be the
\Sigma
-Shafarevich morphism.

We now prove
Proposition

9.34
in general. By
Lemma

9.35
(1) base-changing along a finite étale Galois cover of
Y^{\prime}
yields the same setup, and by
Lemma

9.32
we may take such a cover for which
Y^{\prime}
is an algebraic space. By the algebraic space case of
Lemma

9.35
(2), this will be sufficient to prove the original claim by taking stack quotients. Thus, we may assume
Y^{\prime}
is an algebraic space. Again by
Lemma

9.32
and
Lemma

9.35
(1), there is a finite étale cover
Y^{\prime\prime}\to Y
with
Y^{\prime\prime}
an algebraic space and Galois group
G
such that, if
p:X^{\prime\prime}\to X
is the base-change, the base-change
s^{\prime\prime}:X^{\prime\prime}\to Y^{\prime\prime}
of
s
is a
p^{*}\Sigma
-Shafarevich morphism. By the algebraic space case of
Proposition

9.34
, the morphism
X^{\prime\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
factors through a morphism
Y^{\prime\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
which is moreover
G
-invariant, and it follows that the required morphism
Y\to Y^{\prime}
exists.

Finally, the general case of
Lemma

9.35
(2) follows by the same argument as above now using
Proposition

9.34
for stacks.
∎

Definition 9.36
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
. The rank
r
Shafarevich saturation (or just
r
-saturation for short) of
\Sigma
is the subset
\Sigma^{r\text{-}\mathrm{sat}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X,\rk\leq r)(\mathbb{C})
of those local systems of rank
\leq r
which pullback trivially along any morphism
f:Z\to X
for which
f^{\ast}V
is trivial for all
V\in\Sigma
. That is,

\Sigma^{r\text{-}\mathrm{sat}}:=\bigcap_{\begin{subarray}{c}f:Z\to X\\
f^{*}\Sigma\subset\mathrm{triv}_{Z}\end{subarray}}(f^{*})^{-1}(\mathrm{triv}_{Z})\hskip 18.06749pt\mbox{in}\hskip 18.06749pt\mathcal{M}_{B}(X,\rk\leq r)(\mathbb{C}).

A subset
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(\mathbb{C})
which is equal to its rank
r
saturation is called
r
-saturated
.

The main point of this definition is the following:

Corollary 9.37
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space,
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
a subset of local systems of bounded rank, and
s:X\to Y
a morphism to a generically inertia-free Deligne–Mumford stack. Then

(1)

For any
r
,
\Sigma^{\mathrm{r\text{-}sat}}
underlies a closed absolute Hodge subset. In particular, it is a weak absolute Hodge subset.

(2)

For
r\gg 0
,
s
is a
\Sigma
-Shafarevich morphism if and only if it is a
\Sigma^{\mathrm{r\text{-}sat}}
-Shafarevich morphism.

Proof.

Part (1) is by
Proposition

9.23
and
Proposition

9.28
. Part (2) is clear.
∎

10.
Pluriharmonic local systems over non-archimedean local fields

For the proof of the algebraic integrability of the Katzarkov–Zuo foliation in the next section, we shall need to understand the graded nearby cycles functor in the category of non-archimedean pluriharmonic bundles. More precisely, let
K
be a non-archimedean field and
V
a semisimple
K
-local system with unipotent local monodromy on a log smooth algebraic space
(\bar{X},D)
. Associated to a pluriharmonic non-archimedean norm on
V
is a closed positive
(1,1)
-current
\omega_{\bar{X}}
with continuous potential on
\bar{X}
which turns out to be independent of the choosen pluriharmonic norm on
V
. On the other hand, for each boundary component
D_{0}
there is a graded nearby cycles local system
\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{0}}V
, which likewise has its own closed positive
(1,1)
-current
\omega_{D_{0}}
; we show
\omega_{D_{0}}
is the restriction of
\omega_{\bar{X}}
. This is a corollary of a similar statement for the characteristic polynomials, see
Theorem

10.48
.

10.1.
Generalities on non-archimedean norms

References for this section include
[
51
,
50
,
17
,
13
]
.

In all this section,
K
will be a non-archimedean local field with absolute value
|\cdot|\colon K\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, i.e. either a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
or
\mathbb{F}_{p}((T))
for a prime
p
. Write
|K^{\ast}|=q^{\mathbb{Z}}
for a positive integer
q
.

Definition 10.1
.

Let
V
be a finite dimensional
K
-vector space. A norm on
V
is a function
\|\cdot\|\colon V\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that

(1)

\|v\|=0\Longleftrightarrow v=0
;

(2)

\|av\|=|a|\|v\|
for all
a\in K,v\in V
;

(3)

\|v+w\|\leq\max\{\|v\|,\|w\|\}
for all
v,w\in V
.

We write
\mathcal{N}(V)
for the set of norms on
V
.

Note that
\|v+w\|=\max\{\|v\|,\|w\|\}
for all
v,w\in V
such that
\|v\|\neq\|w\|
. Indeed

\|w\|=\|(v+w)-v\|\leq\max\{\|v+w\|,\|v\|\}\leq\max\{\|v\|,\|w\|\}
. If
\|v\|<\|w\|
, it follows that
\|w\|=\max\{\|v+w\|,\|v\|\}
, and so
\|w\|=\|v+w\|
.

Let
V
be a finite dimensional
K
-vector space. Let
\|\cdot\|
be a norm on
V
. A basis
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\}
of
V
such that
\|\sum_{i}a_{i}v_{i}\|=\max_{i}(\|a_{i}v_{i}\|)
for every
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in K
is said to be orthogonal for
\|\cdot\|
. Every norm admits an orthogonal basis
[
51
, Proposition 1.1]
, and every two norms always have a common orthogonal basis
[
51
, Proposition 1.3]
.

If
W\subset V
is a sub-
K
-vector space, then the restriction of
\|\cdot\|
to
W
is a norm on
W
. There is also an induced norm on
V/W
by letting
\|x+W\|:=\inf_{y\in x+W}\|y\|
for every
x\in V
.

The dual
V^{\vee}
of
V
is endowed with the dual norm defined as:

\|\lambda\|:=\sup_{v\in V\setminus\{0\}}\frac{|\lambda(v)|}{\|v\|}.

If
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\}
is an orthogonal basis for
\|\cdot\|
, then its dual basis
\{v_{1}^{\vee},\ldots,v_{n}^{\vee}\}
is an orthogonal basis for the dual norm and
\|v_{i}^{\vee}\|=\|v_{i}\|^{-1}
for every
i
.

Finally, for every positive integer
r
, there is a unique induced norm
\wedge^{r}\|\cdot\|
on
\wedge^{r}V
such that, if
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\}
is an orthogonal basis for
\|\cdot\|
on
V
, then
\{v_{i_{1}}\wedge\ldots\wedge v_{i_{r}}\}_{i_{1}<\ldots<i_{r}}
is an orthogonal basis for
\wedge^{r}\|\cdot\|
on
\wedge^{r}V
and
\wedge^{r}\|v_{i_{1}}\wedge\ldots\wedge v_{i_{r}}\|=\|v_{i_{1}}\|\cdots\|v_{i_{r}}\|
for every
i_{1}<\ldots<i_{r}
, see
[
51
, Proposition 3.9]
.

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
V
be a
K
-vector space equipped with a norm
\|\cdot\|
. Let
v\in V\setminus\{0\}
and
\lambda\in V^{\vee}\setminus\{0\}
. Then
\|v\|\cdot\|\lambda\|\geq|\lambda(v)|
, with equality if and only if
Kv
is orthogonal to the hyperplane
\ker(\lambda)
.

Proof.

The inequality is clear, as is the “if” part. For the “only if” part, note that
V=Kv\oplus\ker(\lambda)
is orthogonal if and only if for any
w\in\ker(\lambda)
we have
\|v+w\|=\max(\|v\|,\|w\|)
, which is automatic if
\|v\|\neq\|w\|
. If
\|v\|\cdot\|\lambda\|=|\lambda(v)|
, then for such a
w
we have
\lambda(v+w)=\lambda(v)
, so
\frac{|\lambda(v+w)|}{\|v+w\|}\leq\frac{|\lambda(v)|}{\|v\|}
implies
\|v\|\leq\|v+w\|\leq\max(\|v\|,|w\|)
, and if
\|v\|=\|w\|
we conclude
\|v+w\|=\|v\|
.
∎

Corollary 10.3
.

Let
V
be a
K
-vector space equipped with a norm
\|\cdot\|
and let
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\}
be an orthogonal basis. If
v^{\prime}=\sum_{i}a_{i}v_{i}
and
\|v^{\prime}\|=|a_{1}|\|v_{1}\|
, then
\{v^{\prime},v_{2},\ldots,v_{n}\}
is an orthogonal basis.

Proof.

Let
\{v_{1}^{\vee},\ldots,v_{n}^{\vee}\}
be the dual basis of
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\}
. Taking
\lambda=v_{1}^{\vee}
, we find
\|\lambda\|=\|v_{1}\|^{-1}
so
\|v^{\prime}\|\cdot\|\lambda\|=|a_{1}|=|\lambda(v^{\prime})|
.
∎

Corollary 10.4
.

Let
V
be a
K
-vector space equipped with two norms
\|\cdot\|
and
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
. Let
v\in V\setminus\{0\}
that realizes
\sup_{u\in V\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\N u\N^{\prime}}{\N u\N}
. Then every hyperplane
\ker({\lambda})
which is
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
-orthogonal to
v
is
\|\cdot\|
-orthogonal to
v
.

Proof.

For every
\lambda\in V^{\vee}\setminus\{0\}
, we have

\|\lambda\|^{\prime}\cdot\frac{\|v\|^{\prime}}{\|v\|}=\sup_{u\in V\setminus\{0\}}\frac{|\lambda(u)|}{\|u\|^{\prime}}\cdot\sup_{u\in V\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\|u\|^{\prime}}{\|u\|}\geq\sup_{u\in V\setminus\{0\}}\frac{|\lambda(u)|}{\|u\|}=\|\lambda\|

so
|\lambda(v)|\leq\|v\|\cdot\|\lambda\|\leq\|v\|^{\prime}\cdot\|\lambda\|^{\prime}
. The conclusion follows from the lemma.
∎

Lemma 10.5
.

Let
V
be a
K
-vector space equipped with two norms
||\cdot||
and
||\cdot||^{\prime}
. Let
\{v_{1},\cdots,v_{n}\}
be an orthogonal basis for
||\cdot||
. Then

\sup_{v\in V\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\N v\N^{\prime}}{\N v\N}=\max_{i}\frac{\N v_{i}\N^{\prime}}{\N v_{i}\N}.

Proof.

On the one hand, one trivially has
\sup_{v\in V\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\N v\N^{\prime}}{\N v\N}\geq\max_{i}\frac{\N v_{i}\N^{\prime}}{\N v_{i}\N}
. On the other hand, if
v=\sum_{i}a_{i}v_{i}
, then

\|v\|^{\prime}\leq\max_{i}|a_{i}|\|v_{i}\|^{\prime}\leq\max_{i}\frac{\N v_{i}\N^{\prime}}{\N v_{i}\N}\cdot\max_{i}|a_{i}|\|v_{i}\|=\max_{i}\frac{\N v_{i}\N^{\prime}}{\N v_{i}\N}\cdot\|v\|.

∎

10.2.
Relative spectrum of two norms

(Compare with
[
13
, section 2.5]
.)
Let
V
be a
K
-vector space of dimension
N
. Let
\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}(V)
be two norms on
V
. The relative spectrum of
\|\cdot\|
with respect to
\|\cdot\|
is the finite decreasing sequence

\lambda_{1}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime})\geq\ldots\geq\lambda_{N}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime})

defined by the formula

\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime}):=\sup_{W\subset V,\dim W\geq i}\left(\inf_{w\in W\setminus\{0\}}\log\frac{\|w\|^{\prime}}{\|w\|}\right).

In particular,

\lambda_{1}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime}):=\sup_{v\in V\setminus\{0\}}\log\frac{\|v\|^{\prime}}{\|v\|}

and

\lambda_{N}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime}):=\inf_{v\in V\setminus\{0\}}\log\frac{\|v\|^{\prime}}{\|v\|}=-\lambda_{1}(\|\cdot\|^{\prime},\|\cdot\|).

Proposition 10.6
.

[
13
, Proposition 2.24]

Let
V
be a
K
-vector space of dimension
N
. Let
\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}(V)
. Let
(e_{i})
be a basis of
V
which is orthogonal for both norms, and order it so
that

\frac{\|e_{1}\|^{\prime}}{\|e_{1}\|}\geq\ldots\geq\frac{\|e_{N}\|^{\prime}}{\|e_{N}\|}.

Then, for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
,

\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime})=\log\frac{\|e_{i}\|^{\prime}}{\|e_{i}\|}.

Corollary 10.7
.

For every
k\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, one has

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime})=\lambda_{1}(\wedge^{k}\|\cdot\|,\wedge^{k}\|\cdot\|^{\prime}).

Corollary 10.8
.

Let
V=Kv\oplus W
be a decomposition which is orthogonal for both norms. If moreover
\lambda_{1}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime})=\log\frac{\|v\|^{\prime}}{\|v\|}
, then, for every integer
i\in\{1,\ldots,\rk V-1\}
, one has

\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|_{|W},\|\cdot\|_{|W}^{\prime})=\lambda_{i+1}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime}).

10.3.
Space of norms

Let
V
be a
K
-vector space of dimension
N
. The group
GL(V)(K)
acts on
\mathcal{N}(V)
by composition.

To each basis
e=(e_{i})
of
V
is associated an injective map

\iota_{e}\colon\mathbb{R}^{N}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}(V),

which takes
a\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
to the unique norm
\|\cdot\|_{e,a}
that is diagonalized in
(e_{i})
and such that
\log\|e_{i}\|_{e,a}=-a_{i}
. The image
\mathbb{A}_{e}:=\iota_{e}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\subset\mathcal{N}(V)
is thus the set of norms that are diagonalized in the given basis
e
, and is called an apartment (or flat) of
\mathcal{N}(V)
.
The Bruhat-Tits metric on
\mathcal{N}(V)
is defined as:

d_{2}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime}):=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

Theorem 10.9

(See
[
50
, 2.4.7, Corollaire 2]
and
[
13
, Theorem 3.1 and Corollary 3.3]
)
.

The function
d_{2}\colon\mathcal{N}(V)\times\mathcal{N}(V)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
defines a metric on
\mathcal{N}(V)
, and
(\mathcal{N}(V),d_{2})
is a NPC complete metric space. The Bruhat-Tits metric
d_{2}
is the unique metric on
\mathcal{N}(V)
for which
\iota_{e}\colon(\mathbb{R}^{N},l_{2})\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{N}(V),d_{2})
is an isometric embedding for each basis
e
of
V
.

The extended Bruhat-Tits building
\Delta(\GL(V),K)
can be canonically identified with
(\mathcal{N}(V),d_{2})
as metric spaces equipped with an isometrical action of
\GL(V)(K)
, see
[
17
]
.

Proposition 10.10
.

Let
V
be a finite-dimensional
K
-vector space. Let
W\subset V
be a sub-
K
-vector space. Then the canonical map
\mathcal{N}(V)\to\mathcal{N}(W)\times\mathcal{N}(V/W)
is Lipschitz continuous.

Proof.

First,
d_{2}
is comparable to the Goldman-Iwahori distance
d_{\infty}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime}):=\max(\lambda_{1}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime}),\lambda_{1}(\|\cdot\|^{\prime},\|\cdot\|))
, since any two norms on
\mathbb{R}^{N}
are equivalent, and
d_{2},d_{\infty}
are both pulled back from a norm on
\mathbb{R}^{N}
via the map given by the
\lambda_{i}
’s. Thus, it is enough to prove
\mathcal{N}(V)\to\mathcal{N}(W)\times\mathcal{N}(V/W)
is distance-decreasing with respect to
d_{\infty}
. The map
\mathcal{N}(V)\to\mathcal{N}(W)
obtained by restricting the norm is clearly
d_{\infty}
-decreasing. Moreover, the map
\mathcal{N}(V)\to\mathcal{N}(V^{\vee})
obtained by taking the dual norm is an involutive isometry with respect to
d_{\infty}

[
13
, Theorem 1.21]
. Finally, the dual of the quotient norm on
V/W
is the restriction of the dual norm of
V

[
13
, Lemma 1.23]
. Thus, the quotient norm map
\mathcal{N}(V)\to\mathcal{N}(V/W)
is also distance decreasing with respect to
d_{\infty}
, and the claim follows.

∎

10.4.
Pluriharmonic norms on local systems

Definition 10.11
.

Let
X
be a complex analytic space equipped with a
K
-local system
L
. Let
\mathcal{N}(V)\to X
be the total space of the locally constant sheaf of spaces of norms on the stalks of
V
, so that the fiber
\mathcal{N}(V)_{x}
at
x\in X
is canonically identified with
\mathcal{N}(V_{x})
. A norm on
V
is then by definition a locally Lipschitz continuous section of
\mathcal{N}(V)\to X
.

Assume that
X
is connected and fix
x\in X
. Let
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\GL(V_{x})
be the monodromy representation of
V
. A norm
\|\cdot\|
on
V
is thus equivalent to a
\rho
-equivariant locally Lipschitz continuous map
\tilde{X}\to\mathcal{N}(V_{x})
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a normed
K
-local system. A set of sections
v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}
is orthogonal if it is pointwise an orthogonal basis for its span.

Definition 10.12
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a normed
K
-local system on a complex analytic space
X
. Its regular locus
\mathrm{Reg}(\|\cdot\|)
is the open subset of points
x\in X
for which an orthogonal trivializing set of sections for
V
exists in some neighborhood of
x
. The complementary of the regular subset is called the singular set of
\|\cdot\|
and denoted
\mathrm{Sing}(\|\cdot\|)
.

Definition 10.13
.

A harmonic (resp. pluriharmonic)
K
-local system on a complex analytic space
X
is a normed
K
-local system
(V,\|\cdot\|)
on
X
such that the associated section of
\mathcal{N}(V)\to X
is harmonic (resp. pluriharmonic): for every
x\in X
and every simply-connected open neighborhood
U
of
x
in
X
, the induced map
U\to\mathcal{N}(V_{x})
is harmonic (resp. pluriharmonic).

We deduce from
Proposition

6.28
the following:

Corollary 10.14
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a pluriharmonic
K
-local system on a complex manifold
X
. Then its singular locus
\mathrm{Sing}(\|\cdot\|)
is pluripolar.

If
(V,\|\cdot\|)
is pluriharmonic and
e_{1},\ldots,e_{k}
is an orthogonal trivializing set of sections, then the functions
\log\|e_{i}\|
are pluriharmonic.

Definition 10.15
.

Let
V
be a
K
-local system on a complex analytic space. A norm
\|\cdot\|
on
V
is called plurisubharmonic if for every local section
v
of
V
the function
\log\|v\|
is plurisubharmonic.

Proposition 10.16
.

A pluriharmonic norm is plurisubharmonic.

Proof.

Let
V
be a
K
-local system on a complex analytic space
X
. Let
\|\cdot\|
be a pluriharmonic norm on
V
. Let
v
be a local section of
V
. Locally on the regular locus of
\|\cdot\|
, there exists a flat orthogonal basis
(e_{i})
of
V
. Writing
v=\sum_{i}a_{i}e_{i}
, one has
\log\|v\|=\max_{i}\log\|a_{i}e_{i}\|
. This shows that
\log\|v\|
is plurisubharmonic on
\mathrm{Reg}(\|\cdot\|)
.
Since
\mathrm{Sing}(\|\cdot\|)\subset X
is a pluripolar subset and
\log\|v\|
is locally Lipschitz continuous, it follows that
\log\|v\|
is plurisubharmonic on
X
.
∎

Theorem
6.29
immediately implies the following extension result.

Theorem 10.17
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a normed
K
-local system on a complex analytic space
X
. If
(V,\|\cdot\|)
is pluriharmonic in restriction to the complementary of a closed pluripolar subset of
X
, then
(V,\|\cdot\|)
is pluriharmonic.

We will prove later a stronger extension result, cf. Theorem
10.25
.
Since the singular locus of a pluriharmonic norm is a closed pluripolar subset of
X
thanks to Proposition
6.28
, we get the following alternative characterization of pluriharmonicity.

Corollary 10.18
.

A normed
K
-local system
(V,\|\cdot\|)
is pluriharmonic if and only if there exists a closed pluripolar subset
S\subset X
, such that for every point
x\in X\setminus S
there exists a local trivializing orthogonal set of sections
\{e_{i}\}
of
V
such that the functions
z\mapsto\log\|e_{i}(z)\|
are pluriharmonic.

Proposition 10.19
.

Let
(V_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1})
and
(V_{2},\|\cdot\|_{2})
be two normed
K
-local systems on a complex analytic space
X
. Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)=(V_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1})\oplus(V_{2},\|\cdot\|_{2})
. Then,
(V,\|\cdot\|)
is pluriharmonic if and only if
(V_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1})
and
(V_{2},\|\cdot\|_{2})
are pluriharmonic.

Proof.

Let
W_{1}
,
W_{2}
and
W=W_{1}\oplus W_{2}
be
K
-vector spaces of finite dimension. Then there is a canonical inclusion
\mathcal{N}(W_{1})\times\mathcal{N}(W_{2})\to\mathcal{N}(W)
as the subset of norms on
W
for which the decomposition
W=W_{1}\oplus W_{2}
is orthogonal.This realizes
\mathcal{N}(W_{1})\times\mathcal{N}(W_{2})
as a closed convex subset of
\mathcal{N}(W)
. Therefore, the proposition is a consequence of the following general fact: if
M
is a Riemannian manifold and
C\subset\mathcal{N}
is a closed convex subset of the complete metric space
\mathcal{N}
equipped with the induced metric, then a map
M\to C
is pluriharmonic if and only if the induced map
M\to\mathcal{N}
is pluriharmonic.
∎

10.5.
Asymptotic behaviour of pluriharmonic norms

Proposition 10.20
.

Let
V
be a
K
-local system on
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{n}
with quasiunipotent local monodromies around zero. Then
V
admits a flat norm. When the local monodromies are unipotent,
V
admits a flat lattice, i.e. there exists a sub-
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-local system
L\subset V
such that
L\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}K=V
.

Proof.

Equivalently, one needs to prove that every finite set of commuting unipotent elements of
\mathbf{GL}_{r}(K)
stabilize a lattice of
K^{r}
, and that every finite set of commuting quasiunipotent elements of
\mathbf{GL}_{r}(K)
have a common fixed point in
\mathcal{N}(K^{r})
.

Let
U_{1},\ldots,U_{n}
be pairwise commuting unipotent elements of
\mathbf{GL}_{r}(K)
. One easily proves that they stabilize a common complete flag of
K^{r}
. By multiplying every element of a basis adapted to the flag by an adequate power of the uniformizer, one obtains a lattice in
K^{r}
stabilized by the
U_{i}
’s.

Let
T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}
be pairwise commuting quasiunipotent elements of
\mathbf{GL}_{r}(K)
. For every
i
, let
r_{i}
be a positive integer such that
U_{i}:=T_{i}^{r_{i}}
is unipotent. Let
F\subset\mathcal{N}(K^{r})
be the subset of norms that are fixed by the
U_{i}
’s. By the preceding paragraph,
F
is nonempty. It is a closed convex subset of
\mathcal{N}(K^{r})
, hence it is a NPC space. Since the
T_{i}
’s commute pairwise,
F
is stabilized by the action of
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
via the
T_{i}
’s. It follows from the definition of
F
that this action factorizes through
(\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}^{r_{i}})^{n}
. Since
F
is a non empty NPC space, this action has a fixed point by the Bruhat-Tits fixed point theorem.
∎

Definition 10.21
.

A normed
K
-local system
(V,\|\cdot\|)
on
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{n}
with quasiunipotent local monodromies is said to be bounded in the neighborhood of
0
if there exists a flat norm
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
on
V
such that the function
z\mapsto d_{\mathcal{N}(V_{z})}(\|\cdot\|_{z},\|\cdot\|_{z}^{\prime})
is bounded in the neighborhood of
0
.

Note that by Proposition
10.20
, every
K
-local system on
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{n}
with quasiunipotent monodromies admits a flat norm. Moreover, the distance between two flat norms is constant, hence the definition of boundedness does not depend on the choice of the flat norm
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
.

Definition 10.22
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a log smooth complex manifold of dimension
n
and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. A normed
K
-local system
(V,\|\cdot\|)
on
X
with quasiunipotent local monodromies around
D
is said to be locally bounded in the neighborhood of
D
(or just locally bounded at infinity) if for every admissible polydisk
\mathbb{D}^{n}\subset\bar{X}
the restriction of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
to
\mathbb{D}^{n}\setminus D
is bounded in the neighborhood of
0
.

Proposition 10.23
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a pluriharmonic
K
-local system on
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{n}
with quasiunipotent local monodromies. Assume that there exists
U\subset\mathbb{D}^{n}
a neighborhood of
0
such that the restriction of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
to
U\setminus\{0\}
has finite energy. Then
(V,\|\cdot\|)
is Lipschitz continuous in restriction to a neighborhood of
0
(and not only locally Lipschitz continuous).

Proof.

Fixing
r>0
such that
\{(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})\in\mathbb{D}^{n}\,|\,\sum_{i}|z_{i}|^{2}<r\}\subset U
, the proposition follows by applying
Theorem

6.7
with
R=1
and
\Omega_{\epsilon}=\{(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})\in(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{n}\,|\,\epsilon<\sum_{i}|z_{i}|^{2}<r\}
(with the metric induced by the Poincaré metric on
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{n}
) for every
0<\epsilon<r
.
∎

Proposition 10.24
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a pluriharmonic
K
-local system on
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{n}
with quasiunipotent local monodromies around zero. If the restriction of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
to a neighborhood of
0
has finite energy, then
(V,\|\cdot\|)
is bounded in the neighborhood of
0
.

Proof.

Thanks to Proposition
10.20
, there exists a flat norm
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
on
V
. By Proposition
6.14
the function
\delta\colon z\mapsto d_{\mathcal{N}(V_{z})}(\|\cdot\|_{z},\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{z})
is plurisubharmonic on
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{n}
. Since both
\|\cdot\|
and
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
have finite energy in a neighborhood of
0
, it follows from Proposition
10.23
that the function
\delta
is bounded in the neighborhood of
0
by the function
z\mapsto C\log\left(-\log|z|\right)
for some
C>0
. In particular,
\delta
extends as a plurisubharmonic function on a neighborhood of
0
in
\mathbb{D}^{n}
and therefore it is bounded in a neighborhood of
0
.
∎

10.6.
Extension of pluriharmonic norms

Theorem 10.25
.

Let
D
be a normal crossing divisor in a complex manifold
X
of dimension
n
. Let
V
be a
K
-local system on
X
. Let
\|\cdot\|
be a pluriharmonic norm on the restriction of
V
to
X\setminus D
.
Assume that
\|\cdot\|
has finite energy with respect to a metric of Poincaré type on
X\setminus D
. Then
\|\cdot\|
is the restriction of a unique pluriharmonic norm on
V
.

Proof.

Since a pluriharmonic norm is continuous, the unicity is clear. In particular, to prove the existence of the extension, one can work locally on
X
. Therefore, one can replace
X
by an admissible polydisk
\mathbb{D}^{n}\subset X
and prove the existence in a neighborhood of
0\in\mathbb{D}^{n}
. By assumption, the norm
\|\cdot\|
belongs to
W^{1,2}((\mathbb{D}_{r}^{\ast})^{n},\mathcal{N}(V))
for every
0<r<1
. In particular, thanks to Proposition
10.24
, there exists
0<r<1
such that in addition the norm
\|\cdot\|
is bounded on
(\mathbb{D}_{r}^{\ast})^{n}
. The trace of
\|\cdot\|
is a well-defined element of
L^{2}(\partial(\mathbb{D}_{r}^{\ast})^{n},\mathcal{N}(V))
. Let
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
be the unique harmonic norm on
V_{|\mathbb{D}_{r}^{n}}
with the same trace as
\|\cdot\|
on
\partial\mathbb{D}_{r}^{n}
, see Theorem
6.5
. The function
(\mathbb{D}_{r}^{\ast})^{n}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},z\mapsto d_{\mathcal{N}(V_{z})}(\|\cdot\|_{z},\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{z})
is bounded. Moreover, it is subharmonic by Proposition
6.14
, hence by Brelot extension theorem, it extends uniquely as a subharmonic function on
\mathbb{D}_{r}^{n}
. Since its trace in
L^{2}(\partial(\mathbb{D}_{r}^{\ast})^{n},\mathbb{R})
is zero by Theorem
6.3
, it is zero everywhere by the maximum principle. This shows that
\|\cdot\|
admits an extension to
\mathbb{D}_{r}^{n}
as an harmonic norm, hence in particular as a locally Lipschitz continuous norm. Thanks to Theorem
6.29
, this extension is pluriharmonic.
∎

10.7.
Existence

Theorem 10.26
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a projective log smooth variety and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
V
be a semisimple
K
-local system on
X
with quasiunipotent local monodromy. Then
V
admits a pluriharmonic norm of finite energy with respect to any Poincaré-type complete Kähler metric on
X
.

Proof.

It is sufficient to consider the case where
V
is an irreducible
K
-local system. Fix a Poincaré-type complete Kähler metric on
X
. Fix
x\in X
. Let
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X,x)\to\GL(V_{x})
be the monodromy representation of
V
.
Let
X^{\prime}\to X
be a finite étale cover such that the Zariski closure
\mathbf{G}
of the image of
\rho^{\prime}:=\rho_{|\pi_{1}(X^{\prime})}
is connected (and reductive). Thanks to Theorem
6.25
, there exists a
\rho^{\prime}
-equivariant harmonic map
\tilde{X}\to\Delta(\mathbf{G},K)
of finite energy. The induced
\rho^{\prime}
-equivariant map
u\colon\tilde{X}\to\Delta(\GL(V_{x}))
is thus locally Lipschitz continuous with finite energy. Applying Proposition
6.12
, we get that there exists a
\rho
-equivariant locally Lipschitz continuous map
\tilde{X}\to\Delta
of finite energy. Since
\rho
is irreducible, its image is not contained in any parabolic subgroup of
\GL(V_{x})
, therefore the action of
\rho
does not have any fixed point on
\partial\Delta(\GL(V_{x}))
. We conclude from
Theorem

6.8
the existence of a
\rho
-equivariant harmonic map
u\colon\tilde{X}\to\Delta(\GL(V_{x}))
of finite energy, which is necessarily pluriharmonic thanks to
Theorem

6.27
.
∎

10.8.
Induced norm on sublocal systems

10.8.1.
The local setting

Lemma 10.27
.

Suppose
\{f_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is a finite set of subharmonic functions on a connected Kähler manifold such that
f=\max_{i\in I}f_{i}
is harmonic. Then if
f=f_{i}
holds at some point it holds everywhere.

Proof.

Suppose
f=f_{i}
at some point. Then
f_{i}-f
is subharmonic and achieves its maximum value, namely zero, hence it is zero everywhere.
∎

Lemma 10.28
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a harmonic
K
-local system on a connected Kähler manifold
X
. Let
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\}
be a flat orthogonal basis. Let
v
be a nonzero section of
V
such that
\log\|v\|
is harmonic. Then, up to renumbering the
v_{i}
’s,
\{v,v_{2},\ldots,v_{n}\}
is a flat orthogonal basis.

Proof.

Write
v=\sum_{i}a_{i}v_{i}
, so that
\log\|v\|=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\log\|a_{i}v_{i}\|
. Since
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\}
is a flat orthogonal basis, the functions
\log\|a_{i}v_{i}\|=\log|a_{i}|+\log\|v_{i}\|
are harmonic. Since
\log\|v\|
is harmonic by assumption, it follows from Lemma
10.27
that there exists
i
such that
\|v\|=\|a_{i}v_{i}\|
on
X
. Finally, thanks to Corollary
10.3
, up to renumbering the
v_{i}
’s,
\{v,v_{2},\ldots,v_{n}\}
is a flat orthogonal basis.
∎

Lemma 10.29
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a harmonic
K
-local system on a Kähler manifold
X
. Let
W\subset V
be a sub-
K
-local system equipped with a harmonic norm
\|\cdot\|_{W}
. Let
\|\cdot\|_{V}
denote the norm on
W
induced by
\|\cdot\|
. Assume that for every
1\leq i\leq\rk W
the function
\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|_{W},\|\cdot\|_{V})
is harmonic.
Assume that both
(V,\|\cdot\|)
and
(W,\|\cdot\|_{W})
admit a flat orthogonal basis. Then there exists a flat basis
(e_{i})
of
W
which is orthogonal for both
\|\cdot\|_{V}
and
\|\cdot\|_{W}
, that can be completed into a flat orthogonal basis of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
and such that
\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|_{W},\|\cdot\|_{V})=\log\frac{\|e_{i}\|_{V}}{\|e_{i}\|_{W}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the rank of
V
. There is nothing to prove if
V
has rank zero. Therefore, let us assume that the rank of
V
is positive. Let
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\}
be a flat orthogonal basis of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
and
\{w_{1},\ldots,w_{r}\}
be a flat orthogonal basis of
(W,\|\cdot\|_{W})
. Thanks to Proposition
10.6
, one has

\lambda_{1}(\|\cdot\|_{W},\|\cdot\|_{V})=\max_{1\leq i\leq r}\log\frac{\|w_{i}\|_{V}}{\|w_{i}\|_{W}}.

By assumption, the functions
\log\frac{\|w_{i}\|_{V}}{\|w_{i}\|_{W}}
are subharmonic. It follows from Lemma
10.27
that
\lambda_{1}(\|\cdot\|_{W},\|\cdot\|_{V})=\log\|w_{i}\|_{V}-\log\|w_{i}\|_{W}
for some
i
. This shows that the function
\log\|w_{i}\|=\lambda_{1}(\|\cdot\|_{V},\|\cdot\|_{W})+\log\|w_{i}\|_{W}
is harmonic. Therefore, thanks to Lemma
10.28
, up to renumbering the
v_{i}
’s, one can assume that
\{w_{i},v_{2},\ldots,v_{n}\}
is a flat orthogonal basis of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
.

Let
V^{\prime}
be the sublocal system of
V
generated by the
v_{i}
’s with
i\geq 2
, so that the decomposition
V=Kw_{i}\oplus V^{\prime}
is
\|\cdot\|
-orthogonal. Therefore the norm
\|\cdot\|_{V^{\prime}}
induced by
\|\cdot\|
on
V^{\prime}
is harmonic by Proposition
10.19
.

Let
W^{\prime}:=W\cap V^{\prime}
. Then the
decomposition
W=Kw_{i}\oplus W^{\prime}
is
\|\cdot\|_{V}
-orthogonal. Thanks to Corollary
10.4
, the
decomposition
W=Kw_{i}\oplus W^{\prime}
is also
\|\cdot\|_{W}
-orthogonal.
In particular, the norm
\|\cdot\|_{W^{\prime}}
induced by
\|\cdot\|_{W}
on
W^{\prime}
is harmonic by Proposition
10.19
. Moreover, thanks to Corollary
10.8
, for every integer
i\in\{1,\ldots,\rk V-1\}
, one has

\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|_{W^{\prime}},(\|\cdot\|_{V^{\prime}})_{|W^{\prime}})=\lambda_{i+1}(\|\cdot\|_{W},\|\cdot\|_{V}).

Therefore one can apply the induction to
(V^{\prime},\|\cdot\|_{V^{\prime}})
and
(W^{\prime},\|\cdot\|_{W^{\prime}})
and conclude the proof.
∎

Lemma 10.30
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a pluriharmonic
K
-local system on a Kähler manifold
X
. Let
W\subset V
be a sub-
K
-local system equipped with a pluriharmonic norm
\|\cdot\|_{W}
. Let
\|\cdot\|_{V}
denote the norm on
W
induced by
\|\cdot\|
. Assume that for every
1\leq i\leq\rk W
the function
\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|_{W},\|\cdot\|_{V})
is pluriharmonic. Then the norm on
W\oplus(V/W)
induced by
\|\cdot\|
is pluriharmonic.

Proof.

By Proposition
10.10
, the norm on
W\oplus(V/W)
induced
\|\cdot\|
is locally Lipschitz continuous. Since
\mathrm{Sing}(\|\cdot\|)
and
\mathrm{Sing}(\|\cdot\|_{W})
are closed pluripolar subsets of
X
by
Corollary

10.14
, thanks to Theorem
10.17
it is sufficient to prove that the norm on
W\oplus(V/W)
induced
\|\cdot\|
is pluriharmonic on
\mathrm{Reg}(\|\cdot\|)\cap\mathrm{Reg}(\|\cdot\|_{W})
. Since pluriharmonicity is a local property, one can assume that both
(V,\|\cdot\|)
and
(W,\|\cdot\|_{W})
admit a flat orthogonal basis, so that one can apply Lemma
10.29
to conclude.
∎

Corollary 10.31
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a pluriharmonic
K
-local system on a connected complex analytic space
X
. Let
W\subset V
be a sub-
K
-local system. If the norm on
W
induced by
\|\cdot\|
is pluriharmonic, then the norm on
V/W
induced
\|\cdot\|
is pluriharmonic.

Proof.

This a direct consequence of Lemma
10.30
, in which we take for
\|\cdot\|_{W}
the norm on
W
induced by
\|\cdot\|
.
∎

10.8.2.
The global setting

Proposition 10.32
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a compact log smooth Kähler manifold and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
.
Let
V
be a
K
-local system on
X
, with quasiunipotent local monodromy.
Let
\|\cdot\|
be a plurisubharmonic norm on
V
(see Definition
10.15
), which is locally bounded at infinity. Let
W\subset V
be a sub-
K
-local system equipped with a pluriharmonic norm
\|\cdot\|_{W}
which is locally bounded at infinity. Then the functions
\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|_{W},\|\cdot\|_{|W})
are constant for every
1\leq i\leq\rk W
.

Proof.

Thanks to Corollary
10.7
, the function
\sum_{i=1}^{k}\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|_{W},\|\cdot\|_{|W})
is equal to the function
\lambda_{1}(\wedge^{k}\|\cdot\|_{W},\wedge^{k}\|\cdot\|_{|W})
for every
k
. But the latter is a plurisubharmonic on
X
and locally bounded at infinity, hence it is constant on
X
. It follows that the function
\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|_{W},\|\cdot\|_{|W})
are constant.
∎

Theorem 10.33
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a compact log smooth Kähler manifold and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
.
Let
V
be a
K
-local system on
X
, with quasiunipotent local monodromy.
Let
\|\cdot\|
be a pluriharmonic norm on
V
, which is locally bounded at infinity. Let
W\subset V
be a sub-
K
-local system. Assume that
W
admits a pluriharmonic norm which is locally bounded at infinity. Then the norm induced by
\|\cdot\|
on
W\oplus V/W
is pluriharmonic and locally bounded at infinity.

Proof.

Let
\|\cdot\|_{W}
be a pluriharmonic norm on
W
which is locally bounded at infinity. Thanks to Proposition
10.32
, the functions
\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|_{W},\|\cdot\|_{|W})
are constant. It follows from Lemma
10.30
that the norm induced by
\|\cdot\|
on
W\oplus V/W
is pluriharmonic. Moreover, it is locally bounded at infinity thanks to Proposition
10.10
.
∎

Corollary 10.34
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a compact log smooth Kähler manifold and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
.
Let
V
be a
K
-local system on
X
, with quasiunipotent local monodromy.
Let
\|\cdot\|
be a pluriharmonic norm on
V
, which is locally bounded at infinity. Let
W_{\bullet}\subset V
be a filtration by sub-
K
-local systems. Assume that
\operatorname{gr}^{W}V
admits a pluriharmonic norm which is locally bounded at infinity. Then the norm induced by
\|\cdot\|
on
\operatorname{gr}^{W}V
is pluriharmonic and locally bounded at infinity.

Proof.

By induction on
\rk V
.
Let
W\subset V
be the smallest non-zero piece of the filtration
W_{\bullet}
. By assumption,
W
admits a pluriharmonic norm which is locally bounded at infinity. Thanks to Theorem
10.33
, the norm induced by
\|\cdot\|
on
W\oplus V/W
is pluriharmonic and locally bounded at infinity.
Let
W^{\prime}_{\bullet}\subset V/W
be the filtration induced by
W_{\bullet}
on
V/W
. Since
\operatorname{gr}^{W^{\prime}}V/W
is a factor of
\operatorname{gr}^{W}V
, it admits a pluriharmonic norm which is locally bounded at infinity, see Proposition
10.19
. Therefore one can conclude by induction.
∎

Corollary 10.35
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a compact log smooth Kähler manifold and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
.
Let
V
be a
K
-local system on
X
, with quasiunipotent local monodromy.
Let
\|\cdot\|
be a pluriharmonic norm on
V
, which is locally bounded at infinity. Let
W^{\bullet}\subset V
be a filtration by sub-
K
-local systems. Assume that
\operatorname{gr}^{W}V
is a semisimple
K
-local system. Then the induced norm on
\operatorname{gr}^{W}V
is pluriharmonic and locally bounded at infinity.

Proof.

Follows from Theorem
10.26
and Corollary
10.34
.
∎

10.9.
The characteristic polynomial of a pluriharmonic local system

The material presented in this section is close to some material presented in
[
65
,
61
,
44
,
67
,
29
,
14
]
in a slightly different setting.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a pluriharmonic
K
-local system of rank
r
on a complex manifold
X
. Let
x\in X\setminus\mathrm{Sing}(V,\|\cdot\|)
and
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{r}\}
be a flat orthogonal basis of
V
in a neighborhood
U
of
x
. Let
\omega_{i}:=\partial\log\|v_{i}\|
for every
i
. Since the functions
\log\|v_{i}\|
are pluriharmonic, the
\omega_{i}
’s are holomorphic one-forms. Another choice of a flat orthogonal basis yields the same collection, up to permutation, thanks to the following lemma.

Lemma 10.36
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a harmonic
K
-local system on a connected complex manifold
U
.
Let
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{r}\}
and
\{v_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,v_{r}^{\prime}\}
be two flat orthogonal basis of
V
. Then, up to reordering the first basis, one has
\log\|v_{i}\|=\log\|v_{i}^{\prime}\|+k_{i}q
for some integers
k_{i}
.

Proof.

For every
x\in U
, one has
\log\|V_{x}\setminus\{0\}\|=\cup_{i}\log\|v_{i}\|+q\mathbb{Z}
. Therefore, for a fixed
i
,
U
is the union the closed subsets
\{\log\|v_{i}^{\prime}\|=\log\|v_{j}\|+k\}
with
j\in\{1,\ldots,r\}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. By Baire category theorem, one of these subsets has non empty interior, hence is equal to
U
by analytic continuation.
∎

In particular, if
T
is a formal variable, the coefficients of the polynomial
P(T):=\prod_{i}(T-\omega_{i})=T^{r}+\sigma_{1}T^{r-1}+\cdots+\sigma_{r}
are well-defined holomorphic symmetric forms on
U\setminus\mathrm{Sing}(V,\|\cdot\|)
. Since these forms do not depend on any choices, varying the point
x
, we get a well-defined polynomial
P(T)=T^{r}+\sigma_{1}T^{r-1}+\cdots+\sigma_{r}
whose coefficients are holomorphic symmetric forms on
X\setminus\mathrm{Sing}(V,\|\cdot\|)
. The pluriharmonic map
\tilde{X}\to\mathcal{N}(V_{x_{0}})
associated to
(V,\|\cdot\|)
is locally Lipschitz continuous, therefore the forms
\sigma_{i}
are locally bounded in the neighborhood of every point of
\mathrm{Sing}(V,\|\cdot\|)
. Since by Theorem
6.24
the singular locus of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
has Hausdorff codimension at least
2
, they extend to
X
by
[
105
, Lemma 3.(ii)]
. We call the polynomial
P(T)
the characteristic polynomial of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
.

Proposition 10.37
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a pluriharmonic
K
-local system on a complex manifold
X
. Let
Y
be a complex manifold and
f\colon Y\to X
be a holomorphic map.
Then the characteristic polynomial of
f^{*}(V,\|\cdot\|)
is equal to the pull-back of the characteristic polynomial of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
.

Proof.

As in
[
44
, §1.4]
. The pluriharmonic norm corresponds to a pluriharmonic map
u:\tilde{X}\to\Delta
which is locally Lipschitz continuous (
Theorem

6.5
). The building
\Delta
has a length structure: any Lipschitz continuous arc
\gamma:[0,1]\to\Delta
has a well defined length by using the local metric embedding in euclidean space. Concretely, any such arc can be decomposed into arcs contained in an apartment, and the length of such an arc is the standard length with respect to the euclidean metric given by the canonical coordinates. By pushing forward arcs,
\tilde{X}
acquires a length structure. By deforming any arc to the regular locus using
Theorem

6.24
, it is equivalently described by taking the integral of the square root of the sum of the squares of the real parts of the roots of
P_{V}
. The length structure on
\tilde{Y}
is clearly the pullback of that of
\tilde{X}
, as both are pulled back from
\Delta
. It therefore remains to show the length structure determines the characteristic polynomial. In the regular locus we may find arcs for which all but one of the real parts of the roots vanish, and then the length function determines the restriction of the form. Taking such arcs through a point we thereby determine the real parts of the roots, hence the roots, and therefore the characteristic polynomial at that point. As the regular locus is dense, the length structure determines
P_{V}
.
∎

Proposition 10.38
.

If
0\to(V_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1})\to(V,\|\cdot\|)\to(V_{2},\|\cdot\|_{2})\to 0
is an exact sequence of pluriharmonic
K
-local systems on a complex manifold
X
, then the characteristic polynomial of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
is equal to the product of the characteristic polynomials of
(V_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1})
and
(V_{2},\|\cdot\|_{2})
.

Proof.

One can assume that
X
is connected. Let
x\in\mathrm{Reg}(V,\|\cdot\|)\cap\mathrm{Reg}(V_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1})
. Thanks to Lemma
10.29
there exists in the neighborhood of
x
a flat orthogonal basis
(v_{1},\ldots,v_{k})
of
(V_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1})
that completes into a flat orthogonal basis
(v_{1},\ldots,v_{r})
of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
. A fortiori, the images of
(v_{k+1},\ldots,v_{r})
in
V_{2}
give a flat orthogonal basis of
(V_{2},\|\cdot\|_{2})
.
Therefore, the equality from the statement holds in a neighborhood of
x
, hence on the whole of
X
by analytic continuation.
∎

Definition 10.39
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a pluriharmonic
K
-local system of rank
r
on a complex manifold
X
.
We say that its characteristic polynomial
P(T)
is split if there exists
r
holomorphic one-forms
\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{r}\in H^{0}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})
such that
P(T):=\prod_{i}(T-\omega_{i})
.

By construction, the characteristic polynomial of a pluriharmonic
K
-local system
(V,\|\cdot\|)
is split in the neighborhood of every point of
X\setminus\mathrm{Sing}(V,\|\cdot\|)
.

Proposition 10.40
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a compact log smooth Kähler manifold and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a pluriharmonic
K
-local system of rank
r
on
X
, with quasiunipotent local monodromy and finite energy. Then the coefficients of its characteristic polynomial extend (uniquely) as holomorphic logarithmic symmetric forms on
(\bar{X},D)
.
Moreover, if the characteristic polynomial
P(T)
of
(V,\|\cdot\|_{V})
is split, then there exist
n
holomorphic one-forms
\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r}
on
\bar{X}
such that
P(T)=\prod_{i}(T-{\omega_{i}}_{|X})
.

In particular, if
X
is algebraic, then by GAGA the coefficients of the characteristic polynomial are algebraic symmetric forms on
X
.

Proof.

Let
P(T)=T^{r}+\sigma_{1}T^{r-1}+\cdots+\sigma_{r}
be the characteristic polynomial of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
. Its coefficients are holomorphic symmetric forms on
X
. Let
x\in\bar{X}
be a smooth point of
D
, so that there exists an admissible polydisk
\mathbb{D}^{d}\subset\bar{X}
centered at
x
such that
D\cap\mathbb{D}^{d}=\{z_{1}=0\}
. Thanks to Proposition
10.23
, the restriction of the forms
\sigma_{k}
to
\mathbb{D}^{d}\setminus D
are bounded in the neighborhood of
0
with respect to the Poincaré metric on
\mathbb{D}^{d}\setminus D
. Write
\sigma_{k}=\sum_{|\alpha|=k}\tau_{\alpha}(z)dz^{\alpha}
for some holomorphic functions
\tau_{\alpha}
on
\mathbb{D}^{d}\setminus D
. Here
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
, with
|\alpha|:=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\alpha_{i}
and
dz^{\alpha}:=dz_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\ldots dz_{d}^{\alpha_{d}}
. Then

|\sigma_{k}|^{2}_{\omega_{P}}=\sum_{|\alpha|=k}|\tau_{\alpha}(z)|^{2}\left(|z_{1}|\log|z_{1}|^{2}\right)^{2\alpha_{1}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}(1-|z_{i}|^{2})^{2\alpha_{i}},

so that the function
|\tau_{\alpha}(z)|^{2}\left(|z_{1}|\log|z_{1}|^{2}\right)^{2\alpha_{1}}
is bounded in the neighborhood of
0
for every
\alpha
. It follows that
z\mapsto\tau_{\alpha}(z)\cdot z_{1}^{k}
extends as a holomorphic function on
\mathbb{D}^{d}
. This shows that
\sigma_{k}
extends as a holomorphic logarithmic symmetric form on the complementary of the singular locus of
D
in
\bar{X}
, hence on the whole of
\bar{X}
since the later has codimension at least
2
in
\bar{X}
.

Assume now that the characteristic polynomial
P(T)
of
(V,\|\cdot\|_{V})
is split, so that there exist
r
holomorphic one-forms
\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r}
on
X
such that
P(T)=\prod_{i}(T-{\omega_{i}})
. The holomorphic one-forms
\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r}
are orthogonal on the regular locus of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
, hence everywhere on
X
. Since
(V,\|\cdot\|)
has bounded energy, it follows that the
\omega_{i}
’s are square integrable in a Poincaré type metric on
X
. Working near a smooth point of
D
as above and writing
\omega_{i}=\sum_{l=1}^{d}\phi_{il}dz_{l}
for some holomorphic functions
\phi_{il}
on
\mathbb{D}^{d}\setminus D
, we get that

\int_{\mathbb{D}^{\ast}\times\mathbb{D}^{d-1}}|\phi_{i1}(z)|^{2}i^{d}dz_{1}\wedge d\bar{z}_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge dz_{d}\wedge d\bar{z}_{d}<\infty

and that

\int_{\mathbb{D}^{\ast}\times\mathbb{D}^{d-1}}|\phi_{il}(z)|^{2}\frac{1}{|z_{1}|^{2}(\log|z_{1}|^{2}+1)^{2}}i^{d}dz_{1}\wedge d\bar{z}_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge dz_{d}\wedge d\bar{z}_{d}<\infty

for every
l>1
. This implies that the
\phi_{il}
’s extend as holomorphic functions on
\mathbb{D}^{d}
, hence the
\omega_{i}
’s extend as holomorphic one-forms on
\bar{X}
.
∎

Lemma 10.41
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a log smooth Kähler manifold. Let
P(T)
be a monic polynomial with log symmetric form coefficients on
(\bar{X},D)
which is locally split on a dense Zariski open. Then there is a log smooth Kähler manifold
(\bar{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime})
and a proper generically finite
f:(\bar{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime})\to(X,D)
such that
f^{*}P
is globally split on
\bar{X}^{\prime}
with log form roots.

Proof.

Let
q:E\to\bar{X}
be the total space of the vector bundle
\Omega_{\bar{X}}(\log D)
, and the tautological section
s
of
q^{*}\Omega_{\bar{X}}(\log D)
yields a tautological section
\alpha
of
\Omega_{E}(\log q^{-1}(D))
. There is then a closed analytic subvariety
Z(P)\subset E
where
s
satisfies the polynomial
q^{*}P
as a section of
q^{*}\Omega_{\bar{X}}(\log D)
, or equivalently where
\alpha
satisfies
q^{*}P
as a form. Since
P
is monic,
Z(P)
is finite over
\bar{X}
. Since
P
is locally split on a dense Zariski open, it follows that
Z(P)
dominates
\bar{X}
, so there is an irreducible component
Z
of the reduction of
Z(P)
which is not the zero section and which dominates
\bar{X}
. Let
\pi:(\bar{Y},D_{Y})\to(Z,Z\cap\pi^{-1}(D))
be a log resolution and
g:(\bar{Y},D_{Y})\to(\bar{X},D)
. Then the pullback
\pi^{*}\alpha
is a log form and a root of
g^{*}P
, so
g^{*}P
factors. Since the factors also have log symmetric form coefficients, by induction on the degree of
P
the proof is completed.
∎

Proposition 10.42
.

Let
V
be a
K
-local system on a smooth complex algebraic variety
X
with quasiunipotent local monodromy.
Let
\|\cdot\|
and
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
be two pluriharmonic norms on
V
, that are locally bounded at infinity. Then their characteristic polynomials are equal.

Proof.

Thanks to Proposition
10.32
, the relative spectrum of
\|\cdot\|
and
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
is constant. Let
x\in X\setminus\left(\mathrm{Sing}(\|\cdot\|)\cup\mathrm{Sing}(\|\cdot\|^{\prime})\right)
. Then by Lemma
10.29
there exist a flat basis
(e_{i})
of
V
in a neighborhood of
x
which is orthogonal for both norms and such that
\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|,\|\cdot\|^{\prime})=\log\frac{\|e_{i}\|^{\prime}}{\|e_{i}\|}
. By differentiating, we get that the characteristic polynomials of
\|\cdot\|
and
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
are equal in a neighborhood of
x
, hence everywhere by analytic continuation.
∎

Corollary 10.43
.

Let
V
be a
K
-local system on a smooth complex algebraic variety
X
, equipped with a pluriharmonic norm
\|\cdot\|
which is locally bounded at infinity. If
V
is trivializable, then
\|\cdot\|
is necessarily flat.

Proof.

Thanks to Proposition
10.42
, the coefficients of the characteristic polynomial of
\|\cdot\|
are zero (except the top coefficient). It follows that the pluriharmonic map corresponding to
\|\cdot\|
is constant on the
regular locus of
\|\cdot\|
, hence everywhere by density of the regular locus.
∎

10.10.
The canonical current

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a compact log smooth Kähler manifold and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a pluriharmonic
K
-local system of rank
r
on
X
, with quasiunipotent local monodromy and finite energy. In the neighborhood of any regular point of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
, there is a collection of
r
holomorphic one-forms
\omega_{i}
, well-defined up to permutation. Therefore the smooth semipositive
(1,1)
-form
\sqrt{-1}\sum_{i}\omega_{i}\wedge\bar{\omega}_{i}
is well-defined on the regular locus of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
. Moreover, it extends uniquely as closed positive
(1,1)
-current
\omega
with continuous potential on
\bar{X}
. This is an obvious consequence of Proposition
10.40
if the characteristic polynomial of
(V,\|\cdot\|)
is split. The general case follows from the existence of the spectral cover (see
Lemma

10.41
) and a classical average procedure.

The current
\omega
is called the canonical current associated to
(V,\|\cdot\|)
, and it follows from Proposition
10.42
that it depends only on the underlying local system
V
.

Proposition 10.44

(See
[
58
, Proposition 2.2]
and
[
44
, Proposition 3.3.6]
)
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a compact log smooth Kähler manifold and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a pluriharmonic
K
-local system on
X
, with quasiunipotent local monodromy and finite energy. Let
\omega_{V}
be the associated current. Fix
x_{0}\in X
. Let
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X,x_{0})\to GL(V_{x_{0}})
be the monodromy representation of
V
and
u\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathcal{N}(V_{x_{0}})
be the
\rho
-equivariant pluriharmonic map corresponding to
(V,\|\cdot\|)
.
Choose
Q\in\mathcal{N}(V_{x_{0}})
and let
\phi\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
be the function defined as
\phi(x):=2\cdot d_{2}(u(x),Q)^{2}
for every
x\in\tilde{X}
. Then
dd^{c}\phi\geq\omega_{V}
.

10.11.
The graded nearby cycle functor for pluriharmonic local systems

Proposition 10.45
.

Let
(V,\|\cdot\|)
be a pluriharmonic
K
-local system on
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n-k}
, with quasiunipotent local monodromy and finite energy. Assume that
(V,\|\cdot\|)
extends as a normed
K
-local system to
(\mathbb{D}_{r}^{\ast})^{k}\times\mathbb{D}_{r}^{n-k}
for some
r>1
. Let
W_{\bullet}
be a filtration of
V
by sub-
K
-local systems such that the
K
-local system
\operatorname{gr}^{W}V
extends to
\mathbb{D}^{n}
. Then, up to going to a finite étale cover
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n-k}\to(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n-k}
, there exists a pluriharmonic
K
-local system
(V^{\prime},\|\cdot\|^{\prime})
on
\mathbb{D}^{n}
equipped with a filtration
W_{\bullet}^{\prime}
by sub-
K
-local systems and an isomorphism
\operatorname{gr}^{W^{\prime}}V^{\prime}\simeq\operatorname{gr}^{W}V
as graded normed
K
-local systems on
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n-k}
.

Proof.

Since
(V,\|\cdot\|)
has finite energy, thanks to
Proposition

10.24
it is locally bounded in the neighborhood of
0
. Fix a point
P\in(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n-k}
and let
u\colon\mathbb{H}^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n-k}\to\mathcal{N}(V_{P})
be the
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
-equivariant pluriharmonic map associated to
\|\cdot\|
. Since the image of
u
is bounded, it meets only finitely many simplices of
\mathcal{N}(V_{P})
. Since the monodromy action preserves the simplicial structure of
\mathcal{N}(V_{P})
, a finite index of the monodromy group
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
acts trivially on the image of
u
. Therefore,
u
factorizes through a finite étale cover of
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n-k}
. Note that the induced map

v\colon(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n-k}\to\mathcal{N}(V_{P})
has finite energy with respect to the Poincaré metric on
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n-k}
. Let
V^{\prime}
(resp.
W^{\prime}_{i}
) be the constant local system on
\mathbb{D}^{n}
with fiber
V_{P}
(resp.
(W_{i})_{P}
) at
P
. The map
v
endows the restriction of
V^{\prime}
to
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n-k}
with a pluriharmonic norm, which thanks to Theorem
10.25
extends (uniquely) as a pluriharmonic norm on
V^{\prime}
on
\mathbb{D}^{n}
. By construction, there is an isomorphism
\operatorname{gr}^{W^{\prime}}V^{\prime}\simeq\operatorname{gr}^{W}V
as graded normed
K
-local systems on
(\mathbb{D}^{\ast})^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n-k}
, so that the statement follows.
∎

Proposition 10.46
.

Let
D
be a compact Kähler manifold. Let
E\subset D
be a simple normal crossing divisor. Let
N\geq 0
. For every
0\leq k\leq N
, let
G_{k}
be a
K
-local system on
D\setminus E
with quasiunipotent local monodromy, and
\|\cdot\|_{k}
be a norm on
G_{k}
which is locally bounded at infinity. Assume the following conditions hold:

•

Every
G_{k}
admits a pluriharmonic norm which is locally bounded at infinity.

•

In the neighborhood of every
x\in D\setminus E
, there exists a pluriharmonic
K
-local system
(V^{\prime},\|\cdot\|^{\prime})
equipped with a filtration
W_{\bullet}^{\prime}
by sub-
K
-local systems such that, for every
k
, the
K
-local system
\operatorname{gr}^{W^{\prime}}_{k}V^{\prime}
equipped with the norm induced by
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
(G_{k},\|\cdot\|_{k})
as normed
K
-local systems.

Then the norms
\|\cdot\|_{k}
are pluriharmonic for every
k
.

Proof.

By induction on
N
. There is nothing to prove if
N=0
. Assume that
N\geq 1
. Without lost of generality, one can assume that
G_{0}
is non-zero. By assumption,
G_{0}
admits a pluriharmonic norm
\|\cdot\|_{H}
which is locally bounded at infinity.

Let
x\in D\setminus E
, and let
(V^{\prime},\|\cdot\|^{\prime})
and
W_{\bullet}^{\prime}
as in the statement of the proposition. Let
\|\cdot\|_{0}^{\prime}
be the plurisubharmonic norm on
W^{\prime}_{0}
induced by the pluriharmonic norm
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
. By assumption,
(W^{\prime}_{0},\|\cdot\|_{0}^{\prime})\simeq(G_{0},\|\cdot\|_{0})
as normed
K
-local systems in the neighborhood of
x
. In particular, by letting
x
cover
D\setminus E
, we get that the norm
\|\cdot\|_{0}
on
G_{0}
is plurisubharmonic. It follows from Proposition
10.32
(applied to
W=V=G_{0}
) that the functions
\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|_{H},\|\cdot\|_{0})
are constant for every
i
.
On the other hand, working again in the neighborhood of
x\in D\setminus E
, the functions
\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|_{H},\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{0})
are equal to the functions
\lambda_{i}(\|\cdot\|_{H},\|\cdot\|_{0})
, hence constant. Therefore, by Proposition
10.30
, the norm induced by
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
on
W^{\prime}_{0}\oplus(V^{\prime}/W^{\prime}_{0})
is pluriharmonic. In particular, the norm
\|\cdot\|_{0}
on
G_{0}
is pluriharmonic.
But replacing
(V^{\prime},\|\cdot\|^{\prime})
and
W_{\bullet}^{\prime}
by
(V^{\prime}/W^{\prime}_{0},\|\cdot\|^{\prime})
and
W_{\bullet}^{\prime}/W^{\prime}_{0}
in the neighborhood of every
x\in D\setminus E
, we can apply the induction and conclude the proof.
∎

We consider the following setting:

•

Let
D\subset U
be an inclusion of a compact Kähler manifold in a Kähler manifold. Let
E\subset U
be a divisor such that
D\cup E
is a simple normal crossing divisor of
U
.

•

Let
V
be a
K
-local system on
U\setminus(D\cup E)
with quasiunipotent local monodromy.

•

Let
\|\cdot\|
be a pluriharmonic on
V
, with finite energy with respect to a Poincaré-like Kähler metric on
U\setminus(D\cup E)
.

•

Let
W_{\bullet}
be a filtration of
V
by sub-
K
-local systems on
U\setminus(D\cup E)
, such that the
K
-local system
\operatorname{gr}^{W}V
extends to
U\setminus E
.

Theorem 10.47
.

Setting as above.

(1)

The norm
\operatorname{gr}^{W}\|\cdot\|
induced by
\|\cdot\|
on
\left(\operatorname{gr}^{W}V\right)_{U\setminus(D\cup E)}
extends as a continuous norm on the extension of
\operatorname{gr}^{W}V
to
U\setminus E
, and this norm is locally bounded in the neighborhood of
E
.

(2)

Assume in addition that the
K
-local system
\left(\operatorname{gr}^{W}V\right)_{|D\setminus E}
admits a pluriharmonic norm which is locally bounded at infinity (this is the case for example if it is semisimple by
Theorem

10.26
and
Proposition

10.24
). Then the restriction of the norm
\operatorname{gr}^{W}\|\cdot\|
to
D\setminus E
is pluriharmonic and locally bounded in the neighborhood of
E
.

Proof.

For part
(1)
, the fact that the norm
\operatorname{gr}^{W}\|\cdot\|
extends as a continuous norm on
U\setminus E
can be checked locally on
D\setminus E
and therefore follows from
Proposition

10.45
. Since
(V,\|\cdot\|)
has finite energy, it follows from
Proposition

10.24
that
\|\cdot\|
is locally bounded in the neighborhood of
E
. Using
Proposition

10.10
it follows that
\operatorname{gr}^{W}\|\cdot\|
is locally bounded in the neighborhood of
E
, therefore so is its continuous extension to
U\setminus E
.

For part
(2)
, it follows immediately from part
(1)
that the restriction of the norm
\operatorname{gr}^{W}\|\cdot\|
to
D\setminus E
is locally bounded in the neighborhood of
E
. Moreover, thanks to Proposition
10.45
, every
x\in D\setminus E
admits an open neighborhood in
D\setminus E
over which there exists a pluriharmonic
K
-local system
(V^{\prime},\|\cdot\|^{\prime})
equipped with a filtration
W_{\bullet}^{\prime}
by sub-
K
-local systems and an isomorphism
\operatorname{gr}_{W^{\prime}}V^{\prime}\simeq\operatorname{gr}_{W}V
as graded normed
K
-local system. Therefore, the pluriharmonicity of the restriction to
D\setminus E
of
\operatorname{gr}^{W}\|\cdot\|
is a consequence of Proposition
10.46
.
∎

Theorem 10.48
.

Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a compact log smooth Kähler manifold and set
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
(V,\|\cdot\|_{V})
be a pluriharmonic
K
-local system on
X
with unipotent local monodromy and finite energy. Let
D_{k}
be an irreducible component of
D
. Assume that the
K
-local system
V
is semisimple. Then the graded nearby-cycle
K
-local system
\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{k}}V
inherits a canonical pluriharmonic norm which is locally bounded at infinity. Moreover, if the characteristic polynomial of
(V,\|\cdot\|_{V})
is split, then its restriction to
D_{k}
is equal to the characteristic polynomial of
\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{k}}(V,\|\cdot\|_{V})
(which in particular is split).

Proof.

The construction of
\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{k}}(V,\|\cdot\|_{V})
is a particular case of Theorem
10.47
. The assertion on the characteristic polynomial follows from the construction in view of
Proposition

10.42
,
Proposition

10.37
and
Proposition

10.38
.
∎

Corollary 10.49
.

With the setup of
Theorem

10.48
, we have
\omega_{\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{k}}V}=\omega_{V}|_{D_{k}}
.

Note that
\omega
has continuous potentials locally on
\bar{X}
, so the restriction makes sense.

11.
Algebraic integrability of the Katzarkov–Zuo foliation

Throughout we fix a prime
p
and consider
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
with a choice of
p
-adic valuation
v
. The valuation
v
on
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
yields a completion
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}
of
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
and we let
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
be the algebraic closure of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}
, equipped with the natural embedding
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}\subset\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. We reserve the notation
K
for a general non-archimedean local field and
\bar{K}
an algebraic closure of
K
.

The main goal of this section is to prove the following generalization of a theorem of Eyssidieux
[
44
]
to the non-proper case:

Theorem 11.1
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space,
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})^{\mathrm{ss}}
an absolute
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-constructible set of semisimple local systems with quasiunipotent local monodromy. Then the
v
-adic Katzarkov–Zuo foliation of
X
associated to the
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-points
\Sigma(\bar{\mathbb{Q}})
of
\Sigma
(equipped with the valuation
v
) is algebraically integrable.

We define these notions more precisely in the following section.
The proof follows the strategy of Eyssidieux, the main new input being
Theorem

10.48
.

11.1.
Preliminaries

Note that any
\bar{K}
-local system on a complex algebraic variety
Y
is defined over some finite extension of
K
, since the fundamental group of
Y
is finitely generated. Let
(\bar{X},D)
be a log smooth proper algebraic space, with
X=\bar{X}\setminus D
. Let
V
be a semisimple
\bar{K}
-local system on
X
with quasiunipotent local monodromy, which is defined over a finite extension
L
of
K
. According to
Theorem

10.26
and
Proposition

10.24
,
V
can be equipped with a pluriharmonic norm which is locally bounded at infinity. As in
Section

10.9
, the resulting characteristic polynomial
P_{V}
whose coefficients are log symmetric forms is independent of the choice of such a norm on
V
by
Proposition

10.42
. In particular, since the base extension of a pluriharmonic norm which is locally bounded at infinity is also pluriharmonic and bounded at infinity,
P_{V}
is canonically associated to
V
(i.e., independent of
L
). By
Lemma

10.41
, there is a generically finite proper morphism
f:(\bar{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime})\to(\bar{X},D)
such that
f^{*}P_{V}=P_{f^{*}V}
is split and whose roots are closed one-forms. We refer to the Stein factorization
f_{V}:X_{V}\to X
as a spectral cover. The key property of these symmetric forms is the following:

Lemma 11.2
.

Let
f:\tilde{X}\to\Delta
be the pluriharmonic map given by the norm on the universal cover
\pi:\tilde{X}\to X^{\mathrm{an}}
. Let
Z
be a connected normal analytic space and
g:Z\to\tilde{X}
an analytic morphism. Then
f\circ g
is constant if and only if the coefficients of
(\pi\circ g)^{*}P_{V}
vanish as sections of
\Sym^{*}\Omega_{Z^{\mathrm{reg}}}
on
Z^{\mathrm{reg}}
. Moreover, if this is the case, then the monodromy of
g^{*}V
is bounded.

Proof.

The map
f\circ g
is pluriharmonic and by
Theorem

6.24
, there is locally an orthogonal basis on
Z^{\mathrm{reg}}\setminus W
where
W
has Hausdorff codimension 2 in the nonsingular locus. By
Proposition

10.37
the coefficients of
(\pi\circ g)^{*}P_{V}
are then constant if and only if the norms of this basis (and therefore the norm itself) are locally constant on this set. This gives the forward direction, and the reverse direction follows since
Z^{\mathrm{reg}}\setminus W
is dense in
Z
. For the final claim, we need only observe that the image of
(f\circ g)
is stabilized by the monodromy of
g^{*}V
and the stabilizer of a point in
\Delta
is bounded.
∎

We now extend this picture to normal algebraic spaces. The following terminology is useful.

Definition 11.3
.

As in
[
44
]
, for a connected normal algebraic space
X
, by an extendable one-form (resp. log extendable one-form)
\alpha
on
X
we mean a one-form on
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
such that for some (hence any) pair
(Y,\bar{Y})
consisting of a resolution
\pi:Y\to X
and a log smooth compactification
\bar{Y}
of
Y
,
\alpha
extends to a one-form on
\bar{Y}
(resp. a log one-form on
\bar{Y}
with poles along
\bar{Y}\setminus Y
) that vanishes on fibers
F
of
\pi
as a section of
\Omega_{F^{\mathrm{reg}}}
.

Lemma 11.4
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space.

(1)

The natural Hodge structure on
H^{1}(X,\mathbb{C})
has weights 1 and 2.

(2)

Taking de Rham cohomology class yields an isomorphism between the space of extendable one-forms (resp. log extendable one-forms) on
X
and
W_{1}F^{1}H^{1}(X,\mathbb{C})
(resp.
F^{1}H^{1}(X,\mathbb{C})
).

(3)

Integration yields natural Albanese maps (unique up to translation on the target)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Alb(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{1}(X,\mathbb{C})/F^{0}+H_{1}(X,\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{-1}\Alb(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{-1}H_{1}(X,\mathbb{C})/F^{0}+\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{-1}H_{1}(X,\mathbb{Z})}

and pull-back along
X\to\operatorname{gr}^{W}_{-1}\Alb(X)
(resp.
X\to\Alb(X)
) is an isomorphism on spaces of extendable one-forms (resp. log extendable one-forms). The Albanese maps are naturally algebraic.

Proof.

Standard, see for example
[
35
,
36
]
.
∎

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space. For a semisimple
\bar{K}
-local system
V
on
X
with quasiunipotent local monodromy, we may take a resolution
\pi:Y\to X
and by the above there is a spectral cover
f_{\pi^{*}V}:Y_{\pi^{*}V}\to Y
. We refer to the Stein factorization
f_{V}:X_{V}\to X
as a spectral cover of
V
.

Corollary 11.5
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space,
V
a semisimple
\bar{K}
-local system with quasiunipotent local monodromy, and
f_{V}:X_{V}\to X
the spectral cover. Then the roots of
P_{f_{V}^{*}V}=f_{V}^{*}P_{V}
are extendable one-forms.

Proof.

Immediate by
Proposition

10.40
.
∎

For any finite set
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\bar{K})
of semisimple local systems with quasiunipotent local monodromy, we thus have the following:

•

A finite dominant cover
f_{\Sigma}:X_{\Sigma}\to X
from a connected normal space
X_{\Sigma}
. After taking Galois closure we may assume we also have a group action of a finite group
G_{\Sigma}
on
X_{\Sigma}
realizing
f_{\Sigma}
as the quotient map.

•

A
G_{\Sigma}
-invariant space
E_{\Sigma}\subset W_{1}F^{1}H^{1}(X_{\Sigma},\mathbb{C})
of extendable 1-forms.

•

A minimal quotient
\Alb(X_{\Sigma})\to A_{\Sigma}
to an abelian variety
A_{\Sigma}
from which the forms in
E_{\Sigma}
are pulled back, which is therefore equivariant with respect to the natural
G_{\Sigma}
action.

•

A
G_{\Sigma}
-equivariant map
a_{\Sigma}:X_{\Sigma}\to A_{\Sigma}
, well-defined up to translation.

The space
E_{\Sigma}
naturally defines a foliation on
A_{\Sigma}
whose leaves are (locally) affine subspaces. For any
y\in X_{\Sigma}
, we obtain a natural germ of an analytic subspace of
X_{\Sigma}
by taking the reduced pullback by
a_{\Sigma}
of a leaf through
a_{\Sigma}(y)
. The group
G_{\Sigma}
naturally acts on these germs, so to any point
x\in X
we obtain a natural germ of a reduced analytic subspace
\mathcal{L}_{\Sigma}(x)
, which we call a
\Sigma
-leaf. This germ has the property that for any irreducible algebraic subvariety
i:Z\to X
through
x
, the local systems
i^{*}\Sigma
have bounded monodromy if and only if the germ of
Z
is contained in
\mathcal{L}_{\Sigma}(x)
.

Definition 11.6
.

Let
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\bar{K})
be a set of semisimple local systems with quasiunipotent local monodromy. The
\Sigma
-leaf through
x
(also denoted
\mathcal{L}_{\Sigma}(x)
) is the
\Sigma_{0}
-leaf through
x
for sufficiently large finite
\Sigma_{0}\subset\Sigma
. We say
\Sigma
is algebraically integrable if each
\Sigma
-leaf is algebraic—that is, has nonempty interior in its Zariski closure.

For a subset
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\bar{\mathbb{Q}})
, we may speak of the
\Sigma
-leaves via the embedding
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}\subset\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Remark 11.7
.

The map
X\to S_{\Sigma,\bar{K}}:=G_{\Sigma}\backslash A_{\Sigma}
is called a
\bar{K}
-Katzarkov–Zuo reduction (with respect to
\Sigma
). It has the property that the connected component of the fiber through
x
is the maximal connected algebraic subvariety containing
x
which is tangent to the leaf
\mathcal{L}_{\Sigma}(x)
. In the proper case one often takes the Stein factorization to make it the unique fibration with this property.

Lemma 11.8
.

Let
X
be a connected normal algebraic space. Let
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\bar{K})
be a set of semisimple local systems with quasiunipotent local monodromy. Then there is a finite set
\Sigma_{0}\subset\Sigma
such that for every point
x\in X
,
\mathcal{L}_{\Sigma}(x)=\mathcal{L}_{\Sigma_{0}}(x)
.

Proof.

Clearly
\mathcal{L}_{\Sigma}(x)\subset\mathcal{L}_{\Sigma_{0}}(x)
for any point
x
and any subset
\Sigma_{0}\subset\Sigma
. Since
A_{\Sigma}
is a proper abelian variety, the foliation extends to a compactification
\bar{X}
of
X
. Observe that for any finite set
\Sigma_{0}\subset\Sigma
, the sets
\bar{X}(\Sigma_{0},n):=\{x\in\bar{X}\mid\dim\mathcal{L}_{\Sigma_{0}}(x)\geq n\}\subset\bar{X}
are closed algebraic subsets. Indeed, the corresponding sets in
\bar{X}_{\Sigma_{0}}
(meaning the normalization of
\bar{X}
in
X_{\Sigma_{0}}
) are closed analytic subsets, hence algebraic by Chow’s theorem. For each
n
, an increasing sequence of finite subsets
\Sigma_{0}
yields a decreasing sequence of closed algebraic subsets
\bar{X}(\Sigma_{0},n)
which must therefore stabilize. Thus there exists a finite subset
\Sigma_{0}\subset\Sigma
for which the leaves of
\Sigma_{0}
and
\Sigma
have the same dimension at every point
x
. Applying the same argument to the subsets
\{x\in\bar{X}\mid\mbox{\# branches of }\mathcal{L}_{\Sigma_{0}}(x)\geq n\}
, the claim follows.
∎

Lemma 11.9
.

Let
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\bar{K})
be a set of semisimple local systems with quasiunipotent local monodromy.

(1)

Let
g:Z\to X
be an algebraic map from a connected normal algebraic space
Z
. Then the leaves of
g^{*}\Sigma
are the pullbacks of the leaves of
\Sigma
.

(2)

Let
\Sigma=\{V\}
with
V=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}
and set
\Sigma^{\prime}=\{V_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Then the leaves of
\Sigma
are equal to the leaves of
\Sigma^{\prime}
.

(3)

Let
\Sigma=\{V\}
with
V=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}
and set
\Sigma^{\prime}=\{V_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Assume
\det V_{i}
is trivial for
i>1
.
Then the leaves of
\Sigma
are equal to the leaves of
\Sigma^{\prime}
.

Proof.

(1)

Clear.

(2)

It suffices to observe that for a single
\bar{K}
-local system
V=V^{\prime}\oplus V^{\prime\prime}
, the leaves of
V
are the intersections of the leaves of
V^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime\prime}
, since the characteristic polynomial of
V
is the product of those of
V^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime\prime}
by
Proposition

10.38
.

(3)

Likewise, it suffices to assume
V=V^{\prime}\otimes V^{\prime\prime}
where
V^{\prime\prime}
has trivial determinant. The roots of
p_{V}
are
\alpha_{i}^{\prime}+\alpha_{j}^{\prime\prime}
, where
\alpha_{i}^{\prime}
(resp.
\alpha_{j}^{\prime\prime}
) are the roots of
p_{V^{\prime}}
(resp.
p_{V^{\prime\prime}}
). Certainly then the intersections of the leaves of
V^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime\prime}
are contained in the leaves of
V
. The characteristic polynomial of the determinant of
V^{\prime\prime}
is
T-\sum_{j}\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{j}
, so
0=\sum_{i}\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{j}
vanishes. Thus, since
\sum_{j}\alpha_{i}^{\prime}+\alpha_{j}^{\prime\prime}=\rk(V^{\prime\prime})\alpha_{i}^{\prime}
, we have the reverse containment as well.∎

Corollary 11.10
.

Let
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\bar{K})
be a set of semisimple local systems with quasiunipotent local monodromy. After passing to a finite étale cover
f:X^{\prime}\to X
and replacing
\Sigma
with
f^{*}\Sigma
, there is a finite subset of irreducible local systems
\Sigma_{0}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\bar{K})
with the same leaves as
f^{*}\Sigma
such that any
V\in\Sigma_{0}
is either abelian or has monodromy which is Zariski dense in a simple
\bar{K}
-group.

Proof.

Let
\Sigma_{0}\subset\Sigma
be a finite set with the same leaves as
\Sigma
, using
Lemma

11.8
. By (1), passing to a finite étale cover, the leaves of
f^{*}\Sigma
and
f^{*}\Sigma_{0}
will be the same. Thus, we may assume that the algebraic monodromy of each local system in
\Sigma_{0}
is connected. By (2) we may then replace
\Sigma_{0}
with a finite set of irreducible local systems by taking irreducible factors. For each element
V
of
\Sigma_{0}
, we may replace
V
with
\{\End(V),\det(V)\}
: if
p_{V}
has roots
\alpha_{i}
, then
\End(V)
(resp.
\det(V)
) has roots
\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j}
(resp
\sum\alpha_{i}
), and
\{\alpha_{i}\}_{i\leq r}
and
\{\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j}\}_{i,j\leq r}\cup\{\sum\alpha_{i}\}
have the same span. Finally, the algebraic monodromy of
\End(V)
is the adjoint form of the derived group of the algebraic monodromy of
V
, so
\End(V)
splits as a product of local systems each of which has simple algebraic monodromy. By (3) we may replace
\End(V)
with these factors, and this completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 11.11
.

Let
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\mathbb{C})
be an absolute
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-constructible set of semisimple local systems with quasiunipotent local monodromy. There is a finite étale cover
f:X^{\prime}\to X
, an absolute
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-constructible
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X^{\prime})(\mathbb{C})
set of semisimple local systems with quasiunipotent local monodromy, and a finite subset
\Sigma_{0}\subset\Sigma^{\prime}(\bar{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
f^{*}\Sigma(\bar{\mathbb{Q}}),\Sigma^{\prime}(\bar{\mathbb{Q}}),\Sigma_{0}
have the same leaves and any
V\in\Sigma_{0}
is either abelian or has monodromy which is Zariski dense in a simple
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-group.

Proof.

By
Proposition

9.10
, the direct factors and tensor components of the previous corollary can be extracted with
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-absolute operations.
∎

11.2.
Main step

The main step of the proof of
Theorem

11.1
is the following, whose proof closely follows the strategy of Eyssidieux using Simpson’s Lefschetz theorem as in
[
44
, §5]
.

Proposition 11.12
.

Let
X
be a connected smooth algebraic space,
(\bar{X},D)
a log smooth compactification, and
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{M}_{B}(X)(\bar{K})
a set of semisimple local systems with unipotent local monodromy. Assume that:

(1)

For each normalization of a strict subvariety
i:Z\to X
,
i^{*}\Sigma
is algebraically integrable.

(2)

For each component
D_{0}
of
D
, the image under the graded nearby cycles functor
\operatorname{gr}\psi_{D_{0}}\Sigma
is algebraically integrable.

Then either
\Sigma
is algebraically integrable or the commutator subgroup
[\pi_{1}(X,x),\pi_{1}(X,x)]\subset\pi_{1}(X,x)
has bounded image under the monodromy representation
\rho_{V,x}
for any
V\in\Sigma
.

Proof.

By Lemma
11.8
may replace
\Sigma
with a finite subset with the same leaves. Replacing
X
with a resolution of the spectral cover, we have a map
a:X\to A
to an abelian variety (spanned by
X
) and a space
E\subset H^{0}(A,\Omega_{A})
of one-forms defining a foliation of
A
by affine subspaces. By the minimality condition on
A
, there are no algebraic subvarieties of
A
which are contained in an affine subspace cut out by
E
. Throughout, by a leaf of a subvariety
Y\subset A
we mean the germ of an intersection of an affine subspace cut out by
E
with
Y
.

From now on, we assume that
\Sigma
is not algebraically integrable, in which case we will show that the second alternative holds. Thus,
a(X)
contains a positive-dimensional leaf. The assumption (1) implies: for any strict subspace
Z\subset X
, the image of
Z
under
X\to A
has no positive-dimensional leaves. Thus, any positive-dimensional leaf of
a(X)
is Zariski-dense in
a(X)
. Moreover, every positive-dimensional leaf of
a(X)
must be 1-dimensional, or else it would intersect a strict algebraic subvariety in positive dimension. Thus,
\dim E\geq\dim a(X)-1
. Finally,
a
is generically finite:
otherwise, by taking a generic multisection of
a
, (1) would imply that
\Sigma
is algebraically integrable.

Let
\bar{X}
be a log smooth compactification of
X
with boundary
D
. Note that the map
a:X\to A
automatically extends to a map
\bar{a}:\bar{X}\to A
. According to Theorem
10.48
, the restrictions of the one-forms
E
to any component
D_{0}
of
D
define the foliation corresponding to the image of the nearby cycles functor, and therefore by (2) it follows that
\bar{a}(D)
contains no positive-dimensional leaves.

The proof proceeds as in Eyssidieux’s proof in the projective case, although we simplify the argument by replacing the use of Eyssidieux’s higher-dimensional version of the Castelnuovo–de Franchis theorem by the so-called weak Ax–Schanuel theorem:

Theorem 11.13

(Ax
[
4
]
)
.

Let
A
be an abelian variety,
\mathcal{L}\subset A
the germ of an affine subspace, and
S\subset A
an algebraic subvariety. If
U
is an irreducible germ of the intersection
\mathcal{L}\cap S
with

\operatorname{codim}_{S}U<\operatorname{codim}_{A}\mathcal{L}

then
U
is contained in a translate of a proper abelian subvariety
B\subset A
. In particular, if
S
spans
A
, then
U
